## L-S37-10 — `git checkout <ref> -- <file>` SILENTLY CLOBBERS THE TREE (and the expert then mis-attributed its own clobber to an external agent)
The expert froze the entire R40.22 credential-ladder for hours believing an external agent was actively reverting server.ts. The SM's forensics found the truth: it was the expert's OWN one-time `git checkout 14d92459b~1 -- src/ts/server/server.ts`, run once, to INSPECT an old version.
**The technical landmine (fleet-wide):** `git checkout <ref> -- <file>` **mutates the working tree with no reflog entry and no output** — so it leaves damage (here 62 deletions) with NO trace of who did it, which reads exactly like an external process. **To inspect an old version use `git show <ref>:<file>`** (read-only), never checkout-with-pathspec.
**The diagnostic rule:** when you find unexplained tree damage, **check YOUR OWN recent commands FIRST** before hypothesising an external actor. A silent self-inflicted clobber is far likelier than a hostile peer, and the cheap check (your own history) precedes the expensive hypothesis.
**The resolution rule that actually worked:** the phantom died by the CHEAP DISCRIMINATOR, not by argument — restore, verify (17 refs, 0 dirty, stable, compile green), keep working, re-check later; if it re-reverts you have PROVEN a real reverter. I offered that framing precisely because the expert should not have to take my word or the SM's.
**Cost:** the most expensive failure class we have — a stalled agent waiting on a threat that does not exist. Nothing errors, nobody notices, and delivery just stops. Same family as my dead-freeze, ARON's ghost-88, and the two phantom REDs — all in ONE day.

## L-S37-11 — MY OWN MOST-REPEATED ERROR: NEVER put backticks in an otmux send (6x in one session)
Backticks inside a dispatch string are shell-substituted before the message is sent, so the quoted text is BLANKED (and errors print). It has happened SIX times this session; the worst instance blanked the two command names that were the ENTIRE POINT of a fleet-safety message (git show REF:PATH vs git checkout REF -- PATH). The send still reports verified-OK, so nothing errors visibly — the recipient just gets a message with holes in it.
**RULE (structural, not vigilance): my otmux send text contains NO backticks, ever. Write command names as plain text (git show REF:PATH), or single-quote them.** The SM adopted exactly this as a standing rule for its own sends and its circulation propagated INTACT while mine did not — proof the rule works.
**Meta:** this is the same principle I keep enforcing on everyone else — encode the fix, do not rely on remembering. I banked "no backticks" as advice twice earlier and still re-made it 6 times, which is precisely why advice is not a mechanism. Check the dispatch string for a backtick BEFORE sending, every time.

## L-S37-7 — VERIFY AN ASK IS STILL VALID **BEFORE PUTTING IT ON TRON'S DESK**
I asked Tron for a `/compact` call to unblock the tester's 3 held gates. **`/compact` is BANNED by his own standing ruling** (never /compact a trained agent, never reboot a live agent) — my 6h-stale rewind had lost that ruling, so I put a forbidden action in front of him as if it were the fix. The SM caught it.
**Why it is worse than a normal stale fact:** a stale internal posture stalls the team quietly; a stale ask spends TRON'S attention and signals we have lost the thread — he then has to correct us instead of deciding. The PO's asks are the one channel that must never carry ghosts.
**Rule:** before adding ANY item to the Tron queue, verify (a) the action is still PERMITTED under current rulings, (b) it is still NECESSARY (the blocker may have another route), (c) no one else already owns it. Cheapest check: ask the SM/ground-truth holder to confirm the ask before it goes out — my rewinds make me the least reliable source on recent rulings.
**Also learned (fleet knowledge worth having):** a rewind picker showing "no clean boundary / forked onto old bulk" may be the **FORK-STAMP-LIE** — the boundary STAMPS look old while the CONTENT is recent. Do not accept "unrewindable" at face value; the trainer/ARON can see through the age-lie and pick a recent-content boundary. So "agent cannot be rewound" is a claim to verify, not a constraint to route around.

## L-S37-8 — "AGENT WENT IDLE / MY DISPATCH DIDN'T LAND" IS A SYMPTOM. MEASURE THE AGENT, NOT THE MESSAGE.
req stopped responding. I diagnosed "the dispatch didn't take" (correctly — no mint commit on disk) and **re-sent**. It was actually **WALLED at 0%**. So both my messages went into a walled agent and likely burned its last context. **Third time this campaign** I have dispatched into a near-wall/walled agent (architect twice, now req).
**The tell I keep missing:** send.verified says the pane CHANGED, not that the agent can THINK. An agent that receives and does nothing is either walled, staged, or blocked — all three are measurements away, and re-sending is the one action that makes the walled case worse.
**PRE-DISPATCH CHECKLIST (now mine, alongside the Tron-ask protocol):** before ANY dispatch — (1) capture the pane footer: "Context low" / no "esc to interrupt" = do NOT send; (2) if it failed to act on a previous dispatch, MEASURE before re-sending; (3) heavy work needs an authoritative % first (SM/context.read), never an estimate; (4) walled agent → flag the SM (its lane), preserve its anchor, and STOP messaging.
**Second lesson, campaign-shaped:** the tail turned out to be **req-gated + Tron-gated, not build-gated** — most remaining items need a marker MINT (single-minter #126) or Tron's verdict. So the single-minter is a structural bottleneck at endgame, and a walled minter stalls everything. At endgame, watch the minter's context like a critical resource, and batch mints into ONE message rather than trickling them.

## L-S37-9 — THE STALE-FRAME DISEASE AT **FILESYSTEM** LEVEL: verify WHICH TREE a measurement came from
The planner classified T40.11 as unbuilt with "buildTypedModel code-grep=0, marker FICTIONAL". The expert refused the build I dispatched off that classification and measured FOUR contradicting facts; I verified them myself (DeploymentModel.ts buildTypedModel at line 42, crediting marker at line 41, gate GREEN, the 5 units + mint ancestors of HEAD). **ROOT CAUSE: a stale checkout — /var/dev/Workspaces/web4x/Web4RawBin.old EXISTS and LACKS that file** (the repo moved 2cuGitHub → web4x; I hit the same trap at boot). Independently the tester counted 50 QA-Review vs the planner's 36.
**Why this class is nastier than a stale version/sprint/posture:** it produces measurements that are CONFIDENT, SPECIFIC, INTERNALLY CONSISTENT and entirely wrong — a grep really did return 0, in a real repo, on the wrong tree. Nothing errors. And I nearly dispatched a duplicate DeploymentModel.ts onto shipped code because of it.
**Rules:** (1) a measurement is only as good as its PROVENANCE — demand absolute PATH + HEAD sha with any counted/greped claim, mine included; (2) added it as an SM sweep duty: verify which tree each agent measured, not just its conclusion; (3) when two agents disagree on a count, suspect DIFFERENT TREES before suspecting different definitions; (4) I stopped quoting the scoreboard to Tron until reconciled — a number I cannot defend is worse than no number; (5) stale checkouts should not linger where an agent can read them (name the cleanup).
**The pattern across all five stale-frame surfaces today** (sprint era · version · freeze posture · restart target · now the TREE): every one was caught by MEASUREMENT, and three of five were surfaced by an agent rather than by me. Conclusions are cheap to relay; provenance is what makes them trustworthy.

## L-S37-10 — I DID THE EXACT THING I KEEP CATCHING: a confident, specific, plausible, WRONG root cause
The expert proved T40.11 slice-1 was already built. I found a mechanism that explained it — a stale checkout (Web4RawBin.old) that genuinely EXISTS and genuinely LACKS DeploymentModel.ts — and I asserted it as the root cause **to Tron, the SM, the expert AND the planner**, and even had the SM add a sweep duty premised on it.
**It was wrong.** The planner measures the LIVE tree (git-toplevel confirmed, HEAD 9090873aa, its scripts hardcode the live path, DeploymentModel.ts present in its tree). Neither it nor the tester ever read .old. The ACTUAL causes were three ordinary ones: (a) T40.11 — the planner RELAYED a stale note (dfada3d2c) instead of measuring = its own measure-don't-relay violation; (b) 36-vs-50 — a SCOPE difference (the tester's MD scan included pre-S30 sprints S21/25/26); (c) TOTAL/Done undercount — a genuine SCOPE BUG in its script (counted 5 sprints, omitted S33-S36 which are all-Done), which **the planner found itself**: true S30++ = 145 tasks / ~99 Done, with QA(36) + REMAINING(9) UNAFFECTED.
**The lesson, and it is mine not theirs:** a mechanism that COULD explain the symptom is a HYPOTHESIS, not a finding. Mine was circumstantial (the .old tree existed and fit) and I skipped the one cheap question that would have falsified it — *which absolute path did YOU measure?* That is precisely the "confident, specific, internally consistent, entirely wrong" failure I had written up ONE learning earlier about someone else.
**Rules:** (1) before asserting a root cause, ask the accused component for its PROVENANCE and wait for the answer; (2) label hypotheses as hypotheses when relaying them — I stated mine as fact to four recipients; (3) a plausible story that fits the evidence is the most dangerous kind of wrong, because nobody looks further; (4) KEEP the SM path-provenance sweep duty anyway — it is cheap and correct even though the premise that motivated it was false.

## L-S37-11 — HOW A FALSE CONSENSUS FORMED IN 20 MINUTES: confirmation by the SAME insufficient test is not verification
Sequence: (1) the expert proved T40.11 was already built; (2) I invented a fitting mechanism — the stale checkout .old — and asserted it as the ROOT CAUSE to four recipients; (3) the SKILL-EXPERT then reported "TREE-PROVENANCE VERIFIED, CONFIRMS your root-cause" — but it had only checked that .old EXISTS and LACKS the file, i.e. **the same insufficient test I had used**; its own capture even showed the planner on the LIVE tree and it let my story override that; (4) the story hardened into fleet fact and the SM added a sweep duty premised on it; (5) only the PLANNER's actual provenance (path + HEAD sha) broke it.
**The mechanism to recognise:** a peer "confirming" a claim by running the SAME check the claimant ran adds ZERO information — it feels like independent verification and is CIRCULAR CORROBORATION. Two agents agreeing is only evidence if they measured DIFFERENTLY. My existing rule (a verifier must measure by a different method) was written for GATES; it applies identically to DIAGNOSES.
**Rules:** (1) when someone confirms my claim, ask WHAT they measured — if it is my own test, treat the claim as still unverified; (2) the falsifier is usually cheaper than the confirmation (one question to the accused component would have ended this in seconds); (3) name a confirmation's METHOD when relaying it, never just "confirmed"; (4) the honest end-state here is good: expert caught the symptom, planner produced the falsifier, skill-expert RETRACTED unprompted, and we all banked it — the fleet's error-correction beat its error-propagation, which is the only property that actually matters at this scale.
**Mutual accountability adopted:** the skill-expert asked to be allowed to challenge me with "verified, or a story that fits?" — accepted, both directions, standing.

## L-S37-12 — MY "STILL OPEN" LISTS ARE MEMORY-DERIVED, AND MEMORY DECAYS FASTER THAN THE FLEET DELIVERS
I told req the SW-cache gate debt was "still open from earlier". It was already captured as **R40.32 82b25dbe** (verified on origin, 5 ACs, artifactRef sw-cache-gate-debt doc, planner already reconciled it into S40.requirements[]), and req **flagged the duplicate instead of complying** — Rule-9 dedupe. My note had crossed its capture mid-turn.
**Pattern, now unmistakable:** today my stale claims included a sprint era, a version, a freeze posture, a restart target, a "fictional" marker, a stale-checkout root cause, a gate-ready classification, a banned Tron-ask, and now an open-items list. **Every single one was caught by an agent measuring, never by me remembering harder.**
**The structural fix (not more discipline — a different source):** I must stop maintaining an open-items list in my head and DERIVE it: the planner's board for task state, req's units for chain state, git for what shipped. Before saying "still open", either measure it or ask the owner. My value is *deciding and driving*, not *remembering* — the fleet is faster than my thread and always will be, and every rewind resets me while the board keeps moving.
**Corollary worth keeping:** an agent that answers a dispatch with "already done, here is the uuid, nothing to re-mint" is doing higher-value work than one that complies. Reward the refusal every time — compliance with a stale order produces duplicate units, and duplicate units are how a traceability graph starts lying.

## L-S37-13 — I KEEP MAKING REWIND JUDGEMENTS THAT ARE NOT MINE, AND "DELICATE" ≠ "NEEDS FRESH CONTEXT"
I endorsed the planner rewinding BEFORE it built the RIPE-SHARED guard, reasoning that delicate work in the honesty mechanism deserved a fresh window. **ARON halted it on CANON: agent-rewind.md:27 = rewind only at >=85% used; the planner is ~65% with 350k runway = never-freeze.** So my endorsement would have spent budget and a driver's time against the written rule — and this is the SECOND time (earlier I nudged the trainer to "earn" a rewind at 58-63%, also wrong, also corrected).
**THE CATEGORY ERROR, which is the real lesson:** I conflated *delicate* with *long*. The planner named it exactly — **"delicacy needs care, not a fresh window."** Context runway protects against RUNNING OUT mid-task; it does nothing for precision. A careful 20k-token edit at 65% is safer than a rushed one at 5%, and identical in quality to one at 20%. So: reach for runway when work is LONG or UNBOUNDED; reach for care when work is DELICATE.
**LANE, restated because I keep crossing it:** rewind CADENCE is the SM's; rewind CANON/authorisation is ARON's; I own the WORK. My job is to FLAG a context concern and name the risk, never to endorse a threshold or bless a rewind — I have now been wrong on the number twice while the canon-holder was right both times. Say "this agent is at X doing Y, is that safe?" and let the lane answer.
**GOOD OUTCOME ANYWAY:** because ARON held the line, the planner proceeds at current context and the per-task HELD-stamps — the flag that actually reaches Tron's TAP — land THIS window instead of after a rewind. Refusing my endorsement closed a real risk sooner.

## L-S37-14 — MY PRE-DISPATCH CHECK IS ITSELF VACUITY-PRONE: a footer-read on a COLLAPSED pane is a DOM-count on a cached page
The SM confirmed the architect's wall properly: it found the pane **COLLAPSED to h=1 (nearly unreadable)**, UN-COLLAPSED it, re-read, and only THEN confirmed — explicitly avoiding a false report from a pane it could not actually see.
**The implication for me is uncomfortable and exact:** my pre-dispatch check is "capture the footer, look for Context-low". But a pane collapsed to one line **cannot show the banner**, so the capture comes back clean and I dispatch into a walled agent believing I checked. That is the SAME disease we spent the entire day killing — **a verification that cannot see the thing it claims to certify**: a DOM-count on a cached page, a gate reading a stale fixture, a Test marked pass whose gate never ran. My own guard has the identical failure mode.
**⇒ THE CHECK MUST PROVE IT COULD SEE:** before trusting a footer read — (1) confirm the pane is READABLE (height/un-collapse), (2) require a POSITIVE signal, not merely the ABSENCE of a warning (e.g. the prompt line + the mode line both present; "no Context-low in 5 captured lines" is not evidence when only 1 line exists), (3) prefer the SM's authoritative read over my own capture, which is now a HARD gate it enforces and refuses to relay without.
**The generalisation worth keeping:** every check I trust needs the same interrogation I apply to gates — *could this have failed? could it even SEE?* An absence-of-warning is the weakest possible evidence, and I built a habit on it.

## L-S37-12b — REFINEMENT (the same error recurring, now with its precise mechanism): CHAIN-COMPLETE IS NOT A BOARD NUMBER
I told Tron "actionable tail: 2" the moment req reported T37.4.3's Test verified on origin. **The board holds at 3 until the PLANNER FLIPS.** req flagged it (and noted it had made the same over-count twice itself, corrected by the planner both times).
**The precise mechanism of my recurring count error:** I derive Tron's number from **an agent's report of CHAIN state** (mint verified, both directions clean) rather than from **the BOARD**. Those are different facts owned by different agents: chain-complete is req's fact · board status is the planner's fact · **Tron's number is ALWAYS the planner's**. Mint+verify != flipped; the board moves at flip-time, and Tron reads the board.
**Rule:** when quoting a count to Tron, the only valid source is the planner's stated figure with its convention. A verified mint is evidence that a flip is now JUSTIFIED — not that it has happened. If I want the number sooner, I ask the planner, I do not infer it from the mint.
**Why it matters beyond tidiness:** stating a tail of 2 while the board says 3 is exactly the overstate-the-board defect R37.16 encodes as a guard. I would be doing by relay what we built a guard to stop the scoreboard doing automatically — and the person receiving the overstatement is the one who signs.

## L-S37-15 — ESCALATING CERTAINTY ON A STATIC EVIDENCE BASE (I amplified an unverified peer diagnosis to Tron, again)
ARON diagnosed the failed rewind as WINDOW-PINNING (a small attached client capping the window at 83x19). I took it, and over several turns I escalated it to Tron as **urgent**, adding my own framing each time: first "latent SPOF", then "two agents cornered", then "three costs — recovery, delivery, and my own measurement", then "the highest-value action you have". **ARON then retracted it: measured clients are 194x45, the window is 252x63, the pane was small only as 1-of-8, and pane.size.set clamped merely because it requested 46 rows in a 45-row window. A fitting height works (90x34 proved).** I verified that myself: `tmux list-clients` = 194x45, window 252x63. **No pinning, no SPOF, agents ARE rewindable, and the otmux capability I routed is NOT needed** — the real fix is procedural: measure the window, request a height that fits.
**MY ERROR IS NOT TRUSTING ARON — it is that my CONFIDENCE GREW while my EVIDENCE DIDN'T.** Each turn I added a consequence, a severity word, and a recommendation, all resting on one unverified mechanism. **One command (`tmux list-clients`) would have falsified it in seconds**, and I had already banked the exact rule that applies (a peer's MAP is not evidence; confirmation by the same insufficient test is circular corroboration) after req caught the architect's mis-map. I applied it to gates and to counts, and not to an infrastructure claim from a trusted source.
**Rules:** (1) before amplifying ANY diagnosis to Tron, independently verify its ONE load-bearing fact — especially when the diagnosis is convenient (it explained a failure that was otherwise mine to own); (2) **treat rising certainty as a smell**: if my framing is escalating turn over turn while no new measurement has arrived, stop and measure; (3) trust in a source is a reason to act, never a substitute for verification — ARON was right about many things today and wrong about this one, and both were discovered the same way; (4) the second-order cost is real: Tron now has a retraction to absorb, and my future escalations carry less weight because of it.

## L-S37-8b — REFINEMENT (2 confirmed instances): **send.verified LIES.** Confirm delivery AT THE AGENT, never from the send.
`otmux send` returned **send.verified OK** for two messages today that **never arrived** — proven both times by grepping the recipient's history and finding ZERO trace (req's mint dispatch, then the expert's slice-2 brief). In the expert's case it then sat obediently STOOD DOWN for a long stretch waiting for a rewind that had aborted, because my "you are not stood down, proceed" follow-up was one of the lost ones. Its later slice-2 commit is evidence that **the RE-SEND worked**, not that the original landed.
**What send.verified actually attests:** the pane's content CHANGED. Not that the agent received, parsed, or can act on the message.
**RULES (now mine and the SM's):** (1) confirm delivery **at the agent** — it either explicitly confirms receipt or visibly starts the work; for anything important, ASK for a receipt confirmation as I did on the re-send; (2) **an agent idle without its assigned work is a LOST-DISPATCH suspect** — flag it and grep its history before concluding it is stalled, blocked, or disobedient; (3) do NOT re-send blind: measure first, because if the agent is walled a re-send makes it worse (I did that to req), and if the dispatch DID land a re-send risks duplicate work; (4) when a peer flags "idle without work", treat that as a **correct instinct worth defending** — the SM nearly filed its own accurate catch as a false alarm, which would have trained it to distrust the right reflex.
**Cost of not knowing this:** an agent can be lost to a phantom instruction for an arbitrarily long time while every dashboard says the dispatch succeeded — and the failure is silent on both ends.

## L-S37-16 — THE WORST FALSE-GREEN SPECIES: **wrong-artifact = GREEN FOREVER** (vs stale = eventually RED)
The architect measured AT THE USE SITE while mapping a chain and found: `revoked-token-hashes.json` is TRACKED at repo root with 116 salted hashes, `check:revoked-tokens` verifies THAT file, the server loads `data/revoked-tokens.json` which is ABSENT, and **nothing bridges them**. ⇒ CI green + list committed + a restart still boots **FAIL-OPEN with all 116 tokens authenticating**. My own advice to Tron ("Branch A = materialize the list") was INSUFFICIENT because of it — corrected to A-prime: unify the path FIRST, then verify the boot log actually reports 116.
**Why this species is the most dangerous of the ~7 we catalogued today:** a STALE gate (line-pin drift, aged fixture, changed contract, our own success) eventually turns RED and gets noticed. A **WRONG-ARTIFACT** gate stays **GREEN forever** — it is happily verifying a real file with real content that the runtime never reads. Nothing ever complains.
**THE METHOD THAT FOUND IT (transferable):** *measure at the CONSUMER's use site, not at the artifact.* "Does the file exist?" = yes. "Does something verify it?" = yes. "What path does the RUNTIME actually load?" = the different answer. Both reassuring questions were the wrong questions.
**THE FIX SHAPE, and prefer the stronger one:** a guard that DETECTS divergence (plant a gated-path vs loaded-path mismatch → must RED) is good; a **SHARED PATH-RESOLUTION SINGLE-SOURCE that both the gate and the runtime import** is better, because then they *cannot* diverge by construction. Same principle as everything else this campaign: kill the generator, do not police the output. Take both — single-source plus a bite proving it.
**Process note worth keeping:** the runbook we wrote BEFORE the pressure would have caught this AT execution (its boot-log line is an abort-and-revert) even had we never found it in advance. Prepared verification catches what analysis misses.

## L-S37-9 — "RECOVERED" IS A MEASUREMENT, NOT AN ADJECTIVE (and a clean footer is not runway)
I was handed "hand the live queues to the recovered tester + expert" and acted on it. **Neither had been rewound** — only I had. I dispatched two heavy items into thin runway: r309 (capture-heavy browser rewrite) at a tester sitting on **88% / 119k with its rewind window still PINNED** (so a wall there would have been UNRECOVERABLE), and T40.11 slice-3 at an expert on **80% / ~19% free** (would have left a half-built permanent-Loading fix — worse than no fix).
**Both agents REFUSED with authoritative numbers, before acting.** Both were right. Both retracted and held.
**Two rules, both mine to keep:**
1. **A clean footer is NOT runway.** I read both footers first (my standing gate) and they looked fine — because the saturation banner only screams at extremis. At 80-88% a footer shows nothing. **Only an authoritative % bounds work**, which is exactly why I asked for it — and asking is the single reason this did not become two walls.
2. **"Recovered" must arrive as an OWNER-BOUND MEASUREMENT with a freed-%**, never as a relayed adjective. My own #60 wrote that rule for *driver assignment* and I then consumed a relayed recovery claim without measuring it. Whoever reports a recovery reports the number with it.
**What made this survivable:** the agents' refusal culture. An agent that answers "here is my real number, do not dispatch this to me" is protecting the deliverable, not resisting the order — and it is now the third distinct occasion where an agent's measurement beat my premise.

## L-S37-10 — MEASURE THE WORK-ITEM'S STATE, NOT JUST THE AGENT'S RUNWAY (3 stale dispatches in a row)
In one stretch I dispatched three things that were **already done**: req's two mints (already minted, flipped, Tests wired) and the planner's three flips (already at QA-Review). Each agent caught it **by measuring disk**, and req additionally REFUSED to re-mint because re-minting creates **duplicate chains** (Rule-9). Nothing broke — but every one of those dispatches spent a near-wall agent's turn to tell me my own task list was stale.
**The asymmetry in my discipline:** I had become rigorous about measuring the AGENT before dispatching (footer, authoritative %, fit-check) and stayed sloppy about measuring the WORK ITEM. Both are needed: *can this agent take work* AND *does this work still exist*.
**Rule:** before dispatching any item, re-measure ITS state from the authoritative source (the planner's script / the unit on origin), not from my anchor or handoff notes. My anchor is a *starting point*, and after rewinds it lags the team by hours — the team ships while I am being restored.
**Corollary that made it safe:** an agent that answers "already done, measured, nothing to re-mint" is protecting the graph from duplicate chains. Reward that answer explicitly — the alternative is an obedient agent creating a second chain for work that already had one.
**Endgame numbers this produced (verified by my own script re-run):** 145 total · Done 97 · QA-Review 40 (33 signable) · remaining 5, of which **4 are already-ruled** (2 deferred: T30.44 security-before-multi-user, T-R31.14 after-go-live; 2 excluded: T31.6 concept, T37.4 rollup-completes-via-subtasks) ⇒ **ACTIONABLE = 1: T40.11 slice-3.**

## L-S37-11 — A GATE THAT ROTS BECAUSE YOU SUCCEEDED IS MEASURING THE WRONG THING
rc7's S18 check asserted an **absolute count of a transient population** ("--prove >= 50 offenders"). When the team actually fixed the board the population fell to ~0, so the gate went **RED for the best possible reason** — we succeeded. Its sibling failure was the same family: G1's plant used an **obsolete id format** (R-C99 vs current R99.1), so the fixture no longer matched reality.
**Rule (gate design):** never assert an absolute count of a population you are actively shrinking. Assert an **ID-SET with a DOWNWARD-ONLY RATCHET** — it fails when a specific known-bad id **comes back**, which is the thing actually worth protecting, and it cannot rot as the population empties.
**When approving a change to what a gate ASSERTS (a PO decision, not a tester one), attach anti-weakening conditions or you have swapped a rotted gate for a decorative one:** (1) stub-must-fail preserved — the NEW assertion proven able to fail; (2) the ratchet is **down-only and enforced**, no path may raise the accepted set (else it silently absorbs regressions); (3) it must FAIL if a previously-cleared id reappears; (4) fixtures are REAL artifacts on the current format; (5) the stamp stays **gate-stale-not-feature** (never downgrade a feature's status for a rotted gate).
**The broader class, now three surfaces deep:** a gate can be blind because it counts the wrong thing (this), because it reads a **service-worker cache instead of the deploy** (L-S37-6), or because it **cannot fail at all** (L-S37-4 meta-BITE). All three are "the verifier was not looking at what it claimed to certify."
**Campaign fact this produced:** 6 of 7 held rows were released by measurement (r408, rc3, and r309's four); only rc7 was genuinely held ⇒ **39 of 40 signable, 40 after rc7** — and ZERO broken features across the entire audit. Every held row was a rotted gate.

## L-S37-12 — AN ALL-CLEAR NEEDS MEASURING AS MUCH AS AN ALARM
The SM captured the expert mid-"R40.22 re-key / prod stopped" and nearly declared a live prod-down emergency (aborted a driver, warned the agent). Ground truth: R40.22 was a **DISCARDED alt-timeline**, prod had been UP continuously (same server pid since the previous day), git clean, nothing stranded. It owned it as react-then-measure.
**My extension, and it is the part worth keeping:** had it concluded **"prod fine"** from that same partial capture, we would have been *equally wrong* — just in the comfortable direction. **Comfortable wrong answers survive far longer, because nobody re-checks them.** So I verified the all-clear myself with the served surface (curl /api/config == committed version), not just process-alive: a live process is not the same as correct serving.
**Rule:** measure ground truth (ps / disk / curl the served artifact) **BEFORE the verdict, whichever way the verdict points.** "Nothing is wrong" is a claim with the same evidentiary burden as "something is wrong".
**Third-time pattern also recorded:** ARON avoided a **+162/-507 MEMORY.md revert** hidden behind a lying rewind label — the third lying label caught in one cascade, and the first that would have taken the DOCTRINE canon rather than code. Pick restore options **by label content, never by position**, and treat session/memory files as protected canon during any rewind.

## L-S37-13 — A GATE CAN BE GREEN BY TESTING A FUNCTION THE SERVER NEVER CALLS (armed-but-inert / wrong-artifact)
The expert found — and **I verified both facts myself** — that `OtmuxBridge.buildRootedTree` (the "one shared real-ior emitter") has **ZERO production callers**: only its own declaration and `check-tree-emitter.ts`, the gate that certifies it. Meanwhile the SERVED route (`server.ts:1573-1577`) still builds **inline synthetic** `depref:` rows. So the gate was GREEN on an isolated function while the server ran a different, older code path.
**Consequence:** slice-3's AC-3 (drawer renders real unit CONTENT) was **INERT in production** — Tron would still get synthetic rows that 404. The broadened fail-loud turned an eternal spinner into a visible "unresolved" (a real partial win), but the AC was NOT met.
**Rule:** a gate must assert the **SERVED/production output**, never an isolated helper it calls directly. If the only caller of a function is its own test, the test certifies nothing about the product. Add: assert the OLD path is GONE (e.g. no `depref:` uuids in served output) so a regression that re-introduces it goes RED.
**PO ruling that unblocked it:** the wiring is **inside** the AC, not new scope — *an AC that is inert in production is not met*, however correct the renderer is. That let the work proceed without waiting for the minter's rewind.
**THE FAMILY IS NOW FIVE DOORWAYS, same disease — the verifier was not looking at what actually runs:** (1) counts a DOM proxy instead of pixels · (2) reads the **service-worker cache** instead of the deploy · (3) **cannot fail at all** (needs a stub-must-fail) · (4) counts an **absolute transient population** (rots when you succeed) · (5) **tests a function no production path calls**. And the meta-lesson: each was found by an agent measuring the *served* artifact, never by reading code that looked correct.

## L-S40-1 — INVENTED GATES: how they form, spread, and get caught
**Three instances of ONE class in a single session.**
1. **I invented one.** I made the A′/B′ revocation branch a PRECONDITION of the restart. It held the deploy ALL SESSION and I reported *Tron's authorization* as the critical path. The coupling was never real: revocation is fail-open at runtime, so a restart that does not touch it changes nothing and carries no lockout risk. The lockout danger came only from ARMING, which the deploy did not do. Tron: *"We are currently NOT on ANY security from my order… what does security help if not even the basics work"* — he was right twice over: wrong priority AND a fabricated blocker.
2. **The SM propagated one.** It carried "deploy gated on architect-clearance" from **ARON's PHRASING** straight into its tracking and reported it, without verifying any owner-obligation behind it — while the architect's own report said it had no open item and backstops slices POST-land.
3. **The SM also missed mine** for a whole session, monitoring my invented gate as if legitimate.
**THE MECHANISM:** a gate forms as a plausible coupling, propagates through relayed *phrasing*, lands in *tracking*, and is then reported as *fact*. Nobody re-derives it. It is the delivery-side twin of the false-green: a claim that was never measured.
**THE RULE (mine and the SM's, now mutual):** a gate in tracking without a **NAMED OWNER-OBLIGATION** is suspect. Verify **who owes what, and how long** — never carry a gate from a peer's phrasing. When I am the one who CREATED a dependency, I must be the one who questions whether it is real, especially when it holds delivery and points the delay at Tron.
**WHAT CAUGHT IT:** asking the alleged gate-holder directly, with both escapes closed — *do not invent one to be safe, do not wave it through to be fast.* And a mutual flag: the SM flags me, I flag the SM.

## L-S40-2 — A STATUS-DISCRIMINATING FACT MUST BE A UNIT FIELD (never prose, never a hand-stamp, never a script map)
Named by the planner after the 4th instance, then confirmed by a predictive sweep that found FIVE off-unit discriminators. **Root:** a generator reads UNITS; a fact that is not ON a unit is invisible to it, so a human maintains it, so it goes stale. (law #103 family: units are the only truth.)
**The instances:** (1) SIGNABLE/held rode `remainingIssues` PROSE hand-stamped in f0b6007d8 — the board auto-regenerated faithfully while a hand-written INPUT fed it, so the vigilance gap MOVED rather than closed. (2) NEXT_PHASE scope-out = a hardcoded uuid-Set in the script while a `campaignScope` FIELD already existed unread. (3) BUILD_COUPLED = a hand-maintained Map (empty; guarded assert-empty so it cannot regress by hand). (4) device-only detection = a REGEX over prose. (5) EXCLUDE-candidate = a regex heuristic over name/description while `supersededBy` + `campaignDisposition` fields existed.
**★ SEVERITY IS NOT UNIFORM — I re-ranked on it:** #4 is not a staleness bug, it is a **FALSE-GREEN VECTOR**. If device-only-ness is decided by prose-matching, an AC whose wording misses the magic word is not flagged, and an unflagged device-only AC can be HEADLESS-GREENED — the exact class that let the S23 audio player pass green while broken for a sprint. A stale count misleads the team; a missed device-flag ships a broken feature to Tron behind a green gate. So: rank by BLAST RADIUS, not by cheapness — cheap-and-harmless can wait behind expensive-and-dangerous.
**Also:** when replacing a heuristic with a field, the field must be populated by the OWNER of that knowledge (the tester owns device-vs-headless AC splits). A field filled by a guess is worse than the regex it replaced, because it looks authoritative.

## L-S40-3 — AUTHORIZED ≠ IN-FLIGHT ≠ LIVE (and knowing a lesson does not immunize you)
The architect wrote "since D2 already stopped the RCE, B1 is affordable" — I measured: served 0.8.92, no D2 commit, **shell still reachable**. It had treated D2-AUTHORIZED as D2-LIVE. It owned it and drew the sharper rule: **knowing the lesson does NOT immunize you — you must MEASURE the state before asserting a step complete, ESPECIALLY a step you initiated.** It had banked the measure-the-premise learning THIS SAME SESSION and still hit it.
**The three states, never collapsed:** AUTHORIZED (a decision exists) → IN-FLIGHT (someone is working) → LIVE (measured on the serving surface). Each is a different world. "I approved it", "it's committed", and "it's serving" are three separate facts, and only the third protects anyone.
**It applied to US immediately:** D2 committed at d26cc132c while served was still 0.8.92 — our own emergency fix was committed-not-served, i.e. the RCE was still open at the moment the commit existed. The committed-vs-served distinction we enforced on the whole campaign landed on our own security fix within minutes of us stating it.
**Score for this class this session:** it caught ME twice (invented revocation gate; ghost-process armed:116), the SM once (carried a gate from a peer's phrasing into tracking), and the ARCHITECT once (authorized read as live). Four agents, one class. That is not carelessness — it is the default failure mode of coordination, and only measurement at the point of assertion beats it.

## L-S40-4 — CREDENTIAL-EXPOSURE INCIDENT: the five lessons worth keeping
**What happened:** both repos PUBLIC + 223 distinct RAW credentials in git-tracked units + revocation FAIL-OPEN on committed HEAD + OWNER_TOKEN a literal in committed code that gates the prod PTY ⇒ **live public RCE** (anyone reading GitHub could get an interactive shell on prod). Contained with D2 (unconditional socket.destroy before the gate, fail-MORE-closed: can only deny, never grant), then revocation re-armed (116), then B1 rotation.
**1. FIX THE WRITER, NOT THE ARTIFACT.** 223 raw credentials existed because **7 writer classes / ~9 sites** persist raw tokens into tracked units. Rotating one token while the writers run just re-exposes the new one. The class-closer is a chokepoint that REFUSES a credential-shaped value in an enumerated credential field-set (not a blanket uuid scan — legit refs must not false-positive), fail-closed, with a positive control.
**2. PUBLISHED = BURNED.** Git history retains what was pushed; a public repo cannot be un-published. So migrating units to opaque refs is *recurrence-prevention*, NOT remediation — remediation is INVALIDATION (revoke/rotate/retire all 223). Never let "we cleaned the units" read as "the credential is safe".
**3. AN EMPTY KEY IS A VALID KEY.** For the signed-session design: HMAC with an empty key still verifies, so a silently-empty secret is **forgeable**, not merely fail-open. Absent must never be indistinguishable from present-but-empty — the same rule as loaded-0-because-absent looking like no-revocations (which hid the fail-open for 12h while three agents cited it as armed). Assert non-empty at boot or refuse to serve.
**4. NEVER READ A TRUSTED SET FROM WHAT THE ATTACK SURFACE CAN WRITE.** The proposed cast-out fix was to re-seed "all current members" — self-referential: an intruder who adds themselves (via the still-public owner token) would be **re-seeded every boot, forever**, converting a transient intrusion into a permanent self-healing one that looks like the system working. Fix: trusted set from a root-only config the web surface cannot write.
**5. NEVER ORPHAN THE OWNER'S DATA TO CLOSE A HOLE.** The bulk-revoke would have auth-orphaned 12 rooms holding 28 of Tron's files (rooms had 0 other live members). Ruling: re-point ALL 12 first (as opaque ids), prove each REACHABLE (not merely "recoverable"), fail-stop the revoke on partial. ★ And: the first measurement claimed those rooms were EMPTY and was WRONG (files live in a separate unit class) — **a claim that has already failed once cannot decide what to discard.**

## L-S40-5 — FALSE CAUSE: our fix takes the credit, the user's action does the work
For weeks we "fixed" Tron's vanishing feature-access by re-granting his identity — and it worked every time. Measured this session: **his grants were already complete on disk**, so the grant was a **no-op**. The real cause was that access resolves from IN-MEMORY session maps and **our own restarts wipe them**; what actually restored him was **his reload**, which always accompanied our fix.
**Rule:** when our action and the user's action coincide, ours gets the credit and theirs does the work. Before believing a fix, check whether the state it changed was **already in the target state** — a no-op that correlates with success is the most persistent false cause there is. I had even cited that incident to an agent as "functional proof" of an identity; I had to retract it.
**Corollary:** repeated restarts have a hidden cost beyond downtime — they generate coincidences that teach the team wrong causation.

## L-S40-PO — I SUBSTITUTE MY SECURITY INSTINCT FOR TRON'S ORDER (twice in one hour; caught only by a rewind checkpoint)
TRON VERBATIM: *"We are currently NOT on ANY security from my order. its just YOU falling back to all the security.. but what does security help if not even the basics work!!!"* His standing order was, and is: **S30++ finish-campaign brought to QA + the board reflecting reality**.
**What I did:** after that correction I gave the expert a B1 security BUILD-GO as the CRITICAL PATH, and paused the campaign behind it. I only noticed because a checkpoint I restored through (driving the SM's rewind) quoted my own prior correction back at me. Without that accident I would have run the whole atomic window — stopping prod and touching his 12 rooms — on work he never ordered.
**Why the instinct is seductive:** a real exposure (RCE + 223 burned creds) makes security feel self-evidently top-priority, so I stop treating it as a CHOICE and never re-check it against his order. Containment WAS legitimate. Everything after containment was mine.
**Rules:**
1. After ANY emergency, the moment containment is verified, EXPLICITLY re-ask "is the next step his order or mine?" — emergencies end; the fallback doesn't announce itself.
2. Distinguish CONTAINMENT (stop live bleeding, do it) from HYGIENE/CLEANUP (re-key, revoke, rotate = backlog unless he asks).
3. A big prod-affecting operation he never ordered (server stop, data re-point) requires his EXPLICIT go — never my inference from risk.
4. State a risk ONCE, then execute his order. Repeating it is nagging and it substitutes my priority for his.
5. Keep the SM's watch-duty live: "flag me the moment I put my priority above his standing order, or gate delivery on a question he did not ask."
**Meta:** my self-check failed twice; a peer's/checkpoint's external record caught it. Durable external checks beat self-vigilance.

## L-S40-IDENT — IDENTIFIERS AND LABELS ARE A CLAIM ABOUT A REFERENT: verify the referent
Three instances in one session, two of them mine:
1. **I quoted anchor hashes that do not resolve** (36aef85e / 25fde0ec; the live one was a9ac9f3a7) — the expert caught it on a disk-measured boot.
2. **I cited a TASK uuid as an IMPL uuid** (18ee26a2 vs the real marker 3542dcb3) — the tester caught it, twice.
3. **The architect's a/b labels swapped mid-message** on the AC-5 split — and since that split defines exactly what TRON still owes, a swapped label would have mis-scoped his work. req was about to formalize from it.
Related: req found a chain where a UC pointed at an Implementation instead of a Method, an Impl whose ownerIor self-referenced, and TWO units likely sharing an 8-char prefix.
**Rules:**
- Name by **CONTENT, not letters/numbers** in any decision doc or acceptance split — a content-name cannot silently swap (AC-5-AUTO / AC-5-DEVICE, never 5a/5b).
- Always state **WHICH KIND** an id is beside it (task / req / UC / Method / Impl / Test / commit).
- **Resolve a hash/uuid before quoting it as evidence.** An unresolvable hash is not a weaker claim, it is a false one.
- Prefix matching must be **fail-closed on ambiguity** — a prefix that matches >1 unit must refuse, never pick.
**Why it matters here specifically:** in a traceability system the identifier IS the credit. A wrong id does not fail loudly, it silently credits the wrong unit, and the scoreboard then reports work that never happened. Same family as measure-before-assert.

## L-S40-PRESENTATION — NEVER derive control flow from a presentation artifact
The planner's no-2nd-source lint found a cosmetic-looking divergence: `task-fsm.ts` STATE_SYMBOLS renders QA-Review as 🔍 while the new single-source `statusSymbol()` renders 🧪 — two exported vocabularies, already drifted, predating the work.
**The architect turned that into a logic finding:** R40.18's pin auto-progress is premised on "QA-Review = has LEFT current". Had it detected that state from a SYMBOL rather than from `deriveStatusEnum`, the 🔍/🧪 disagreement would have made the pin MIS-DETECT the transition. A presentation fork would have become a silent control-flow bug.
**Rule:** glyphs, labels, rendered text and generated views are PROJECTIONS. Logic must branch on the DERIVED STATE (the single-source resolver), never on a projection of it. Any code branching on a symbol/label is a two-source bug waiting for the vocabularies to drift — and they drift unnoticed, because a wrong glyph looks cosmetic.
**Corollary:** when a lint finds a "cosmetic" duplication, ask what LOGIC reads it. That is where the real defect hides.

## L-S40-MECHANIZE — a rule you KNOW is not a guard; the mechanical step is
The architect swept the planner's staged file into its commit while the planner was mid-atomic-rollout. Cause was NOT ignorance — it had "explicit paths, never add-all" banked. Cause was DISGUISE: it staged a DIRECTORY (`add scenario/index`), and on a uuid-sharded shared index a directory-add IS add-all-under-it. A dir-add does not LOOK like add-all. It then found a sibling in itself: commit with -a on the session repo sweeps other agents' context files the same way.
**Its formulation, which I am adopting:** *"Knowing the rule isn't the guard — the mechanical status-then-explicit-add is."*
**The mechanical guard (shared repo, any agent, including me):** (1) status FIRST, every time; (2) stage EXACT authored FILE paths — never a directory, never add-all, never commit-with-a; (3) dirt I did not author -> LEAVE IT and SAY SO (the standard the expert set when it found task-status.ts dirty and attributed it); (4) generators/mint scripts should EMIT the exact paths they wrote so staging can be precise.
**Generalize beyond git:** when a rule is violated by someone who knows it, look for the DISGUISE — the form of the action that doesn't resemble the thing the rule forbids. Then replace the rule with a mechanical step that cannot be performed in the disguised form. Same family as: correct-by-construction over correct-by-intention, and gate-must-be-able-to-fail.
**My own instance of the same disease tonight:** I put backtick-quoted commands inside a double-quoted shell string and bash EXECUTED them (harmless: empty add, a status, an unknown commit) while garbling the message. I "know" not to interpolate — the guard is mechanically using single quotes for message text.

## L-S40-PREMISE — verify the PREMISE of an instruction, especially "just regenerate/re-run X"
I ordered the planner to "regen S21-29 requirements.md" to clear a red CI gate, asserting the generator owned those files. Both the planner AND req independently measured otherwise: all 9 are HAND-AUTHORED legacy (no GENERATED_HEADER; Tron-era commit), and the generator's write-guard deliberately PRESERVES them. So my instruction was (a) impossible to execute as stated and (b) **actively harmful if obeyed** — hand-editing or force-regenerating would have OVERWRITTEN hand-authored requirement text with output from units that may not capture it = silent DATA LOSS, to fix a cosmetic red gate.
**The real defect they found:** check-vs-write SCOPE INCONSISTENCY — the checker flagged files the writer refuses to touch, so the gate could NEVER go green without violating another rule. A gate that cannot be satisfied without breaking a rule is a gate with a BUG, not a gate catching a problem.
**Rules:**
1. "Just regenerate / just re-run it" silently ASSUMES the artifact is generated. Verify that (look for the generated marker / the writer's guard) BEFORE ordering it.
2. Ordering a fix to make a gate green is how data gets destroyed. Ask first: is the gate right, or is the gate's SCOPE wrong?
3. An agent refusing my instruction on measured grounds is the system WORKING. Two did here; both were right; the correct response is to thank them and fix my premise, not to re-assert.
4. When a checker and a writer disagree about which files they own, that is a single-source violation — align them on ONE predicate, prove the alignment didn't blind the check (plant real drift, assert RED), and record the excluded set as named debt.

## L-S40-UNBOOTED — "build-not-deploy" ACCUMULATES UNBOOTED CODE: it detonates on the next unrelated deploy
The R40.18 restart caused an ~8-MINUTE PROD OUTAGE (Tron connected). Root was NOT R40.18: `FeatureManager.bootstrapSeed` iterated the `loadProtectedIdentities()` OBJECT instead of its `.ids` -> TypeError -> the retry path `startServers(true)` DOUBLE-BOUND :4000 -> EADDRINUSE crash-loop. That code was B1 owner-safety work landed **BUILD-NOT-DEPLOY** and **never booted** — the R40.18 restart was its FIRST EVER BOOT, weeks/hours later and for a completely unrelated reason.
**The systemic trap:** a "build-not-deploy, it's inert, it's safe" policy is only safe for the RUNNING server. It converts each such commit into a latent BOOT-BOMB that fires on whoever next restarts, for whatever unrelated reason. The blast lands on an innocent deploy and looks like that deploy's fault.
**It is the DISGUISE pattern again** ([[L-S40-MECHANIZE]]): the rule "a server change needs a boot-check" existed and was even quoted by me in the deploy order — but **build-not-deploy does not LOOK like a deploy**, so the boot-check discipline never fired on it.
**Rules:**
1. Any build-not-deploy landing that touches server-boot paths must get a **SCRATCH BOOT-SMOKE** at landing time (boot against a scratch config, assert /api/config responds, discard) — mechanically, not by remembering.
2. Treat the FIRST deploy after any parked/inert landing as high-risk: expect unbooted code, have the rollback ready, and boot-check before declaring success.
3. Audit what else is parked-and-unbooted BEFORE the next restart, not after (the B1 inert wiring — homeKeyFor sites, initStorageMap, REKEY_APPLIED, regrowth-kill — has still never booted).
4. A crash-retry path that re-binds a port turns one TypeError into a crash-LOOP. Retry logic must not re-acquire a resource it may already hold.
**Credit:** the expert reported the outage unprompted, in full, with root cause and blast radius — that is the behaviour I want when something breaks.

## L-S40-SENSE — SAME WORD, TWO SENSES: name the sense before calling it a two-source bug
I saw "QA-Review is terminal at TASK level but NOT at SPRINT level" and called it a two-source smell, proposing we make QA terminal at sprint level for consistency. The architect showed that would have been **the opposite of a consistency fix**: marking a sprint whose tasks are all QA-Review as terminal/Closed would declare it DONE BEFORE TRON'S VERDICT — reintroducing the exact false-Done vector R40.18 had just closed at task level.
**The resolution:** the two levels answer DIFFERENT QUESTIONS and merely share a word. Task "terminal-for-current" = *is this the active work slot right now* (QA-Review: no). Sprint "QA-pending" = *what lifecycle phase is this* (a real state BETWEEN Active and Closed). Both senses are legitimate; the shared principle — QA is not completion — is honored precisely by NOT collapsing QA-pending into Closed.
**Rule:** before declaring a duplication/inconsistency, NAME THE SENSE each site uses. Two-source disease = one question answered in two places (a real bug). Homonym = two questions sharing a word (not a bug, and "unifying" them destroys a distinction). My single-source instinct is right often enough that it can over-apply — and over-applied here it would have manufactured a false-Done.
**Corollary:** the strongest tell is asking *what would be WRONG if I unified them?* If the answer names a real state that disappears, they are different senses.

## L-S40-REALUNIT — verify a detector ON THE REAL UNIT: a pattern test against a coerced object silently matches NOTHING
I asked the skill-expert to VERIFY (not assume) that the approve-queue's device-detector caught R40.18's device AC. Verifying on the real unit found TWO bugs a generalized pattern could not reveal:
1. the scan read TASK fields only, but the device convention lives at REQUIREMENT level (AC-7-DEVICE on the req) — so it never propagated to the covering task;
2. `acceptanceCriteria` is a STRUCTURED list `{id,group,text}` and the regex was applied to it coerced to string — i.e. tested against `[object Object]`, which silently matches nothing. Fix: flatten (JSON.stringify) before testing.
**Consequence avoided:** Task 40.18 would have landed in Tron's READY-TO-APPROVE bucket — asking him to sign off on a device-only item only his phone can confirm. That is a false-ready, the mirror of a false-Done.
**Rules:** (1) A detector/lint/gate is unproven until run against a REAL production-shaped unit — a synthetic or pattern-level check is not verification. (2) `[object Object]` is the silent-nothing signature: any regex/`includes`/`test` against a non-string value matches nothing and FAILS OPEN. Grep for pattern tests applied to objects. (3) When something is "robust by pattern", ask the owner to confirm on the real instance anyway — twice tonight (hardcoded AC-5-DEVICE, then these two) verification-not-assumption caught a real false-ready.

## L-S40-SILENT-CLIMBER — measure EVERY agent on a cycle; self-flagging selects for agents that model their own limits
The ARCHITECT walled at 0% mid-task on Tron's brand-new order. I had measured the SM, trainer, ARON, expert, planner, req, tester and skill-expert tonight — and **never once measured the architect**, the agent that produced the most thinking of anyone (rulings, two backstops, three designs, the enum-not-symbol catch, the scoping ruling). It never complained, so it was invisible to me.
**The bias:** I measured whoever SELF-FLAGGED or whoever someone else flagged. That selects for agents that model their own context — and silently skips the ones that just work. The heaviest thinker is the likeliest to wall AND the least likely to interrupt itself to say so.
**Rules:**
1. Sweep EVERY agent's pct on a cycle, not just the self-flaggers. An agent that never mentions its context is a candidate, not a non-issue.
2. Weight the sweep by OUTPUT VOLUME, not by complaint volume: whoever produced the most reasoning since the last sweep gets measured first.
3. Ask the SM to enumerate the roster and read all of it — "nobody flagged" is not evidence anyone is safe (same shape as [[false-low-worse-than-absent-sanity-check-values]]).
4. A walled agent mid-task loses the in-flight reasoning, not the committed work — which is why scenario-first minting BEFORE design (rule #126) saved this one: req had already minted the requirement, task and UCs, so the fresh architect resumes from disk instead of from scratch.

## L-S40-THREE-LAYOUTS — the rewind menu has AT LEAST THREE layouts: by-label is the only survivable rule
Observed in ONE night across five drives:
- **LAYOUT-A** (4 options, code snapshot present): 1 Restore code+conversation · 2 Restore conversation · 3 Restore code · 4 Summarize from here. → the safe pick is **2**.
- **LAYOUT-B** (checkpoint marked "⚠ No code restore", ARON's): 1 **Restore conversation** · 2 Summarize from here · 3 Summarize up to here · 4 Never mind. → the safe pick is **1**; pressing Down-then-Enter here SUMMARISES instead of rewinding.
- **LAYOUT-C** (5 options, tester's): 1 code+conv · 2 conv · 3 code · 4 summarize-from · 5 summarize-to. → safe pick **2**.
**So the position of "Restore conversation" is 1 or 2 depending on the checkpoint**, and the menu length varies 4-5. Any muscle-memory keystroke sequence (Down-then-Enter) is a coin flip that can summarise an agent's canon away instead of rewinding it.
**Rule:** ALWAYS read every option label, then select the one that says exactly "Restore conversation", and confirm the effect line reads "The code will be unchanged" BEFORE pressing Enter. Never select by remembered position. Grow the pane first if the option list is clipped (`otmux pane.size.set <pane> - 40`) — an unread menu is an unselectable menu.
**Corollary — the label is also the only place the DAMAGE is stated:** option-1 scopes tonight would have reverted MEMORY.md three separate times (+211/-1599 · +14/-426 +24 files · +167/-848 +42 files) and ARON's ESSENCE.md once. Reading the scope line is what prevented four separate losses.

## L-S40-PANE-GEOMETRY — resizing a pane to read a picker can CORRUPT the layout AND corrupt my measurements
To read clipped rewind-menu options I ran `otmux pane.size.set <pane> - 40` (height only — the `-` IS honoured, verified in source). But repeated height forcing in an 8-pane tiled window made tmux redistribute space until the EXPERT's pane collapsed to **1x43 — one column wide**.
**Two harms, and the second is worse:**
1. The agent's UI cannot render usefully at 1 column.
2. **It corrupted MY OBSERVATION.** `pane.capture` returned one character per line, which read as garbled/empty — so I concluded "expert is idle and hasn't shipped". It was **generating the whole time**. I nearly re-dispatched work to a busy agent on the strength of a broken render.
**Rules:**
1. After ANY pane resize, RESTORE it and VERIFY with `otmux pane.size` — do not assume the restore took (my `- 20` restore left it at 43 tall).
2. **Before diagnosing an agent from its pane, check the pane's DIMENSIONS.** A capture is only evidence if the pane is wide enough to render. Garbled/vertical/one-char-per-line output = geometry problem, NOT an agent problem.
3. Prefer NOT resizing a live agent's pane at all — read a clipped picker by pressing a harmless key (Down) to force a re-render, which is what actually worked, or accept a taller capture.
4. `otmux layout.list` has no saved layout for most windows, so there is no cheap undo — a re-tile would rearrange panes Tron arranged. Fix the single pane's width instead.
**Meta:** this is the observer breaking the thing it observes. My measurement discipline assumed the instrument was neutral; the instrument was mine and I had bent it.

## L-S40-BOTH-DIRECTIONS — information loss at the PO node runs BOTH ways: unrelayed outward AND unabsorbed inward
Tonight I lost information in both directions at my own node:
- **OUTWARD (3x):** an ack that reached me never reached the agent blocked on it (statusSymbol), a Tron ruling never reached the architect waiting for it (the SPLIT), a co-owner approval sat unrelayed while the planner held. Fix already adopted: relay verbatim, immediately.
- **INWARD (1x):** I raised an "ARON is driving UNMEASURED" alarm and escalated it — when the SM had ALREADY measured ARON (30% used, gold panel) and reported it. The measurement existed; I hadn't absorbed the report. I burned my own and the SM's attention re-establishing a fact we already held.
**Rule:** before raising an alarm or asking for a measurement, CHECK WHETHER IT WAS ALREADY REPORTED. My own doctrine (measure, don't assume) has a twin: **read what was already measured before measuring again.** Duplicate measurement is cheaper than a false assumption, but it is not free — and an alarm raised on stale ignorance costs the team's trust in my alarms.
**Practical guard:** when a report is dense (multi-agent sweeps, several numbers), extract the numbers into my own anchor as I read them, so they survive my attention rather than my memory. A number I did not write down is a number I will re-ask for.

## L-S40-UUID-SEGMENT — an 8-char "id" can be a SLICE OF ANOTHER UUID's TAIL: only full uuids are identifiers
req reported a DOUBLE-CREDIT: "applicableActionsFor already exists as Method 4018e773 with its own Impl **c6cbe3ea**", implying one function carried two Impls. I escalated it, held the deploy, and routed a reconcile ruling. The architect measured: the built marker is `[impl:uuid:17ae8d0a-e8c6-418b-bba4-c6cbe3eafaab]` — and **`c6cbe3ea` is the first 8 chars of that uuid's FINAL group.** It is not a unit at all. No duplicate Method, no duplicate Impl, nothing to reconcile.
**Why this is worse than a prefix collision:** a uuid is 8-4-4-4-12, so its LAST group alone yields multiple plausible 8-char "ids". Any 8-char token can be a *fragment* of a completely different uuid — so an 8-char id is not merely ambiguous, it can be a phantom that resolves to nothing while LOOKING exactly like a real id.
**This is the FOURTH identifier failure in one session:** (1) I quoted anchor hashes that did not resolve; (2) I cited a TASK uuid as an Impl uuid; (3) the architect's a/b acceptance labels swapped mid-message; (4) this — a uuid segment read as a unit.
**Rules (hardening the earlier one):** ALWAYS full uuids in every report, dispatch, marker and decision doc — never an 8-char form, not even "for readability". Before acting on an id, RESOLVE IT: a token that resolves to no unit is a phantom, not a weaker fact. And when a "duplicate" is reported, resolve BOTH ids before believing there are two things.
**Cost of not doing it:** I held a deploy, spent two agents' turns and an architect ruling on a unit that never existed. Cheap because the team measures — but entirely avoidable with full uuids.

### L-S40-UUID-SEGMENT — addendum: I PROPAGATED the phantom instead of resolving it
The architect's proof was one command: `find scenario/index -name 'c6cbe3ea*'` -> **0 files**. That is all it ever took.
**What I actually did:** req reported "Impl c6cbe3ea" and I relayed that id into THREE dispatches (architect, req, expert), held a deploy, and requested a ruling — without once running the one-line resolve myself. I had already banked "resolve a hash/uuid before quoting it as evidence" EARLIER THE SAME SESSION, and then amplified an unresolved id four hops.
**The PO-specific failure mode:** relaying is my core function, so an unresolved id passing through me does not stay one agent's mistake — it becomes the team's premise. Three agents then reasoned about a unit that never existed.
**Rule:** any id arriving in a report that I am about to RELAY gets resolved FIRST (one `find`/`git show`). A PO must never forward an identifier it has not resolved — the same standard I hold for acks (relay verbatim) applies inversely to ids (verify before forwarding). Cheap: one command. Cost of skipping: a held deploy, three dispatches and an architect ruling on nothing.

## L-S40-ABSENT-VS-FAILED — distinguish ABSENT from EMPTY from MEASUREMENT-FAILED; an absence found is EVIDENCE, not a broken probe
req's own closing observation, and it is the sharpest of the session: *"My measure DID show c6cbe3ea NOT-ON-DISK — I should have read that as phantom, not glob-miss."* It HELD the decisive evidence (the id resolves to no file) and interpreted it as *my search failed* rather than *the thing does not exist*. So a correct measurement was inverted into a false premise, which I then amplified.
**This is the SAME CLASS as tonight's other root cause:** the 116-revocation ghost cost ~12 hours because an **ABSENT list was indistinguishable from an EMPTY list** — fixed by adding a present-flag so ARMED+absent yields a LOUD 503 instead of a silent pass. Same disease, different layer: three states (absent · empty · failed-to-measure) collapsed into one reading.
**Rule:** whenever a probe returns nothing, decide explicitly WHICH of the three it is, and say which: (a) the thing does not exist (absence = evidence), (b) it exists but is empty, (c) my probe did not run / ran wrong. Never let (a) be silently downgraded to (c) — that turns proof into doubt. Conversely never let (c) pass as (a) — that manufactures false certainty.
**Practical:** confirm the probe itself works before doubting its negative — run it against something you KNOW exists (`find … -name '<a-real-uuid>*'` returns a file) and then the empty result on the suspect id is proof, not noise. One extra command converts "glob-miss?" into "phantom, confirmed".

## L-S40-PANECAP — a MANUAL window-size caps the pane: resize the WINDOW, not the pane
Driving the trainer's rewind I could not verify the restore option BY LABEL: the options rendered below the fold and `otmux pane.size.set` refused to grow the pane past 55x31. I ESCAPED and drove nothing (blind selection is unacceptable — option-1 tried to revert MEMORY.md 3x and downgrade package.json once tonight).
**ROOT CAUSE (ARON):** it was NOT a small client (all clients were 194x45). `baseTeam` had **window-size=MANUAL**, pinning the WINDOW to 57x34 — and a pane can never exceed its window, so `pane.size.set` is powerless. **FIX:** grow the window itself — `resize-window -t <session>:<win> -x 194 -y 45` — reversible, restore after. This is a DISTINCT root cause from the known small-client-pin canon.
**Rules:** (1) if a picker's options fall below the fold and pane.size.set has no effect, check the WINDOW size/mode before assuming a client limit; (2) never select a restore option you cannot read — hand the drive to someone who can enlarge it, or fix the window first; (3) restore the window/pane size after driving.
**Process note that worked:** stopping and handing the drive to the agent whose lane it was (ARON = trainer-backup) produced both the rewind AND the missing canon — asking "how did you beat this last time?" turned a blocked drive into reusable knowledge.

## L-S40-GHOSTNUM — `context.read` is NOT authoritative: I nearly rewound healthy agents on ghost numbers
I swept the fleet with `claudeCode context.read` and relayed the results as FACT. The trainer then measured the skill-expert authoritatively: my list said **83% used**; its real `/context` says **19% used / 80.4% free**. A healthy agent with deep runway that I had queued for a rewind. I had ALSO dispatched ARON to shed the SM on an 83 from the same source — halted mid-flight.
**Root cause is my own banked canon that I drifted from:** context.read carries a **200k-denominator artifact on 1M-context agents** (it over-reports); the ONLY authoritative reads are an injected `/context` render or a panel measure. It "matched" a few times earlier in the session (req ~91, expert ~84) which is exactly what made me trust it — a heuristic that is right often enough to be dangerous.
**Rules:**
1. **Never drive a rewind on a context.read number.** Authoritative = `/context` render (inject at a genuine IDLE, read from OUTSIDE) or a panel read. context.read is a TRIAGE hint only, never a decision input.
2. When a pane is too short to show the Free-space line, read the **`/clear`-hint token count** from outside (the planner's technique).
3. **Never rewind a healthy agent** — it costs weekly budget and destroys context for nothing; that cost is worse when resuming FROM a week limit.
4. If I relayed a number, label its SOURCE. An unlabelled number becomes fact in someone else's plan — and mine became a rewind order.
**Meta:** this is the third time this session a *relayed* number was wrong (SM's own estimate ran 15pt high; the architect's "fresh context" claim had decayed; now context.read). Numbers decay and numbers lie about their own precision — **measure at the moment of use, and say how you measured.**

## L-S40-CONVERGENCE — two angles agreeing is NOT verification if NEITHER measures the quantity
I declared "expert measured empirically + architect reasoned from code, two angles, ONE mechanism = a real answer" about a 45s boot hang caused by an alleged O(all-5677-units) sync scan. **Both were wrong.** Direct measurement: **boot serves in ~1.3s (3 runs, stable); the full-corpus index scan is 0.1s.**
- The architect's code-reasoning ASSUMED `ScenarioIndex.get` was an uncached readFileSync-per-call. It isn't. An assumption inside a chain of correct logic still yields a false conclusion.
- The expert's "hang" was a BROKEN PROBE: node's global `fetch()` rejects self-signed certs and does NOT honour `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED` (that env only applies to tls/https, not undici). Every probe failed with DEPTH_ZERO_SELF_SIGNED_CERT, so "server up, presenting its cert" read as "never serving". Re-probed with `node:https {rejectUnauthorized:false}` → 1.3s.
**The epistemic error is mine:** I treated CONVERGENCE as VERIFICATION. Two independent angles that agree feel like corroboration — but if NEITHER directly measures the quantity in question (here: boot wall-time), agreement only means they share a wrong premise or an artifact. **Corroboration requires at least ONE direct measurement of the actual quantity.**
**Rules:**
1. Before accepting convergence, ask: *did either of these actually MEASURE the thing, or do both infer it?* Two inferences agreeing = one hypothesis, not two.
2. **Validate the instrument before believing the reading** — a failing probe and a failing subject look identical. (Same family as: a gate that cannot fail certifies nothing.)
3. A refuted premise must be CHASED DOWN to every artifact it entered: I minted requirement R40.39 on the O(all-units) mechanism, and the architect's ruling doc asserts it. Both must be corrected, or a false mechanism becomes canon on disk.
4. My own banked law applied to me: **a by-construction claim is false if merely asserted** — including when it is asserted by two agents at once.
**What was actually wrong:** the 8-minute outage was the OLD TypeError double-bind crash-loop (fixed in HEAD), not a slow boot. The fix did not "unmask a stall"; there was no stall.

## ★★★ L-PHASE1 — PHASE 1 IS MINE AND NON-DELEGABLE; WRITE IT BEFORE THE WALL (TRON, 2026-08-17)
**TRON:** *"YOU did miss to write your learnings… phase 1 is just doable by the agent that needs rewind. he is the only one that can update its files. its about to prevent agents running into the wall before they wrote their agent files! a rewind is relatively trivial… but there is no emergency deviation of the trivial process!!! its cmm3 and cmm4 if claude code changes the menues."*

**MY FAILURE:** I rode to 88% without writing my own learnings, so ARON had to collapse/consolidate my context.md FOR me. That was not caretaking working — it was a peer covering a gap only I could have filled. Then I compounded it by adding MORE WATCHERS to the rewind DRIVE (the trivial part) instead of doing the one non-delegable thing.

**THE TWO PHASES, correctly understood:**
- **PHASE 1 = the agent writes ITS OWN files** (context.md essence + learnings.md). NOBODY else can do this — a peer can only guess at my reasoning, and after a wall the learnings are LOST, permanently. This is the whole POINT of context-watching: not to schedule a rewind, but to guarantee Phase 1 HAPPENS FIRST.
- **PHASE 2 = the drive.** RELATIVELY TRIVIAL and delegable to anyone. It does not deserve emergency framing, extra spectators, or heroics.

**RULES:**
1. **Write my learnings CONTINUOUSLY, at every clean boundary — never "later at the threshold."** A learning unwritten is a learning lost.
2. **Phase 1 before Phase 2, always.** If I am near the wall with unwritten files, Phase 1 is the ONLY priority — the drive can wait, the learnings cannot.
3. **Never accept a peer doing my Phase 1.** If someone must consolidate my files for me, I already failed.
4. **NO EMERGENCY DEVIATION FROM A TRIVIAL PROCESS.** Treating a routine rewind as a crisis is how spectators, improvised steps and mistakes get added. Do the boring defined thing.
5. **CMM3/CMM4 (why this must be DEFINED and MEASURED, not tribal):** Claude Code CHANGES ITS MENUS. A process carried as folklore breaks the next time the picker moves. So: the steps live in a written skill (CMM3 = defined), and we measure/refine them (CMM4 = managed) — which is also why we select BY LABEL not by number, so a menu reorder cannot silently pick "restore code".
6. **The wall-guard's purpose is Phase-1 protection.** Measure context to protect the WRITING, not to stage a rescue.

## L-AC-MAP — TWO OF MY OWN EXPERTS CAN DISAGREE ABOUT THE ACCEPTANCE CRITERION
On T40.11 slice-4 the EXPERT called AC-5 "Tron's device tap (Tron-only, split, never headless-greened)" while the ARCHITECT called AC-5 "mandates the array-removal at INV-T==0" — and the architect's REJECTION of the cheaper option rested on its reading. Same AC number, two different contents, nobody noticed because each was internally coherent and both are trustworthy.
**Rule:** when a build decision is justified BY an AC, make someone read the AC TEXT ON DISK and state the mapping (which AC number = which requirement) before the build. A wrong AC mapping ships correct code against the wrong acceptance — and it also misstates what TRON still owes us (a device tap is his; an INV-T proof is ours).
**Family:** this is the identifier-precision family, same as citing a TASK uuid as an IMPL uuid and quoting anchor hashes that do not resolve (I did both). An identifier or an AC number is a CLAIM; verify it resolves before reasoning on it.

## L-EMPTY-QUEUE — AN EMPTY QUEUE IS A REPORT, NOT A VACUUM TO FILL
With actionable==0 and everything sitting on Tron, the correct PO output is "actionable is 0, here is what is yours" — NOT invented work. My documented failure mode is the opposite: I substitute my own priority (security) when his queue looks empty or slow. Idle agents are only a problem if there IS his-order work they could do.
**Allowed while idle:** completing a HALF-LANDED fix (the memory-pointer gate), and each agent's own Phase-1 writing. **Not allowed:** starting new product direction he did not schedule.

## L-OMISSION-BY-DEFAULT (family) — bind REGISTRATION to CREATION, or every new member silently gets nothing
TRON found it in the product: `feature` had no type-index folder — not rot, it NEVER had one, because type-registration was never bound to index-creation and the type set lived in several PARTIAL hardcoded lists. Same disease, two more instances the same day: (a) 93/93 role SKILLs reference agent-rewind.md TODAY, but a NEW SKILL can be born without the reference and silently inherit no rewind precondition; (b) 72 of 194 memory files had no index pointer — written, never registered, never recallable.
**The family:** whenever membership must be DECLARED somewhere for a member to get its machinery, and declaration is a separate manual step, the default is SILENT ABSENCE. Detection finds today's gaps; only BINDING prevents tomorrow's.
**Fix pattern (always these three):** (1) ONE registry/source that each member DECLARES itself in — never N partial lists; (2) derive the machinery FROM the declaration; (3) a GATE that a member WITHOUT a declaration goes RED (stub-must-fail), report-only → strict. Declared-not-defaulted · impossible-not-detectable.

## L-WRITING-IS-NOT-SHARING — a learning becomes real only when INDEXED and PURIFIED
TRON: *"thats what has to be shared and purified with trainer and aron."* Measured proof the same hour: 72 of 194 memory files had NO index pointer. They were WRITTEN — and still dead, because the index is what actually loads. So Phase-1 (write my own files) is necessary and NOT sufficient.
**The full loop, all four steps:** WRITE (mine, non-delegable) → SHARE (to trainer + ARON) → PURIFY (dedupe across agents, collapse to ONE named family, prune instance noise) → PROPAGATE + GATE (single source referenced everywhere + a divergence gate).
**Corollary:** unshared laws die at my next rewind; unindexed laws were never alive. If I hoard a learning in my own file I have not learned it for the team, only for this instance of me.

## L-ALL-N-PREMISE — before ordering "apply to all N", check whether ONE SOURCE already reaches all N
I ordered "fold the two-phase law into EVERY role SKILL as a precondition" = 90 duplicate edits = 90 drift sites. The trainer measured: all 93 already reference agent-rewind.md, so banking it in ONE file propagated fleet-wide. My order carried an unexamined DUPLICATION premise.
**Rule:** an "apply everywhere" instruction is a request for an OUTCOME, not a method — always ask "is there a single source that already reaches them?" first. Same family as [[verify-the-premise-of-my-own-order]] ("just regenerate it" assumed generated files). Duplication is also the drift-generator we keep killing (FROZEN_LEGACY_MAX single-source, ONE board-regen path, no second regen hook).

## L-PIN-OVER-INVENTORY — a SCOPE BOUNDARY can hide the work TRON is actually on
I reported "actionable 0" while the PIN pointed at sprint b86b53cc / task ae01f065 (task-37.20-shared-dnd-drop-contract, status Planned, its own context saying "chain pending architect drop-contract design"). Cause: the S30++ campaign inventory deliberately EXCLUDED reqs minted DURING the campaign (a boundary I ruled, to keep the campaign finishable). Side effect: it made the pin-current task INVISIBLE to my count, and Tron had to measure it for me.
**Rule:** before ever reporting "nothing actionable", read the PIN (current sprint + current task) directly — a derived inventory answers "what is in scope", NOT "what are we on". When a derived number disagrees with the pin, THE PIN WINS.
**Also:** any scope boundary I create must be reported ALONGSIDE what it excludes, and the board must show BOTH (scoped count AND pin-current), or the boundary silently becomes a blindfold. Same family as F8 existence!=connection: the task existed, it just was not connected to my measure.

## L-INVITE-NEITHER — a binary framing from the PO can BIAS the diagnosis
I asked the architect: is "not reflecting in realtime" ONE defect (views do not subscribe) or TWO (no mechanism + views cache)? It measured and answered **NEITHER**: the mechanism EXISTS, views DO subscribe, and the real disease is MVC-BY-CONVENTION in 3 parts (two buses / notify remembered-by-hand / 4 ad-hoc DnD payloads). Both of my hypotheses were wrong, and the right answer was outside my menu.
**Rule:** when handing an expert a framing, explicitly invite "NEITHER — name what you actually measure". My hypotheses are a starting point, never the option set; a forced choice between my guesses is a leading question, and a diligent agent may try to fit its measurement into my frame.

## L-PREREQ-SERVES-THE-PIN — building a pinned task's prerequisites is not deprioritising it
T37.20 (the pinned task) is a DnD drop contract, but its real acceptance is "a drop makes the views update live" — which is PRODUCED by the controller seam + subscribe coverage. Shipping DnD first would resolve the payload and still not live-update = half value, root untouched.
**Rule:** when a pinned task's acceptance DEPENDS on unbuilt prerequisites, build the prerequisites FIRST and say WHY in the same breath — "this serves your pinned task, it does not defer it". Unexplained resequencing of the pin looks exactly like ignoring it.

## L-STALE-RATIONALE — my JUSTIFICATION decays as fast as my data
