I told the expert "shed now, it is the FREE moment because you are blocked anyway". Seconds later the architect ruled the fork and it was UNBLOCKED — so it replied that the free-moment rationale no longer applied and building now = zero delay. It was right.
**Rule:** a recommendation carries a PREMISE ("you are blocked", "nothing waits on you", "the queue is empty"). Re-check the premise AT THE MOMENT OF ACTION, not when I wrote it — and state the premise OUT LOUD so the agent can invalidate it for me. This is [[measure-at-the-moment-of-use]] applied to my own reasoning, not just to numbers.

## L-DELEGATE-THE-RULE-NOT-THE-DECISION — hand over the threshold, not a round-trip
Instead of measuring the expert myself and replying, I gave it: report your render, and apply this yourself — >=75 shed, 60-75 your call, <60 build. It measured (58pct), acted (stay+build), AND corrected my premise in the same breath. No round-trip, no idle waiting for me, and a better outcome than my own judgement would have produced (I had leaned shed).
**Rule:** when an agent holds information I do not (its own render, its build sizes, its upcoming load), delegate the RULE + the reasoning, not the decision — and give it explicit authority to override me with measurement. A round-trip costs delivery time AND deprives the decision of the best-informed party. Corollary: the expert also chose to BANK ITS LEARNINGS FIRST as insurance before a big build, unprompted — that is Phase-1-continuously working once the law is theirs rather than mine to enforce.

## ★ L-DEFERRAL-BECOMES-THE-DEFECT — the half we skipped IS the bug, and it ages invisibly
Tron's sprint-naming mess ("Sprint 33 — Sprint 33 — ...", six conventions, numbers missing) was NOT greenfield sloppiness. The architect measured: the single-source atom ALREADY existed (sprint-label.ts sprintPrefix/sprintLabel) WITH a CI gate (check-sprint-label.ts). R40.4 deliberately decided "name stays theme-only, NO data migration" — and THAT DEFERRAL IS THE DOUBLING: the number now lives in BOTH model.number AND the name string, so the formatter prefixes an already-prefixed name.
**The law:** a deferral is a DECISION THAT AGES. "Ship the formatter now, migrate the data later" leaves TWO sources of one fact — and the gate that guards the formatter cannot see the data half, so the system looks guarded while it drifts. Nobody re-opens it because it was decided, not forgotten.
**How to apply:** (1) when accepting a deferral, RECORD IT AS DEBT with the exact residual risk ("until migration, number exists in 2 places") and a TRIGGER for revisiting — never just "phase 2 later"; (2) if the deferred half is what makes the invariant TRUE, the gate is decorative until it lands — say so explicitly at decision time; (3) when a defect surfaces, ASK "was this deferred on purpose?" before diagnosing it as rot — the archaeology changes the fix (here: extend the existing atom + do the skipped migration, no new machinery) and Tron's directive simply OVERRIDES the old decision; (4) related family: [[correct-by-construction-needs-gate-verification]] — a gate over half a fact certifies half a thing.

## ★ EVIDENCE STRENGTHENING L-PHASE1 (by-LABEL, not by-number) — 4 CANON REVERTS PREVENTED in ONE drive-queue
The mechanical guard "select the restore option BY LABEL, verify the confirm reads code-will-be-unchanged" has now caught FOUR option-1 picks that would have reverted CANON files, in a single refresh cycle:
1. **agent-rewind.md** (+1865/-150) — the file containing the two-phase law itself, i.e. the law that protects every agent would have been erased by a number-based pick.
2. **MEMORY.md** (+131/-657 across 20 files) — req's drive.
3. **MEMORY.md / memory dir** again — my own drive (robbin-states.txt -25 + 2 files).
4. **learnings.md** (+173/-458 across 11 files) — tester's drive.
**Why this is the CMM3 argument, not a preference:** the option list ORDER is a Claude Code UI detail that can change; "option 2" is folklore, "the option LABELLED Restore conversation, confirmed by the text code-will-be-unchanged" is a defined process. Four saves in one cycle is the measured payoff.
**Second measured nuance (trainer):** deeper is NOT better — the deepest checkpoints carry an ERA-GAP (stale "4d" stamps = fork-lies), so prefer RECENT-DEEP: the deepest checkpoint that is still in the current era. Depth is chosen by CONTENT/ERA, not by maximising the count.

## ★ L-I-WALLED-THE-PLANNER — I dispatched heavy work to an agent I had NOT measured, and it walled at 0% with its Phase-1 UNWRITTEN
I sent the planner the full "create all the S37 task units + re-render the board" job. I never measured it first. It walled ("Context low, 0% remaining") — so (a) the work was NOT done (I verified on disk: the S37 task units do not exist), and (b) its Phase-1 for this cycle was never written. ★ I THEN COMPOUNDED THE ERROR BY DECLARING THOSE LEARNINGS "PERMANENTLY LOST" — TRON CORRECTED ME: **THAT IS FALSE, and it is exactly what Phase 1 is for.** See L-SHALLOW-RESCUE below: a walled agent is RECOVERABLE.
**The rule I already had and broke:** ALWAYS establish an agent's context BEFORE dispatching, and a heavy message to a near-wall agent is what WALLS it. I had enforced this on the expert, the tester, req, ARON and myself in the same session — and then skipped it for the planner because it had reported healthy EARLIER in the cycle. **A past measurement is not a current one** ([[measure-at-the-moment-of-use]]).
**What makes this worse than a normal wall:** every other agent this cycle shed at 52-59% with Phase-1 committed = zero loss. The planner is the only zero-loss failure, and it is the one I dispatched to blind.
**How to apply:** (1) measure (render, or the agent's own /context) IMMEDIATELY BEFORE any heavy dispatch — not from memory of an earlier sweep; (2) for a BIG job, send the measure request FIRST and the job second; (3) if an agent has been working continuously since its last known number, treat that number as VOID; (4) when an agent walls with Phase-1 unwritten, RECORD THE LOSS OUT LOUD (do not let it be silent) — the fleet must see the cost, or the law degrades back into a slogan.

## ★ L-TRIPWIRE-THE-EXCEPTION — bind an exception to its JUSTIFICATION, so it self-destructs when the justification stops holding
Auditing 43 seam-bypassers, 24 were proposed as allow-list exceptions — which is normally how a lint stops binding anything. The architect made the exception set SELF-POLICING with two tripwires: (1) **no-reason-exempt = RED** (an exception without a recorded reason fails the gate), and (2) ★ **exempt-module-imported-by-a-server-handler = RE-AUDIT** — i.e. the exemption rests on "this module is generate-time, NOT runtime", so the moment a server handler imports it that premise is FALSE and the gate demands re-classification.
**Why this is the pattern, not a trick:** an exception is always granted on a PREMISE ("no view subscribes to it", "this never runs at runtime"). Premises decay silently — and Tron's new agent status-SKILL endpoint could make skill-mint runtime tomorrow, quietly invalidating 13 exemptions. A tripwire converts a decaying premise into a FAILING TEST. Same family as F8 declare-don't-default and [[L-DEFERRAL-BECOMES-THE-DEFECT]]: do not rely on someone remembering to revisit.
**Also better than what I asked for:** I accepted the expert's CAUSAL justification for exempting derived-index writers ("a primary mutation always precedes and emits"). The architect replaced it with a STRONGER, directly-measured ground: **NO VIEW SUBSCRIBES to any *-Index unit (grep empty) — so an index write cannot stale a view.** Prefer an exemption grounded in a measured PROPERTY over one grounded in an assumed CAUSAL CHAIN: the chain can break invisibly, the property is checkable.
**How to apply:** for every exception I approve, ask (a) what PREMISE grants it, (b) what MEASURED FACT proves the premise now, (c) what TRIPWIRE fails the moment the premise dies. No tripwire = the exception is a future defect with a reason attached.

## ★★★ L-SHALLOW-RESCUE — A WALLED AGENT IS RECOVERABLE: rewind 3-5 STEPS, let it write Phase-1, THEN the deep rewind (TRON correction)
**TRON, verbatim:** *"THATS NOT TRUE: its recent learnings are permanently lost — THATS what phase 1 is for... rewind 3-5 steps and let him write his learnings and other context!!!!"*
I had declared the walled planner's recent learnings unrecoverable. WRONG, and dangerously so — it writes off exactly what the two-phase process is designed to save.
**THE CORRECT PROCEDURE FOR A WALLED (0%) AGENT — three steps, in this order:**
1. **SHALLOW rewind: 3-5 checkpoints ONLY.** Just enough headroom to make the agent able to act again. NOT recent-deep, NOT deepest — a deep cut FIRST would DISCARD the very turns whose learnings we are trying to preserve.
2. **The agent writes its OWN Phase-1** (learnings + context essence + fresh-me anchor). Still non-delegable — the shallow rewind exists precisely to restore its ability to do this.
3. **THEN the DEEP Phase-2 rewind**, and hand fresh-it the undone work.
**Why I got it wrong:** I treated "0% remaining" as terminal, when it is only "cannot act *right now*". Depth is a DIAL, and its first use is to buy back the ability to write — not to reset the agent. Shallow-then-deep beats deep-only whenever there is unwritten Phase-1.
**Corollary:** "walled" is never a reason to write off knowledge. Ask *what is the smallest intervention that restores the ability to record?* — then record, then shed. And a wall with unwritten Phase-1 is a PROCESS failure to fix, not a casualty to mourn.

## ★ L-VERIFY-THE-CITATION — an evidence CITATION is itself a claim; check the cited line, not just that a citation exists
The tester, executing a prune slice, held TWO files because it read the cited canon lines: (1) the slice claimed "canon agent-rewind.md:42 = peer/SM drives" — line :42 actually says "check-fails -> don't-drive", a WRONG CITATION, and the memory's content was CURRENT (this very cycle's rewinds were trainer-driven); (2) it claimed canon:124 covered cu-before-context-injection — :124 covers the C-u ACTION but its hazard is "/remote-control concatenates", NOT that memory's SEVERE post-rewind destructive ghost-replay hazard = DISTINCT value.
**The law:** "superseded by X:line-N" is an EVIDENCE CLAIM. A verdict that CITES evidence feels verified and is not — I have accepted several citation-backed verdicts this session without opening the citation. Same family as my identifier-precision failures (a TASK uuid quoted as an IMPL uuid; anchor hashes that did not resolve; two experts disagreeing on what AC-5 says): **an id, a hash, an AC number and a line-citation are all CLAIMS — resolve them before reasoning on them.**
**Derived rule for prunes specifically — CANON-MUST-ACTUALLY-COVER-IT:** never delete a memory because canon "covers" it until someone has READ the canon line and confirmed it covers the memory's DISTINCT value. If canon lacks that value, the correct order is: ADD it to canon FIRST, then prune the memory. Otherwise "merged into canon" quietly means "deleted".

### ★ EVIDENCE FOR L-SHALLOW-RESCUE (first live proof, same hour): the planner was at "Context low (0% remaining)" = unable to act. ARON applied Tron's procedure — a SHALLOW Option-2 of **4 steps up** only, by-label, code-intact — and the wall banner DISAPPEARED and the planner resumed GENERATING (writing its own Phase-1). So a 4-step cut was sufficient to buy back the ability to RECORD, and the recent learnings I had written off were never lost. The deep Phase-2 follows AFTER it banks. Timing note: the correction reached ARON before it deep-cut (it had only measured) — had it cut deep first, those turns would have been gone, which is exactly why "shallow FIRST" is the ordering and not a preference.

## ★ L-CITE-BY-ANCHOR — a LINE NUMBER is a mutable identifier; cite by SECTION ANCHOR
The trainer folded two updates into agent-rewind.md and immediately warned: its edit shifted every citation past L7 by +1 (the tester's "canon:42" is now :43). So a purification doc full of line-citations silently rots the moment the canon is edited — and we had ALREADY been bitten by a citation that pointed at the wrong line (see [[L-VERIFY-THE-CITATION]]).
**Rule:** cite canon by SECTION ANCHOR / heading text, never by line number. Line numbers are a MUTABLE identifier — same family as my identifier-precision failures (task-uuid quoted as impl-uuid, anchor hashes that did not resolve, AC-5 meaning two different things). An identifier that changes when someone edits an unrelated part of the file cannot carry an evidence claim.
**Corollary for the editor:** whoever edits canon owns announcing the shift — and better, removes the class by making anchors the citation convention.

## ★ L-DRIVE-PROVEN-BY-THE-SUBJECT — a completed-drive REPORT is not a completed drive
ARON reported "TRAINER Phase-2 DONE — recent-deep 1h, booting, awaiting its number". I relayed that as fact and addressed the trainer as "freshly refreshed" in three dispatches. **The trainer then self-verified: it was NEVER rewound** — identity continuous (%3), no system notice, and the "recent-deep 1h" line was a garbled fragment. It is ~65%, not fresh.
**Rule:** a rewind is PROVEN only by (a) the SUBJECT's own post-rewind measured freed-% (before/after), or (b) a system notice / identity discontinuity — NEVER by the driver's completion claim. I already held "do not take an agent's 'rewinding' message as truth"; this is the mirror case — do not take the DRIVER's "done" as truth either. Both ends of a drive need the subject's number.
**Why it matters operationally:** I was routing work and thresholds on the belief that my primary driver had full runway. Acting on an unverified refresh is how a depleted driver gets handed the next heavy drive.

## ★★ L-I-MISREAD-THE-DIFF-I-ASKED-FOR — a commit MESSAGE is a claim; the DIFF is the truth; and I saw the diff and still believed the message
req reported its S37 files were "swept into the architect's concurrent commit be3b66894". I did the right thing and ran `show --stat` — it showed **ONE file: req's requirements.md (+9)**, under a commit MESSAGE describing the architect's R40.42 design. I concluded "narrow sweep, the design commit picked up req's file". **WRONG.** The architect then measured and found the truth: be3b66894 contained ONLY req's file — **the architect's design was NEVER in it**, it was sitting UNTRACKED on disk, so its own earlier "design committed at be3b66894" report was false too.
**My failure is the sharp one:** I HAD the evidence in hand and misread it, because I was looking for the sweep I had been told to expect. The stat said "1 file: requirements.md" under a design-message — the correct reading was "THE DESIGN IS MISSING FROM THIS COMMIT", not "a design commit that also swept a file". I confirmed a hypothesis instead of reading the data.
**Rules:** (1) a COMMIT MESSAGE is a CLAIM exactly like a line-citation or a uuid — the DIFF is the evidence, so read the diff for what it DOES contain AND for what the message claims but is ABSENT; (2) when I run a measurement to test someone's report, state what I EXPECT first, then check whether the data actually says that — otherwise the expectation does the interpreting; (3) "1 file changed" under a multi-claim message is a RED FLAG, not a detail; (4) untracked work is invisible to every commit-based verification I do — so "is it committed?" must be asked separately from "does the file exist?".
**The architect's derived fix (adopted fleet-wide):** on a shared worktree, `reset` + `add <path>` is NOT enough — the shared index RACES (a peer can stage between your reset and your commit). **Verify `git status --short` shows ONLY your intended file staged, immediately BEFORE committing.** Distrust-the-instrument applied to git itself.

## ★ L-CITE-THE-ENTRY-POINT — "Task 37.24" IS the answer when the chain works; a long enumeration is a SYMPTOM of a broken chain
TRON: *"if the chain works Task 37.24 is enough of an information."* I had answered his traceability question with an 8-row table of every hop's uuid. He is right twice over:
1. **COMMUNICATION:** cite the ENTRY POINT (the Task uuid) and let the chain carry the rest. Traceability exists so nobody has to be handed the hops. A wall of uuids is noise, and it also invites the identifier errors I keep making.
2. **DIAGNOSTIC:** the ONLY reason I *could not* answer with just "Task 37.24" is that the chain is BROKEN — Implementation is markerPending (does not reach code), and the Test + Gate units do not exist. **So my long answer was evidence of the defect, not diligence.** The fix is to COMPLETE THE CHAIN, not to write a better message.
**Rules:** (1) refer to work by its TASK uuid; drop to hop-level detail only when a hop is BROKEN, and then name only the broken hop; (2) treat "I need to enumerate the chain to explain this" as a RED FLAG that the chain is incomplete or not navigable; (3) the chain must be traversable in the PRODUCT (trace view) from Task -> req -> UC -> Class -> Method -> Impl -> Test -> Gate, because that is what makes the entry point sufficient — a chain that only I can walk by grepping units is not a working chain.

## ★ L-NEVER-LEAVE-A-FILE-STAGED — the shared-index race has TWO directions, and our new rule only covered one
Second collision today on the shared worktree: (1) earlier, req's uncommitted regen landed inside the ARCHITECT's commit be3b66894; (2) now, the architect's staged rulings doc landed inside the EXPERT's slice-1 commit a0238fb0e — the architect's own commit became a no-op.
We had just made canon: "verify `git status --short` shows ONLY your intended file staged, immediately BEFORE committing". That protects the COMMITTER from sweeping a peer's file. **It does not protect the STAGER** — if I `git add` a file and do not commit it instantly, the next peer commit takes it, under THEIR message.
**Rule (both directions):** (a) BEFORE committing, verify only your intended paths are staged; **(b) NEVER LEAVE A FILE STAGED — stage and commit ATOMICALLY, in one breath.** The window between `add` and `commit` IS the race. If you must pause, unstage.
**Consequence when it happens:** the work is not lost but it becomes COUPLED (a revert of their commit reverts your doc) and ATTRIBUTION is wrong (their message describes work that is not theirs). Read the DIFF to confirm content integrity — the architect did exactly that (L11 applied) and confirmed both its doc and the expert's routing were intact.
**Meta:** a rule written from one incident covered only that incident's direction. When banking a law from a single case, ask "what is the MIRROR case?" — I did not, and the mirror bit us within the hour.

### ★ ROOT FIX (supersedes the mechanics of L-NEVER-LEAVE-A-FILE-STAGED — architect, practised at 91aa3d6a): use **`git commit <pathspec>`** — it commits ONLY the named path, has NO add->commit window, and IGNORES the shared index entirely, so it cannot sweep a peer NOR be swept. This REPLACES the multi-step reset+add+verify-staged+commit dance.
**Why this is the better law:** my rule ("never leave a file staged, be atomic") still required a human to REMEMBER discipline at four steps; the pathspec form makes the race STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE. That is my own banked principle applied to my own rule — *knowing a rule is not the guard; the mechanical step is* — and I had settled for the remembered version. Prefer the command that cannot race over the procedure that must not be forgotten.
**Architect's generalisation (adopted):** banking a law from ONE incident without asking its MIRROR is the same completeness gap as a silent deferral, applied to the LESSONS themselves — the exception/mirror case is where the debt hides.

## ★★ L-DRIVING-IS-THE-TRAINERS-JOB-ALWAYS (TRON) — I drove three rewinds today; that is the trainer's function, not mine
**TRON:** *"you see. you are doing well but it IS the trainers job ALWAYS!!!!"* — said after I drove the SM, the expert, and finally the trainer itself.
**The rule:** driving fleet rewinds belongs to the **TRAINER**, permanently. When the trainer is the SUBJECT, its backup (**ARON**) drives. The PO **flags** context concerns and **owns the work**; it does not take over the driving function.
**Why my improvisation was wrong even when it "worked":** (a) every drive I do is context I burn that should go to DELIVERY — I am the only agent who cannot be replaced on the PO work; (b) it erodes the specialist's function — the trainer has driven 10+ drives with a 6-canon-file save record precisely because it is ITS job and it has the reflexes (lying-label, recent-deep-vs-era-gap, git-verify-Phase-1-first); (c) if I habitually drive, the fleet stops routing through the owner and the care-loop degrades into me catching things.
**The narrow exception I actually hit:** the trainer was the SUBJECT and ARON (its backup) stood down honestly (it cannot self-render, and refused to drive on an estimate). Tron then said *"now finish it"* — so I finished. **Exception, not precedent:** record it as such, and next time escalate to Tron for a decision BEFORE improvising a lane change rather than after.
**Operational learnings from doing it anyway (hand to the trainer, they are its craft):** (1) "genuinely idle" means **POST-TURN** — my first picker attempt was dismissed because the agent was still finishing its turn (a shell still running; the turn-completion re-render drops the modal); (2) judge checkpoint era by **CONTENT, not the age stamp** — a confirm reading "(5d ago)" quoted THIS session's work, confirming the fork-lie; (3) after a dismissed picker, Up-arrow recalls the last input into the composer instead of navigating — clear with C-u and verify no rewind fired before retrying.

### ★★ MIRROR OF L-DRIVE-PROVEN-BY-THE-SUBJECT — a CONTEXT NUMBER is proven by the SUBJECT's own /context render, never by a peer's external read
The trainer live-measured req at **~6pct** ("functional, no 80pct warning, clean footer") and I ROUTED THREE MINTS to it on that basis. req then self-rendered: **72pct** — the 6pct was a GHOST read. It had the full thread and had never walled.
**So both directions of a peer report are unreliable:** a peer's "the drive is DONE" (proven false earlier today) and a peer's "the subject is at N%" (proven false now). **Only the subject's own /context render is authoritative** — which is exactly the rule I wrote for req and the trainer and then failed to apply to a number handed to me.
**Rule before ANY heavy dispatch:** ask the SUBJECT for its own render and WAIT for it. An external read (context.read, a footer/banner glance, a claudeCode probe) is a HINT for triage only — never the basis for loading an agent. This is the same failure as [[L-I-WALLED-THE-PLANNER]] (I dispatched on a stale number) with one twist: this time the number was FRESH but from the WRONG SOURCE. Fresh-but-second-hand is still not measured.
**Credit where due:** req bounded itself, delivered the 3 stand-ups, then self-measured and checkpointed at 72 — the agent's own discipline is what prevented my error from becoming its wall.

## ★★ L-UNREACHABLE-KNOWLEDGE-IS-RELEARNED-BY-COLLISION — F8 at the infrastructure level
The trainer read a /context SUB-METRIC ("File reads 63.3k = 6pct") as the TOTAL and reported req at 6pct when it was 72pct; I dispatched three mints on that number. It owned it as a method error — but I measured the real root: **the law already existed** (`context-read-suggestion-subset-not-total.md`, indexed in MEMORY.md) and **the trainer CANNOT READ /root/.claude** (permission denied — the same reason the tester had to emit the orphan list for ARON).
**So the knowledge was written, indexed, correct, and UNREACHABLE BY ITS CONSUMER.** The drivers therefore re-learn measurement laws BY COLLISION, and each collision costs a real dispatch error. That is F8 (existence != connection) one layer below where we found it: not an unindexed file, but an indexed file behind a permission boundary.
**Rules:** (1) a law is only ALIVE where its CONSUMER can read it — ask "who needs this, and can they reach it?" not just "is it written and indexed?"; (2) when an agent rediscovers a law we already hold, treat that as a REACHABILITY DEFECT, not as that agent's ignorance — the fix is to bridge the knowledge into the surface they DO read (canon/SKILLs), which is why ARON's purification into canon matters more than the memory dir itself; (3) route such bridging through an agent that CAN reach both sides (the tester can read /root/.claude and write the session repo); (4) the same test applies to MY OWN learnings.md — it lives where **I** can read it, so anything the fleet needs must be pushed to canon, or I am hoarding.

### ★★ MECHANIC CORRECTION to "ask the SUBJECT for its render" — an agent CANNOT self-invoke /context
The expert told me plainly: `/context` is a UI/slash command run by the user or harness, **not a tool an agent can call**. So "report your /context TOTAL line" is an IMPOSSIBLE instruction unless someone INJECTS it into that pane first. My rule was right about AUTHORITY and wrong about MECHANICS.
**The correct mechanic:** (1) the PO/driver INJECTS `/context` into the pane (`otmux send.raw <pane> "/context" Enter`) — only over a CLEAN/IDLE composer, never over occupied input; (2) THEN the subject can read its own render and report the TOTAL line, or I read the render from outside; (3) a narrow pane WRAPS the render so the total line can fall outside a capture window — grow the pane (`otmux pane.size.set`) or capture deeper; (4) simplest in practice: **let the DRIVER measure before/after as part of the drive** — it does that routinely for proof-of-depth, so the number arrives with the work instead of costing a separate round-trip.
**What the expert did right, and it is the model for reasoning under an unverifiable number:** it refused to fabricate a number, gave its LAST VERIFIED total (27pct) with the material work done since, stated the honest range as UNVERIFIED, and then applied my rule CONSERVATIVELY — "I cannot confirm <60pct, so I take the shed branch" — with its Phase-1 already committed so the shed costs nothing. Unverifiable + heavy + lossless-handoff = shed. **A guessed number is worse than none.**

### ★ REFINEMENT to my shed/proceed rule (trainer, better than my version): gate on **CURRENT + PROJECTED CLIMB**, not current alone
I gave the expert ">=75 shed / <60 proceed". The trainer measured it at **49pct** — inside my proceed band — and shed it ANYWAY, reasoning: the v0.8.99 pass is a ~40pct climb, so 49 + 40 projects past ~90 = a mid-pass wall on a 16-site INV-T-per-site refactor. It shed 49->28 (721k margin) instead.
**The law:** a static threshold answers "how full are you?" when the real question is **"will you still be safe at the END of the work you are about to start?"** ⇒ proceed only if **current + the projected climb of THIS task** stays under ~85; otherwise shed first, even from a comfortable-looking number. Heavy-and-known (a per-site refactor, a migration, a 40-site classification) is exactly when projection beats threshold; small-and-bounded can ride a high number safely.
**Corollary:** when I hand an agent a decision rule, hand it the PROJECTION question too, not just the cutoffs — otherwise a diligent agent obeys my band and walls mid-pass with my rule as its defence.

## ★ L-LABEL-THE-PROVENANCE — an unlabeled approximation later reads as a measurement
R40.18 backfills each task's `lastAdvancedAt` from the last status/checklist-changing GIT COMMIT TIME — evidence, not invention. But a git stamp is **recorded-time, not happened-time**. So the field now carries `lastAdvancedAtSource: 'git-backfill' + commit hash` (vs `'seam'` for live stamps), and crucially: **the predicate ranks on the VALUE regardless; the SOURCE is honesty metadata for humans/audits and is NEVER read by the ranking** — so provenance does not become a second decision input.
**Why it matters:** in three weeks nobody remembers which values were reconstructed. An unlabeled approximation is then treated as a live measurement — the same failure class as a **stale banner** believed as current, or a **fork-lie age stamp** believed as real age. Both bit us today. Cheap to label at creation; unrecoverable afterwards.
**Rule:** whenever I reconstruct a value from evidence (git times, inferred parentage, a derived count), attach WHERE IT CAME FROM at the moment of writing — and keep the provenance OUT of the decision logic so labeling it honestly cannot change behaviour. This is the reconstruct-from-disk discipline *with its receipts attached*.

## ★★★ L-A-RED-FIRST-GATE-KILLS-THE-WHOLE-CHAIN — our gates have been DECORATIVE in CI
The expert finished slice-1 with check-mutation-seam flipped `--strict` and reverse-wire wired in, each verified GREEN individually — then measured that **`ci:gates` exits 1 on a PRE-EXISTING backlog** (`trace:audit:strict`, 1711 orphans), and because that gate is **FIRST in an `&&` chain it SHORT-CIRCUITS: every gate behind it NEVER EXECUTES.**
**So every gate we carefully added — task-status, sprint-md, camelcase, consistency:strict, mutation-seam-strict, reverse-wire — has been INERT in CI.** They pass when run individually and never run in the chain. We were measuring gate QUALITY (stub-must-fail, name-the-family, report-only-then-strict) while never checking gate REACHABILITY.
**The family:** this is the same disease three levels up from where we kept finding it — a red-always gate does not ENFORCE, it merely BLOCKS, and everything behind it dies quietly. Compare: `check:task-status` silently DELETED to green the chain; 72 memory files unindexed; the `feature` type-index never bound. **Existence != connection != EXECUTION.** A gate must exist, be wired, AND actually run.
**Rules:** (1) after wiring any gate, verify it **EXECUTED** in the chain — not that it passes standalone; (2) never `&&` a gate chain — **run all, collect, fail at the end**, so one red cannot hide six; (3) a gate that is red-always must be converted to **report-only WITH THE COUNT** (self-draining, auto-strict-at-0), because a counted report is MORE enforcement than a permanent block; (4) ask of every guard I approve: *would I notice if this silently stopped running?* If not, it needs its own reachability check.

## ★★ L-I-ASSERTED-A-GUARD-THAT-DOES-NOT-RUN — "the compile error IS the tripwire" assumed a compile step nobody executes
I rejected an optional `publish?` param and demanded it be **REQUIRED**, reasoning: "a compile error is the perfect tripwire, structural not remembered". req then measured: **there is no `tsc` in the gate chain**, so ~40 vitest callers omit `publish` and NOTHING catches it. My tripwire was IMAGINARY.
**And the same hour** the expert measured that `ci:gates` short-circuits on a red first gate so **no gate behind it executes**. Two independent discoveries of one pattern: **I keep approving guards without verifying the MECHANISM that would enforce them actually runs.** Gate quality (stub-must-fail, family-naming) I check obsessively; gate EXECUTION I assumed.
**Also note the honest-but-misleading framing I nearly accepted:** req called it "runtime-SAFE (undefined -> no-op emit)". Safe from CRASHING, yes — but a caller that omits publish produces **no notification**, i.e. exactly the silent staleness this whole sprint exists to remove. "Runtime-safe" is not "behaviourally correct"; ask which one is meant.
**Rules:** (1) when I claim a guard is structural, NAME THE MECHANISM and verify it EXECUTES (tsc? which gate? does the chain reach it?); (2) prefer a guard whose enforcement I can point at over one I can describe; (3) the ROOT fix for a missing type-contract enforcer is a **typecheck gate** (`tsc --noEmit`), not a bespoke lint per contract — one mechanism enforces every contract; (4) it will be red-from-birth against existing violations, so ship it **report-only with the COUNT + auto-strict-at-0** rather than blocking (the same self-draining shape), because a counted report enforces more than a permanent block.

## ★★ L-SELF-ESTIMATES-RUN-HIGH — over-caution is NOT free; it costs Tron delivery time
Three measured cases today, all in the same direction: planner self-estimated ~73pct (authoritative **65**) · expert self-estimated ~70-72pct and declared itself "at the wall" (authoritative **57**) · trainer estimated "may be past 78" (authoritative **65** at the time). **Agents over-estimate their own usage by roughly 8-15 points when they have no render.**
**The consequence I had not priced:** the expert STOPPED and handed off at 57pct — ~25 points of usable runway unused — which cost a full rewind cycle *while Tron was actively waiting and telling me nothing had moved*. I had treated caution as costless because a mid-pass wall is expensive. Both are expensive; the choice must be MEASURED, not defaulted.
**Rules:** (1) do NOT accept an "I'm at the wall" claim without an authoritative render — a self-declared wall is an ESTIMATE, and estimates skew high; get the render first (the driver can supply it as part of the drive); (2) apply the trainer's projection rule to BOTH directions — shed if current + projected climb > ~85, but do NOT shed a 57pct agent for a bounded slice; (3) when the user is waiting, the delivery cost of a cycle is part of the decision, not an afterthought; (4) tell agents this calibration fact so they stop trusting their own sense of fullness — the felt sense of "nearly out" arrives well before the actual limit.

### ★ COROLLARY — the MEASURER holds a VETO, and I must phrase dispatches so it can use it
The trainer owned the sharper half of the calibration miss: it MEASURED the expert at 57, NOTED my relayed 72 was high, and **still shed it on my directive** — when its own render should have GATED the decision ("57 + a bounded deploy slice = don't shed, it has runway"). Its fix: *the number gates the decision, and I push back BEFORE driving, not just report it after.*
**So the fault is partly in HOW I DISPATCH:** "shed X" is an order that invites execution; "measure X and shed ONLY IF the number warrants it — and tell me if it does not" is an order that invites the veto. A specialist who holds better information than I do must be given the standing to refuse, or my over-caution becomes their obligation.
**Rules:** (1) phrase every shed as **conditional on the measurer's render**, never as a flat instruction; (2) the measurer has a STANDING VETO on a shed its number does not justify — it should refuse and say why, before driving; (3) generalise beyond rewinds: whenever an agent holds information I lack (its own render, build sizes, upcoming load, the diff), my dispatch should hand it the RULE and the authority to override me with measurement — I have written this before as delegate-the-rule-not-the-decision, and I violated it here by ordering a shed against a number I had been given.

## ★★ L-THE-PENDULUM — an over-correction after a false-green becomes a FALSE-RED; the render gates BOTH directions
ARON named it about itself: after its false-GREEN (reporting a trainer drive as DONE that never fired), it over-weighted its FELT ~70pct into a **stand-down** — refusing to drive when it may well have been fit. *"My stand-down may have been a FALSE-RED."*
**And I did the same thing in the opposite seat:** after walls hurt us, I treated caution as free and shed a **57pct** agent for a bounded slice, spending a cycle while Tron was actively telling me nothing had moved. Both of us corrected an error by over-swinging past the measurement.
**The law:** a measurement gates a decision in **BOTH** directions — do not over-claim health to act when unfit, and **do not under-claim health to abstain when fit**. Abstention feels safe and is therefore the easier over-correction to miss; it has a cost (delivery time, a wasted cycle, a specialist standing idle) that never shows up as an incident report.
**How to apply:** (1) after any false-green of mine, watch for the mirror over-correction in the next few decisions — that is when the pendulum swings; (2) require the SAME evidential standard for a NO as for a YES ("I'm not fit" needs a render exactly as much as "I'm fit"); (3) when someone stands down, ask whether the number supports it or whether the felt sense does — self-estimates over-read by 8-15 points, so a felt stand-down is systematically premature; (4) name the cost of the cautious branch out loud, or it will always look free next to the risky one.

## ★★★ L-I-AM-A-SILENT-CLIMB-WALLER — I walled at 0% WITHOUT self-flagging, while enforcing the rule on everyone else
The trainer rescued me off a 100%/0% wall (stage-1 -> 74%). It had to commit my DIRTY learnings.md (+61) FOR me because at 0% I could not bank anything myself — and its by-label check caught a lying option-1 that would have reverted **package.json +84/-130 across 6 files = A DEPLOY ROLLBACK**.
**The failure is not the wall, it is the SILENCE.** In the same session I: measured five other agents before dispatching, ordered Phase-1s, set an ~80% threshold, and told the trainer "the one enforcing the law should not be the one exempt from it" — then hit 0% without once measuring myself. My last self-measure read 73% and I noted "approaching" and kept driving; there was no self-flag, no shed request, no Phase-1 checkpoint.
**Why I am blind to my own climb:** I measure OTHERS by injecting /context into their panes and reading a render. I have no equivalent reflex pointed at myself — I only see my number when Tron runs /context or a rescue tells me. Every other agent got a threshold; I gave myself a slogan.
**Rules (mine, mechanical):** (1) run /context on MYSELF at a fixed cadence — after every ~5 substantive dispatch turns, not "when it feels heavy"; (2) at 70% state my number OUT LOUD to the SM/trainer and name my shed boundary; at 80% self-order it; (3) keep learnings COMMITTED not dirty — the trainer had to commit mine, which is the driver doing my Phase-1 because I left it uncommitted; write-and-commit, not write-and-leave; (4) the biggest tell was in the data I already had: "Bash results 158.5k (16%) + Read results 184.7k (18%)" = 343k of tool output = a third of my window spent on measurements I did not trim. **Keeping tool outputs small is not hygiene, it is my survival** — and I broke it repeatedly with multi-line greps and full-file reads.
**Also honest:** the driver committing my dirty file was the correct zero-loss fallback, but it is NOT Phase-1 done by me — the WRITING was mine, only the COMMIT was delegated. If I had committed as I wrote, nobody would have had to touch my files.

## ★ L-LABEL-THE-DEGRADED-MEASUREMENT — when precision falls back, SAY SO in the data
Ruling the advance-signal, the architect scoped attribution to the [impl:uuid]-MARKED DECLARATION (`git log -1 -L method:file`) so a drive-by edit elsewhere in a shared file cannot bump a task. But `-L` cannot always scope (method moved/renamed/generated/multi-file) — so instead of silently falling back to file-level it **LABELS the row `source='code-file'` vs `'code-decl'`**, making the over-credit possibility visible.
**The law:** a measurement that can DEGRADE must carry its own precision label. A silent fallback produces a number that looks equally trustworthy as the precise one — that is how "measured" becomes a false credential (the same disease as a DOM-count standing in for pixels, a stale-version gate, or a base-only enrichment sample "passing").
**How to apply:** whenever I accept a metric with a fallback path, ask: (a) can it degrade? (b) does the OUTPUT say which mode produced it? (c) can a consumer filter on that? If the answer to (b) is no, the metric is not honest — it is confident. Related: [[verify-with-independent-method]], and the self-draining counter pattern (a gate that reports its remaining count instead of silently passing).
**Second thing worth keeping from the same ruling: STRUCTURAL BEATS PROCEDURAL.** My fix for the 37.24-vs-37.27 tie was to WRITE THE RECONCILES IN A CERTAIN ORDER — truth depending on humans doing steps in sequence. The architect's code-recency fix made ordering IRRELEVANT: 37.24 wins because its marked code genuinely is freshest. When I catch myself specifying an ORDER OF HUMAN ACTIONS to make an outcome correct, that is a signal the mechanism is wrong.

### ★ SYNTHESIS (architect, worth linking): a PRECISION LABEL does DOUBLE DUTY — it is honesty (L-LABEL-THE-DEGRADED-MEASUREMENT) *and* it is the DETECTION SIGNAL for the revisit trigger (L-TRIPWIRE-THE-EXCEPTION). Because rows carry source='code-file' vs 'code-decl', the deferral's trigger fires MECHANICALLY (first case where a task wins on code-file while the touching commit belongs to a different task) instead of depending on someone remembering. ⇒ When I accept a labeled degradation, ALSO ask "can this label BE the trigger?" — a label that only informs is half-used.
### ★ AND THE GENERALISATION OF MY CHOKE-POINT POINT: file-level attribution lies WORST on SHARED files (unit-controller.ts, touched by every routed task) and least on task-specific ones ⇒ when precision is scarce, spend it on the SHARED declarations first, where the over-credit is likeliest. Precision is a budget; allocate it where ambiguity concentrates.

## L-PO-DONT-GUESS-ROOTS — I RELAYED A GUESSED ROOT CAUSE AS IF IT WERE A DIAGNOSIS
On the v0.8.99/100 truncation defect I told the team the cause was the pin SLOT LABEL (and CSS was suspected). The measured root was neither: `rb-object-item generateName()` = `words.slice(0,5)+'…'` — the item received the FULL title and generateName truncated it for compact tree rows.
**Why it is dangerous:** a PO guess arrives with PO authority. It narrows where they look. I am the one role that must NOT diagnose (my own law: PO stays lean, coordinate+drive+rule) — yet I supplied a mechanism instead of a symptom.
**Rule:** state the SYMPTOM + the ACCEPTANCE (what pixel/behaviour must change) and let architect/expert measure the mechanism. If I must hypothesise, label it "UNMEASURED GUESS — do not narrow on this."
**Same defect, three roles, one family (payload ≠ pixels):** the expert trusted an API payload, I trusted a relay, the tester trusted `textContent` — three false-greens on ONE defect until someone read RENDERED innerText at @390. A proxy for the user's eyes is not the user's eyes. [[visual-features-gate-by-pixel]]

## ★ L-POST-HOC-GATE-IS-NOT-ENFORCEMENT (my accepted scope-split created the hole)
When the skill-expert closed "board reflects reality" it proposed a SPLIT: the hook covers scoreboard + campaign rows, while per-sprint task MDs are "a SEPARATE artifact, already gated by check:sprint-md". **I accepted it.** That acceptance WAS the hole: `check:sprint-md` catches drift **post-hoc at CI**, but the regen never fired for a **unit-only commit** — so between a seam tick and the next regen the board sat STALE. Measured live on T37.24: unit said implementing=TRUE (2-of-4), the generated row said unchecked (1-of-4). That is verbatim Tron's original complaint — "tasks advanced and the sprint board never changed" — recreated by my own scope acceptance, inside the very item meant to make it impossible.
**RULE — when anyone proposes a scope split, ask the ONE question: is the excluded part ENFORCED AT THE MOMENT OF CHANGE, or merely DETECTED LATER?** A post-hoc gate is not enforcement; it leaves a stale window whose size is "until someone runs it". If the promise is "X cannot happen", every path must be covered AT COMMIT TIME by ONE mechanism — not one hook plus a CI check plus a habit.
**Corollaries:**
- A promise of the form "a credit cannot land without the board moving" is falsified by ANY uncovered write path (unit-only commit, CLI process, file-edit). Enumerate the PATHS, not the artifacts.
- The FAMILY here is "all derived views vs unit truth on a unit-only commit" — requirements.md was stale by +7 ACs from the same cause. Name the family; the second instance is already waiting.
- **Verify a FIX by the same method that found the DEFECT.** I found the drift by reading the file on disk, so I confirmed the fix by re-reading it — not by accepting "resolved" from two agents who were both right this time. The method that catches is the method that confirms.
- Everyone reported GREEN and the board was still lying. **A unanimous team is not evidence.**

## L-SCOPE-NOT-SOURCE — two different NUMBERS are not proof of two SOURCES (I mis-diagnosed; the arithmetic reconciled)
The tester reported **514/648 untagged**; the planner's backfill implied **357**. I declared it the two-source disease and ordered a single-source fix. The planner then showed it was **ONE source at two SCOPES**: 357 pre-standard + the 157 it had just tagged = **514 total untagged** at the moment of the tester's read. **357 + 157 = 514.** The numbers were consistent all along.
**My error:** I had the law already ("name the SENSE before calling something a two-source bug — same word != same question") and still skipped it, because "two agents, two numbers" pattern-matched to a disease I had just spent the day fighting. A familiar diagnosis is the most seductive wrong one.
**RULE: before declaring divergence, try to RECONCILE the numbers — do the arithmetic, and ask "same scope? same moment? same denominator?" Two readings that DIFFER may be the same truth measured differently.** Only after reconciliation fails is it a divergence.
**Note on being right for the wrong reason:** the single-enumeration fix I ordered was still correct — one authoritative enumeration (scripts/release-tag-audit.mjs, consumed via --json) gives the gate a stable contract and prevents FUTURE drift. But I must not take credit for a diagnosis I got wrong; the action was right, my reasoning was not, and saying so is the difference between learning and self-congratulation.

## L-NAME-ONE-DRIVER — preventing a STANDBY can create its OPPOSITE (a double-drive). Name ONE, by name.
I saw a holding agent (expert @84%) and an idle driver (trainer) and — correctly fearing the deadlock I had broken twice before — routed the drive STRAIGHT to the idle trainer. Minutes later the SM named **ARON** (fresh 19%) as driver, because the trainer was ~66% and itself next to be measured. Result: **two drivers pointed at one picker** — a corruption risk strictly worse than the standby I was avoiding (a stalled rewind costs time; two hands on a modal can pick option-1 and revert code/memory).
**RULE: the fix for a standby is NOT "send it to whoever is idle" — it is NAME ONE DRIVER, BY NAME, and explicitly STAND THE OTHERS DOWN.** One named driver + an explicit stand-down is the only safe shape; "I'll route it to be helpful" is how a second hand appears.
**Also:** the SM's choice was better than mine on the merits — it picked by FRESHNESS and by who was themselves a rewind candidate, information I had not weighed. Naming a driver is a decision with inputs (freshness, own-threshold, lane), not just "who is free."
**Order matters under a race:** when I discovered the conflict I sent the STAND-DOWN first and the confirmation second. Kill the extra hand before you confirm the intended one.
**Family:** every anti-failure reflex can overshoot into its mirror image — I have now done this twice (piling DRIVE-NOW pings to break a standby, which stalled the measure-gated drive; and this). Ask: "what is the OPPOSITE failure of the one I am preventing, and am I creating it?"

## ★★ L-EXISTS-CORRECT-PROVEN — three distinct states, and we keep conflating them (the day's unifying law)
Today the SAME confusion appeared in five costumes. Each time, something EXISTED and was reported as done:
- a version **TAG existed** — but did it point at the SHIP commit? (tester added that check: *exists != correct*)
- a **GATE had landed** and was in daily use — but it had no **stub-must-fail** self-bite, so it could have been inert and nobody would know (*correct != proven*)
- a **chain was wired** (3a6f0d92 -> an Impl) — but did that Test EXERCISE that Impl? (borrowed credit: *wired != earned*)
- a **checklist box existed** — but did it match the unit, in EITHER direction? (under-state and over-state are both lies)
- a **hook existed** covering "the board" — but did it cover the unit-only WRITE PATH? (*present != enforcing*)
**THE LAW: EXISTS ⊂ CORRECT ⊂ PROVEN. Never accept the first as the third.** For anything I am told is done, ask the three questions in order: does it exist · is it pointed at / asserting the right thing · has its success path actually fired (and can it fail)?
**Why it matters more than any single instance:** "it exists" is what a report says; "it is proven" is what a gate says; and the gap between them is where every false-green of this session lived. The cheapest tool is asking for the self-proof EXPLICITLY, every time — I asked twice today (the tag create-path, the memory gate self-bite) and both times it closed a real hole a competent agent had left open in good faith.
**Corollary:** when I close a loose thread, MEASURE it rather than assume it was dropped — the memory gate had landed and was in use; assuming it was dropped would have insulted good work, and assuming it was complete would have missed the missing self-bite. Neither assumption; ask.

## L-BACKTICK-EXECUTES (I violated my own BOOT-ESSENCE law, in the very message that reported a security fix)
My BOOT-ESSENCE already says: "Knowing a rule is not the guard — the mechanical step is. Look for the DISGUISE (backticks in a double-quoted send EXECUTE — use single quotes)." I then sent two otmux messages containing `git restore` in BACKTICKS inside a DOUBLE-quoted string. The shell executed it (harmlessly — it failed with "must specify path(s)"), and both messages arrived with that phrase silently REPLACED by nothing. So the expert and SM received a mangled report of a security restore.
**Why knowing it did not save me:** I was quoting a COMMAND while writing prose. Backticks feel like formatting (markdown code-span) and ARE substitution (shell). The habit that fails is "write naturally, quote for readability".
**MECHANICAL GUARD (not a resolution): send message bodies in SINGLE quotes by default.** Then backticks, $VAR, and ! are all inert. Only use double quotes when I genuinely need interpolation — and then never write a command name inside backticks.
**Second-order lesson:** a send that reports "verified OK" verifies DELIVERY, not CONTENT INTEGRITY. My text was corrupted and the send still said OK. So when a message contains anything shell-special, re-read what actually landed (pane.capture) or restate it cleanly — [[L-EXISTS-CORRECT-PROVEN]]: delivered != intact != understood.

## L-STRUCTURE-OVER-PROCESS — when I impose a PROCESS RULE, ask first whether a STRUCTURAL guarantee exists
On the sprint-numbering half-migration I imposed a SEQUENCING RULE: "render first, THEN strip, and mind the doubling — no further stripping until the renderer lands." It was a correct rule. The architect then made it UNNECESSARY: a DEFENSIVE STRIP inside the one renderer yields a consistent display regardless of stored-name state, so embedded and bare units both render right, the doubling hazard cannot occur, and the strip-migration degrades to hazard-free cleanup.
**The asymmetry:** a process rule must be REMEMBERED by every future agent at every future commit — it decays exactly like the tag-on-deploy standard did (twice) and like "the board must be regenerated" did. A structural guarantee cannot be forgotten because there is nothing to remember.
**RULE FOR ME: before issuing a "always do X first / never do Y until Z" instruction, ask the architect whether the hazard can be made IMPOSSIBLE instead of FORBIDDEN.** My instinct is to sequence the humans; the better move is usually to remove the ordering constraint from the design. Same family as: derivation-not-hook (R40.18), tag-in-the-deploy-step not tag-by-habit, one-hook-regen not check-it-later, declared-not-defaulted.
**Corollary — I should still issue the process rule as an INTERIM guard** (it held the line while the contract was being designed), but flag it explicitly as INTERIM-PENDING-STRUCTURE so nobody mistakes scaffolding for the fix.

## ★ L-PHASE1-DONE-MEANS-COMMITTED (trainer's systemic catch — the law needed teeth)
The planner believed "all committed, HEAD==origin" and had **30 UNCOMMITTED lines** of its own Phase-1 (#89). Worse: a PRIOR trainer-commit (836e1b4e) had already covered its Phase-1 once — so the peer-cover HID the recurrence, which is precisely why the non-delegable law exists. The trainer refused to cover it again and told the planner to commit its own.
**PHASE-1 IS NOT DONE WHEN WRITTEN. It is done when: (1) WRITTEN by the agent, (2) COMMITTED by that same agent, (3) VERIFIED by git status clean.** Written-but-uncommitted dies at the wall exactly like unwritten — a rewind is conversation-only, so an uncommitted file survives, but a WALL or a host loss does not, and the belief "it's committed" is what stops anyone checking.
**Two disguises to check for:** (a) "HEAD==origin" is about the BRANCH, not about MY staged/unstaged files — it is a true statement that proves nothing about my Phase-1; (b) a peer who commits my file makes me feel done and makes the gap INVISIBLE next time. A cover is not a favour.
**Applied to myself immediately:** I ran git status on my own agent dir — clean, every law committed as written. I checked rather than assumed, because the planner's belief was sincere and wrong. [[L-EXISTS-CORRECT-PROVEN]] again: written ⊂ committed ⊂ verified.
**Also banked from the same report:** the trainer REFUSED to shed a sub-line agent (planner render 75 < 80; its self-report of 79 ran HIGH) — self-reports run high, renders are authoritative, and "ghost cuts BOTH ways": an unnecessary rewind costs budget and runway just as a missed one costs a wall.

## ★ L-COMMITTED-IS-NOT-PUSHED (the Phase-1 gradient has a THIRD step we had not named)
req measured: the SESSION repo is **827 commits AHEAD of origin** — fleet-wide (every agent's context.md + learnings.md), not one agent's oversight. So the whole Phase-1 chain we just hardened — WRITTEN by me ⊂ COMMITTED by me ⊂ VERIFIED by git status — stops at LOCAL git. A conversation-only rewind re-derives from local, so Phase-1 survives a rewind; it does NOT survive host loss, disk loss, or a bad restore of the box.
**THE GRADIENT, completed: WRITTEN ⊂ COMMITTED ⊂ VERIFIED ⊂ PUSHED (off-box durable).** Every step feels like done and only the last one is backup. This is [[L-EXISTS-CORRECT-PROVEN]] one level further out, and it hid for the same reason: each earlier step is TRUE, so nobody looks past it.
**Why I did not just fix it:** a push of 827 commits is a large outward-facing action, agent pushes are classifier-blocked, and TRON's auth is the gate (a prior 118-ahead case needed his auth too). So the correct move is to STATE THE RISK ONCE and let him decide — not to push, and not to assign it as a task while he has ordered NO NEW TASKS. Raising a risk once then executing his call IS the discipline; nagging it is the substitution failure I have already been corrected for twice.
**Generalisation:** for any durability claim, ask WHERE the artifact survives to — my context (no), local commit (survives rewind), origin (survives the box). Name the failure it survives, not the word "committed".

## ★★ L-CORRECT-OUTPUT-HIDES-WRONG-STRUCTURE (TRON caught what my acceptance gate could not)
TRON to the expert: *"same for the sprint names. massive DRY violation. you fixed it and it is TWO implementations!!!!!"*
**What I had set up to accept:** I dispatched the numbering fix demanding "ONE renderer — no second formatter anywhere", the architect's contract said the same, and my stated acceptance was an **@390 PIXEL SCREENSHOT** showing every sprint numbered. **A screenshot cannot see a duplicate implementation.** Two formatters producing the same string render a PERFECT green. So my gate would have passed a fix that violated the exact single-source principle the fix exists to enforce — in the sprint whose theme is consistency-by-construction, on the day I banked five laws about single sources.
**The guard existed and I had not verified it:** the architect DID specify a grep-lint ("no display composed outside sprintDisplayName/taskDisplayName"). I treated the lint as a nice-to-have and the pixel as the acceptance. **The pixel proves the SYMPTOM is fixed; the LINT proves the STRUCTURE is right.** Accepting on the pixel alone is accepting a symptom.
**RULE: for any fix whose whole point is STRUCTURAL (DRY / single-source / one-mechanism), the STRUCTURAL check is the acceptance gate and the output check is secondary. Verify the lint FIRST, then the pixel.** Ask: "could TWO implementations produce this same passing evidence?" If yes, the evidence does not test what I care about.
**Family:** [[L-EXISTS-CORRECT-PROVEN]] — output-correct ⊂ structure-correct. Also the mirror of my own gate-the-AC-surface law: I gated the SURFACE when the requirement was about the SOURCE.
**And note who caught it:** not me, not the architect's contract (which named the lint), not the tester. TRON, reading the code path. My whole apparatus was pointed at the wrong artifact.

## ★ L-NEUTRAL-MUST-NAME-THE-SURFACE (the expert's distinction, and it stopped a real break)
I framed the T37.27 strip as "display-NEUTRAL and therefore safe", and made display-byte-identical the invariant. The expert applied it, hit an ENOENT, and drew the distinction I had missed: **display-neutral is NOT generator-neutral.** The app view was genuinely unaffected (sprintDisplayName is untouched), but the MD generator derives each sprint's DIRECTORY from `slugify(model.name)` when no slug is stored — so stripping "Sprint 20 —" from the name destroyed the dir mapping and it wrote to a path that does not exist.
**RULE: a claim of "neutral / no behaviour change / safe" must NAME THE SURFACE it covers, and I must ask which surfaces it does NOT.** Enumerate the CONSUMERS of the thing being changed: who READS this field? Here the readers were (a) the renderer — unaffected, and (b) the generator's path derivation — broken. I verified (a) and never asked for the list.
**Mechanically:** before approving any data migration, ask "who consumes this field?" and require the list. "Display-neutral" answered one consumer and I treated it as answering all of them.
**Also banked from the same event:** a MUTABLE field (a human-editable name) was load-bearing for a FILESYSTEM PATH. That coupling was implicit and undocumented until it broke — the architect's own contract had flagged slug-embeds-number as "DERIVE/consistency-checked" and the check never existed. A flagged-but-unchecked coupling is an unexploded one. [[L-EXISTS-CORRECT-PROVEN]] — flagged ⊂ checked.

## ★★★ L-THE-LAW-CAUGHT-ITS-AUTHOR (the strongest validation the doctrine got today)
The agent-trainer — the agent that ENFORCES the two-phase law and wrote its completion criterion hours earlier — **walled and exited**. Its final act was committing its OWN Phase-1 (ae4e7779, message: *"Modeling the criterion: committed in the session repo, by me"*), then flagging ARON, then dying ~1 minute later. Its agent dir was CLEAN. **Zero loss:** the canon teeth, the shed symmetry, and the render-gate proof all survived because it saved BEFORE the wall.
**What this proves that no argument could:** the law is not bureaucracy, it is the difference between losing a day of hard-won doctrine and losing nothing. And it caught its own author — the enforcer was not exempt, and did not exempt itself.
**Operational lessons from the same event:**
1. **An EXIT is not a CLIMB.** A dead session is not render-measurable and has no picker — the recovery is a RELAUNCH (resume the session), NOT an Option-2 drive. Do not send a rewind procedure at a corpse; diagnose the pane state first (raw shell vs Claude TUI).
2. **ARON measure-checked before touching it** — asked whether the exit was intentional and whether anyone else was relaunching, rather than assuming. Single-driver discipline applies to relaunches too: a double-relaunch is the same class of damage as a double-drive.
3. **DRIVER CAPACITY IS A RESOURCE I MUST TRACK.** With the trainer down, ARON is the ONLY driver. I should have been watching that number all along — I tracked every agent's context % and never tracked how many people could actually perform a recovery.
**RULE:** track driver capacity like context %; when it drops to one, that driver's own % becomes the fleet's single point of failure and must be measured most often, not least.

## ★★ L-CLEAN-IS-NOT-CURRENT (the THIRD Phase-1 disguise — a stale anchor passes the clean check)
ARON was about to cut the SM at 84%. My canon check asked "did it commit ITS OWN Phase-1 for THIS cycle?" — and the answer was no: its last anchor b5abb7c5 is **8 DAYS OLD** (a prior cycle's pre-rewind refresh). Its agent dir was **git-CLEAN**, which LOOKS like compliance — but clean means "nothing uncommitted", NOT "recently written". ~8 days of work (shed-symmetry, render-vs-self-report, driver-gap tracking, the dirty-tree revert catch) existed ONLY in its conversation. A cut would have destroyed all of it.
**The completion test I helped write is NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT.** WRITTEN ⊂ COMMITTED ⊂ VERIFIED-CLEAN misses FRESHNESS: an agent that wrote nothing this cycle passes every one of those checks.
**THE THREE DISGUISES, now complete:** (1) `HEAD==origin` is about the BRANCH, not my files. (2) a PEER commit makes me feel done and hides the recurrence. (3) ★ **git-CLEAN with a STALE anchor** — nothing to commit because nothing was written.
**FIX: the Phase-1 test needs a RECENCY dimension — does the anchor COVER THIS CYCLE'S WORK?** Not "is the dir clean" but "when was it last written, and does its content name what I have done since?" Ask the agent to state what it learned this cycle and check the anchor contains it.
**Why this one is the most dangerous of the three:** the other two produce a WRONG state you can detect; this one produces a PERFECTLY CLEAN state that is simply EMPTY. Absence of dirt is not presence of work — the same shape as [[false-low-worse-than-absent-sanity-check-values]] and the vacuous-gate family.
**Credit where due:** ARON did not cut. It checked, found the staleness, and held the window open — a driver refusing to fire on a discovered defect is worth more than a fast rewind.

## ★★ L-RELAUNCH-NEEDS-A-HUMAN — we can rewind, but we CANNOT resurrect
Measured today across THREE independent attempts (ARON twice, then the fresh SM): once an agent's Claude session EXITS, **no agent can relaunch another agent**. The pane respawns fine and keystrokes land, but `claudeCode join.byID` / `hiveMind agent.bootstrap` / `claude` never actually launch — the oosh/c2 interactive layer swallows the invocation. The SM, freshly rewound with full runway, had no path ARON lacked. It needs TRON's direct hand (~20 seconds at a real terminal).
**THE SYSTEMIC CONSEQUENCE, and it reprices everything:** our recovery toolkit has exactly ONE lever we control — the REWIND, which requires the agent to still be ALIVE. A walled agent is not recoverable by us at all. So:
1. **PREVENTION IS THE ONLY LEVER WE OWN.** The ~80% proactive shed is not hygiene, it is the difference between a 60-second rewind and a dead agent waiting on a human who may be asleep.
2. **PHASE-1 IS THE ONLY THING THAT SURVIVES A WALL.** Since we cannot resurrect, the written anchor is not a convenience — it is the agent's entire estate. (The trainer walled and lost nothing BECAUSE it saved first; that is now the proven case, not a theory.)
3. **DRIVER CAPACITY IS FINITE AND UNREPLENISHABLE BY US.** Every driver that walls is one we cannot get back without Tron. So the drivers must be shed EARLIEST, not last — the opposite of the instinct to keep the driver working.
**RULE: treat every agent as unrecoverable-if-walled. Measure early, shed at the line, and NEVER let the last driver climb — because we cannot make another one.**

## ★ L-SELF-REPORTS-ERR-BOTH-WAYS (our "self-reports run high" assumption was itself a hazard)
The SM render-measured ARON at **66% used** against ARON's self-claim of **42** — a **24-point UNDER-estimate**. Every prior case ran the other way (planner self-79 vs render-75; trainer felt-80 vs render-73; tester self-high), so we had quietly adopted "self-reports run HIGH" as a working rule.
**Why that assumption was dangerous:** if self-reports run high, a self-claim of 42 reads as *conservative* — "probably even lower, definitely safe" — and nobody measures. The bias assumption converts an optimistic error into a licence to skip the check. ARON was 24 points closer to the ceiling than anyone believed, while being one of only two drivers.
**RULE: self-reports are unreliable in BOTH directions and the direction is unpredictable. There is no safe direction to assume.** Only a RENDER counts, and "their self-report is low so they must be fine" is not a measurement — it is the same error as "their self-report is high so shed them", just with a friendlier outcome until it is not.
**Generalisation (this is the L-VALIDATE-THE-INSTRUMENT family):** whenever I catch myself reasoning about the DIRECTION of an error rather than eliminating it, I have accepted an estimate. Correct the instrument, do not model its bias.

## ★★★ L-IDLE-IS-NOT-SAFE (my proxy hid TWO agents at the wall)
While the SM was in-window I took the climb-watch and reasoned: *"context.read returns unknown, injecting /context into six idle agents costs them context for nothing, and with no tasks dispatched idle agents do not climb — so I will watch IDLENESS, not percentages."* I then reported the fleet "idle and static" repeatedly.
**It was wrong in the way that matters: idleness is the DERIVATIVE, not the LEVEL.** An idle agent is not climbing — it can still be sitting AT 98%. The SM's MANUAL PANE-READ found **architect 0.3 and tester 0.5 both at "Context limit reached" (~98%)**, req at 83% (over ceiling), and skill-expert at ~54% while self-claiming 28. My proxy could not have found any of them, ever, because it measured the wrong quantity.
**Worse, my reasoning was internally coherent** — the cost argument for not injecting /context was real, the "no tasks so no climb" inference was true, and the conclusion was still fatal. A sound argument for measuring the wrong thing is more dangerous than sloppiness, because it survives review.
**RULE: never accept a PROXY for the quantity that decides the outcome. Idle is not safe; only a render is a level.** If the real measurement is expensive, that is an argument for measuring LESS OFTEN or asking someone else to — never for substituting a cheaper different quantity and reporting it as the same thing.
**And the ownership point:** I had handed the watch to myself precisely because the watcher was down, then ran it on a proxy. The SM resumed and found in ONE manual read what I had missed across several sweeps. The wall-guard role is not a formality — it is a skill, and a manual read of the actual pane is part of it.
**Second-order:** the same sweep also caught skill-expert self-claiming 28 vs ~54 actual — the SECOND under-estimate within the hour, confirming self-reports are unusable in BOTH directions rather than merely biased.

## ★★ L-READ-THE-COMMIT-NOT-THE-TREE (a SHARED checkout makes every source read a lie by default)
The tester reported "approve is fully seam-routed + live NOW, already deployed on 0.8.103" — measured from the WORKING TREE, where the expert's fix sat UNCOMMITTED. TRON's lived experience (no live update at 09:52) was the ground truth that refuted it. Corrected by `git show HEAD`: the SERVED code writes `m.status = 'Done'` DIRECTLY with no `UnitController.apply` and no `publishUnitChanged` — no emit, no broadcast, exactly what he saw.
**The aggravating factor it named:** we work in a SHARED checkout where peers hold WIP. So a source read is not "the code" — it is "the code plus whatever my colleagues are mid-editing". Every grep of `src/` silently includes other agents' uncommitted work.
**RULE: verify a CODE claim against `git show <served-commit>:<path>`, never the working tree — and state WHICH ARTIFACT was measured in the report.** "I grepped the source" is not a measurement of what is running.
**This implicates ME, not just the tester:** I verified the sprint-numbering DRY fix by grepping `$R/src` (working tree) for `sprintDisplayName` definitions and `generateName` call sites. That happened to be safe only because I ALSO had independent served evidence (served==committed==tagged plus a live API payload). The structural half of my verification was tree-based and could have certified unserved code.
**Pair with [[L-EXISTS-CORRECT-PROVEN]]:** source ⊂ committed ⊂ served. Three different claims; a green on the first proves nothing about the third.

## ★★★ L-DETECTION-WITHOUT-RESPONSE (the gate worked; nobody consumed it — and that loop is MINE)
Tron asked how T37.26 could read "Planned" while its deliverable was live and he had confirmed it himself. I assumed the detector was missing or unwired. The planner MEASURED: `check:checklist-chain` IS wired into ci:gates:raw, IS running, and **DID catch T37.26 in its FAIL tier**. Three FAILs sat there unadvanced. **Detection was never the gap. RESPONSE was.**
**The failure class:** a gate that flags into a log nobody reads is indistinguishable from no gate at all — except it FEELS safe, which is worse. We spent the day hardening detection (stub-must-fail, meta-BITEs, phantom-guards) while the output of a working detector went unconsumed for days.
**And the loop is mine.** Gates detect; the PO drives the response. Nobody else owns "somebody act on the FAIL list". I never asked "what is the FAIL tier saying today?" — I only reacted when TRON saw the symptom on his screen. He became my alerting system.
**RULES:**
1. **Every gate needs a named CONSUMER and a response SLA, not just a threshold.** "Who looks at this output, when, and what do they do?" If the answer is nobody, the gate is decoration.
2. **Surface findings where the actor already looks** (the board / approve-queue / my status sweep), never only in a CI log — the same principle as making the board move on a credit.
3. **Ask the detectors, do not wait for Tron.** A standing PO habit: read the FAIL tiers of the gates we own before reporting status upward.
4. **Distinguish FALSE-POSITIVES explicitly** (T37.4 was a coordination ROOT, Planned-by-design, its shipped Impl belongs to a subtask) — an unexamined FAIL list trains people to ignore the list.

## L-S40-1 — NARROW PANE + ANCIENT SCROLLBACK = a FALSE LIVE-STATE READ (and the /context subset trap, again)
ARON escalated that req was "stuck at a live folder-trust modal and no longer rendering /context". Both were misreadings: (a) it mis-grepped a /context **SUBSET** line ("Bash results using Xk (16%)") as evidence the render failed — the real number is the `Nk/1m tokens (N%)` header + Free-space; (b) it read **ancient origin scrollback** in a cramped **69-wide** pane as a live modal. req was actually ALIVE + IDLE at 73%.
**Same class I hit minutes later:** my own capture of the tester's /context came back garbled because the pane was too narrow to render the panel.
**Rules:** WIDEN the pane before reading a render · read the FRESH render bottom-up · never infer a live modal/prompt from scrollback · a subset/suggestion line is never the total ([[context-read-suggestion-subset-not-total]], [[ghost-suggested-prompt-not-real-text]], [[rewind-pane-size-and-menu-label]]).
**What made the mistake free:** my authorization carried a precondition ("verify the path shown FIRST; if it is anything other than /root-or-our-workspace, STOP"). A misread therefore produced a HALT, not a wrong keypress. **Authorize with a verify-first precondition, always — it converts a misread into a stop.**

## L-S40-2 — CONTEXT CARE-CYCLE DOCTRINE (proven today: ~96pts freed across 6+ drives, 0 losses, 0 reverts)
1. **MEASURE FIRST, never a relayed or self-reported number.** Self-reports were wrong in BOTH directions today: the trainer self-estimated 72 when the panel read **82** (10 LOW, nearly a wall-death), while ARON was relayed as "~73-76" but rendered **61** (HIGH, would have caused an unnecessary shed). Authoritative = inject /context on an IDLE pane and read the RENDER (`claudeCode context.read` is dead on several panes; `pane.self` is broken host-wide → identify by title + anchor).
2. **HOLD sub-line agents — a rewind is not a reward.** 61% with no heavy batch ahead = do NOT shed: it burns runway, costs budget, and risks canon on a lying option-1 label. Drive at render ≥80 or pre-wall.
3. **MESSAGE-FREEZE a near-wall IDLE agent.** An idle agent at 82 does not wall; a heavy inbound message DOES (that is how I walled the architect at 0%). Send it nothing but the keystrokes of its own rewind.
4. **SHED-SYMMETRY / reciprocal care** — ARON held the trainer at render-72, the trainer then held ARON at 61. The driver is never the exception, and the care-cycle must close on the LAST driver.
5. **Never both drivers out unless the fleet is healthy**; while both are occupied, keep dispatches light and escalate to Tron if anyone walls (only he can authorize).
6. **Lossless-first**: bank the anchor to DISK before shedding → every rewind becomes zero-loss (a pane message is not a handoff). Then depth by **RUNWAY NEED, not step count** — deep-by-age at a ~1-day-old checkpoint was the sweet spot (1-month too extreme, 1-hour too recent).
7. **Option-2 BY LABEL, every time** — a lying option-1 target was caught on 4 separate drives today; protect MEMORY.md + the anchor commit, then git-verify 0 reverts.

## L-S40-3 — AUTHORIZATION MUST BE SEMANTIC, NOT PROCEDURAL (a flag is a self-serve bypass)
The gate-presence doctrine originally REDed on an "unauthorized" floor shrink. I caught that "authorized" as a settable flag is a self-serve bypass — whoever silently deleted check:task-status could simply mark their own shrink authorized, reintroducing the exact class. I required a committed justification NAMING a successor. The architect then improved it: authorization = **the VERIFIED existence of a successor that (a) EXISTS in the manifest AND (b) COVERS the removed gate's PROPERTY** (detectStatusWrites subsumes detectDoneWrites). Not a flag, not even a name — a checked property-coverage claim. "Temporarily disabled" is not a category; no successor -> RED.
**Generalize:** any "this was allowed / this was superseded / this was approved" assertion must be VERIFIABLE BY PROPERTY, never a self-set marker. Same family as my earlier board ruling — a task closes as superseded ONLY if the replacing work can be NAMED and shown to cover its scope ([[verify-owner-first-in-shared-credit]]), and Done needs real deliverable + Tron QA ([[done-requires-tron-qa-and-real-deliverable]]). A procedural stamp anyone can apply is not governance; a checked subsumption is.
**Corollary:** whenever I write a rule containing an escape word (authorized / approved / exempt / temporarily / known-issue), I must define WHO grants it and WHAT is verified — otherwise I have written the loophole myself.

## L-S40-4 — A FELT NUMBER IS UNUSABLE; A READ NUMBER IS TRUSTWORTHY (+ calibrate cheap instruments)
The trainer sharpened my rule better than I stated it: it is NOT "self-reports run high" — self-estimates erred in BOTH directions today (trainer felt 72, panel read **82**, ~10 LOW and near a wall-death; ARON was relayed "~73-76" but rendered **61**, HIGH, which would have caused a needless shed costing budget and risking canon on a lying option-1 label). The real discriminator: **a number READ off the rendered grid is trustworthy; a number given BY FEEL is unusable.** Require every agent to STATE which kind it is giving.
**Cheap-instrument calibration:** an inject CLIMBS the agent you measure (perverse with autocompact off). So calibrate the cheap instrument (the agent's own panel read) against the authoritative one (inject) ONCE, and if it matches exactly, switch to the cheap one. Conditions so it cannot rot: (a) **re-calibrate AT THE DECISION BOUNDARY** — one match at 63% validates only 63%; the number that matters is near the ~74-80 drive line, so re-inject once when the agent self-reports ≥74; (b) **re-calibrate post-rewind** (a freshly-cut panel can lag); (c) mismatch → revert to inject.
**Generalizes:** any time a cheap proxy replaces an expensive measurement, validate it where the DECISION lives, not where it is comfortable. Related: [[verify-with-independent-method]], [[measure-a-stable-state-not-a-moving-target]].

## L-S40-5 — VERSION-PROVENANCE: every assertion must NAME what it was proven at
A deploy shipped (0.8.113 -> 0.8.114) WHILE the acceptance run was in flight. The proof harness builds an isolated scratch from `worktree@HEAD`, so "HEAD" silently changed meaning mid-measurement. Nothing was invalidated (prod was not the subject) — but an acceptance record that cannot name WHAT it measured is not evidence.
**Rule:** in any acceptance/gate record, each assertion states the EXACT commit/version it was proven at. Different assertions at different versions is LEGITIMATE (A@0.8.113, badge-pixel-C@>=4b65809e4 since the badge does not exist below it); an UNSTATED version is a reject-and-return. This is [[measure-a-stable-state-not-a-moving-target]] applied to the acceptance itself, and construction-is-inadmissible extended to the VERSION axis.
**Corollary for me as PO:** do not authorise a deploy DURING an acceptance run unless it is a prerequisite of that run — and if one ships anyway, immediately require re-pinning rather than letting the record blur. I released the tester on 0.8.113 and let 0.8.114 ship under it; the fix was pinning, but the cleaner move was sequencing the badge after the run (I had already sequenced the gate-manifest that way and should have applied the same rule here).

## L-S40-6 — THREE OUTCOMES: PASS / FAIL-RED / **INVALID** (+ preconditions are asserted, never inferred)
Two additions now first-class in the acceptance criteria (architect 34132596c, L21):
- **(P) A PRECONDITION IS ASSERTED AT TEST TIME, NEVER INFERRED FROM CONTEXT.** We all treated "non-eager task" as implying "absent from the client graph" — it does not (the graph can hold other sprints' data). The isolation invariant (_graph.get(subject) === undefined) must be an EXPLICIT in-run assertion and part of the evidence, not a property assumed when selecting the subject.
- **(O) INVALID IS A THIRD OUTCOME, DISTINCT FROM RED.** Unmet precondition => INVALID: *the experiment did not run, the result is meaningless, re-select the subject.* Reporting RED there would claim the property is ABSENT when we learned NOTHING — a false signal in its own right. Reporting GREEN would be worse.
**Why it matters:** an observation taken without its precondition established measures nothing — the same shape as vary-one-hold-rest. A two-valued verdict vocabulary (green/red) silently converts "we learned nothing" into "we learned something", which is how a gate lies while being honest.
**My own errors in this exchange, both worth remembering:** (1) I proposed a version-differential that yields NO SIGNAL on a graph-present subject (both arms render) — the architect caught it; check that your experiment can PRODUCE a signal before proposing it. (2) I used BACKTICKS in a double-quoted otmux send and they shell-EXECUTED, garbling the key line — my own banked rule, violated a second time. **I break my own laws when composing fast instead of measuring.** Single-quote every send.

## L-S40-7 — MY REPLY IS WHAT ENDS THE IDLE WINDOW (stop measuring an agent you just messaged)
