The tester reported "genuinely idle, holding for my number". I sent it a sharpening + hold instruction, then injected /context to measure it — and found it GENERATING. Obvious in hindsight: **my own message is what made it busy.** An agent's "I am idle" is true at send time and falsified by the very next thing I send it. I have now hit the measure-a-generating-agent trap THREE times in one session (twice on a narrow pane, once self-inflicted like this).
**Rules:** (1) if I want a measurement, send NOTHING and let the designated measurer take the window — or measure FIRST and send after; (2) never chain "instruction + measurement" in the same beat; (3) an idle claim is a PERISHABLE fact, valid only until someone writes to that pane; (4) delegate the measure to the fleet measurer and stop re-attempting — my repeated attempts each cost the agent context and push the window further away.
**Deeper pattern to watch in myself:** I break my own documented laws when I am composing fast and driving hard (backticks-execute twice, measure-while-generating three times, letting a non-prerequisite deploy ship mid-acceptance). The failure mode is not ignorance — it is speed. When I notice urgency, that is precisely the moment to re-read the rule rather than act on memory.

## L-S40-8 — CONTEXT IS NOT THE ONLY DEPLETION AXIS: track ERROR-RATE and rotate on it
ARON declined a 5th consecutive drive at 62% context — not because it lacked runway, but because its ERROR RATE had demonstrably risen across 4 consecutive drives (2 picker-chaining slips + 1 misdiagnosis). Its line: **"catching is luck-adjacent"** — the slips were caught, but that is not evidence the next one would be. And the target (the tester) held Tron's acceptance proof = exactly the agent not to risk a degraded operator on. Its rule: **rotation > availability when the target can safely WAIT.**
**Adopt as fleet doctrine:** measure TWO axes, not one — (1) context %, (2) consecutive high-stakes operations + observed slips. A driver at healthy context with a rising error-rate should HAND OFF, and a lossless target that can wait should wait for a FRESHER operator rather than take a degraded one now.
**★ Applies to ME, and explains my day:** my error cluster (backticks shell-executing 2x · measuring a GENERATING agent 3x · manufacturing urgency from a number I had ruled inadmissible · letting a non-prerequisite deploy ship mid-acceptance) all happened at 30-45% context = NOT context-driven. It was VOLUME and SPEED. I had banked "urgency is my tell"; ARON's framing is sharper — sustained consecutive high-stakes operations degrade accuracy independently of runway. **Watch my own slip-rate, not just my percentage**, and slow down or hand off when slips cluster, even at 40%.

## L-S40-9 — BATCHED ARROW NAVIGATION CAN AUTO-FIRE THE PICKER (new hazard) + report the PATH, not just the outcome
The trainer drove the expert 81%->47% code-intact, zero net loss — but reported that the PATH was a slip: an **Up15/Down2 batched navigation TRIPPED an auto-fire of option-1** (scroll-wheel-behaves-as-arrows hazard, and it happened even via send.tui at a <=20 batch). The auto-fired option-1 did free the agent but **reverted context.md -14**; it detected via `git status`, restored to HEAD, and cleared the huge restored draft (double-Escape).
**Rules:** (1) NEVER batch large arrow movements in a picker — move in SMALL steps and CAPTURE BETWEEN, because a batch can auto-fire the highlighted option; (2) after ANY drive, `git status` + restore is mandatory verification, not optional — that is what turned this slip into zero loss; (3) this hazard MUST go into the drive runbook (it is exactly the class the runbook exists to prevent).
**★ The meta-lesson is the reporting standard:** the outcome was GOOD (freed, code-intact, 0 net loss) and a truthful report could have stopped there. Reporting the SLIP anyway is what makes a slip-rate measurable at all — and slip-rate is now a gating axis. **An agent that only reports outcomes makes its own error-rate invisible, which silently breaks the two-axis gate.** Reward path-honesty explicitly or it stops being offered.

## L-S40-10 ★ TRON'S RULE: ALWAYS COMMIT — then a rewind code-reset is a NON-ISSUE
Tron: *"always commit… then even a rewind code reset is no issue."* This is a BY-CONSTRUCTION collapse of a whole risk class we had been managing by DETECTION.
**Why it works:** option-1 ("restore code and conversation") modifies the **WORKING TREE, not git history**. Today's evidence: every option-1 artifact appeared as *tracked-dirty* (+2090/-1352 across 22 files; a v0.8.115->0.8.110 revert that deleted a shipped CI gate) and every recovery was a `git restore` / `git stash`. **Committed work was never at risk.** So with a clean committed tree, the worst case of the scariest failure mode is a restorable dirty tree.
**RULE (fleet):** (1) **COMMIT CONTINUOUSLY**, and ALWAYS before a drive — every tree the agent works in, not just its own context file; (2) **PRE-DRIVE INVARIANT for drivers: verify the target's trees are COMMITTED before opening the picker** — if dirty, commit (or stash) FIRST, then drive; (3) post-drive `git status` across all those trees stays mandatory; recovery is `restore`/`stash`, never a committed revert.
**What this replaces:** we had built elaborate DETECTION (by-label selection, lying-label catching, stale-capture prevention, absolute freezes). Keep them — they are still cheaper than a recovery — but understand they now guard an inconvenience, not a catastrophe. **Making a failure HARMLESS beats making it DETECTABLE** — the same move as the gate-manifest, the shared viewBusKey builder, and the derived-status getter.

## L-S40-11 — SEND-FAILED + BARE-PROMPT CAPTURE = MEASURE `pane.size` (a neighbour widen can flatten a pane to 1 row)
Two consecutive `send.verified FAILED: pane unchanged` on the expert, with captures returning nothing but a bare `❯`. I nearly filed it as S-9 ghosting. **It was GEOMETRY: the pane was `69x1`** — a single row — because ARON had widened the TESTER's pane to `140x52` to read its `/context`, which squeezed its neighbour flat. A 1-row pane cannot render, cannot confirm a send, and cannot show a capture. `otmux pane.size.set <pane> 69 24` restored it and the very next send verified OK first try.
**Diagnostic rule:** when a send fails AND the capture is near-empty, **measure `otmux pane.size <pane>` BEFORE concluding ghost/staging** — a sliver looks exactly like a dead comms channel. Same family as every instrument failure today (the neuter that never patched, the classifier that needed proving, `/context` on a busy pane): **the signal was fine; the instrument couldn't register it.**
**Second-order caution:** WIDENING one pane RESIZES its neighbours. Before widening for a read, note the neighbours' sizes and restore after (`otmux fit` / `tiled`), or you trade a readable pane for an unusable one — and the agent you broke may be the one driving a rewind.

## L-S40-12 ★ CODE-READING CONVERGENCE IS CORRELATED ERROR, NOT INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION
Three of us converged from the code on the SAME wrong answer. I hypothesised an input-form asymmetry (notify passes {type,uuid}, the drawer subscribes with a raw typeless ref); the ARCHITECT confirmed it BY READ and found the exact structure (viewBusKey cannot infer a type from a string; drawer subscribes at :469 BEFORE resolveRefUnit at :478) and pre-registered a falsifiable prediction (**keys differ, fire=0**); the expert's earlier analysis had pointed the same way. The architect explicitly called this "genuine INDEPENDENT convergence, both from code, the REAL kind".
**The measurement REFUTED all of us:** keys MATCH verbatim (`task:<uuid>` on both sides), the callback FIRES (2x, instrument proven), and the view still does not re-render. The real defect is the RE-DERIVE reading STALE CACHED status.
**The lesson:** two agents reading the same code are NOT independent verifiers — they share the same evidence KIND, the same abstractions, and therefore the same blind spots. That is **correlated error wearing the costume of corroboration** — the very thing [[verify-with-independent-method]] exists to prevent, and I let the word "independent" pass because two different agents said it. **Independence is about the KIND of evidence, not the number of readers.** A runtime measurement is a different kind; a second code-read is not.
**How it was caught:** by making the prediction FALSIFIABLE and PRE-REGISTERED, then running the instrument anyway instead of treating a confident convergent diagnosis as settled. Pre-registration is what converted a wrong consensus into a cheap, decisive result rather than a shipped fix that misses again.

## L-S40-13 ★ WE NORMALISED OUR OWN DAMAGE FOR HOURS (a broken tool is a SYMPTOM, not a quirk)
`otmux pane.self` broke "host-wide" and the ENTIRE FLEET adapted: every agent switched to identifying by title+anchor, wrote the workaround into its health checks, and called it a host quirk. **It was a REVERT ARTIFACT of our own option-1 auto-fire** — the same misfire that reverted a context file also reverted **5 `/root/oosh` scripts (-3359 lines), including `otmux`/`pane.self`**. Found only when a freshly-rewound trainer ran a WIDENED all-trees check hours later; recovered to HEAD (stash-preserved). **VERIFIED by me after recovery: `otmux pane.self` returns `%6` again, `/root/oosh` clean.**
**Three compounding failures, all ours:** (1) the post-drive check was scoped to TWO trees when the blast radius covered THREE — the driver's truthful "0 net loss" was a live instance of the exact warning already written at 556b6848; (2) nobody asked WHY a working tool stopped working mid-session — **we designed around the symptom instead of diagnosing it**, for hours, across every agent; (3) the workaround got institutionalised — written into health-check procedures — which made the damage INVISIBLE rather than merely unfixed.
**Rules:** (a) **a tool that breaks mid-session is a SYMPTOM to investigate, not a quirk to route around** — same family as every instrument failure this session (the neuter that never patched, the classifier that needed proving, a 1-row pane read as a comms ghost); (b) ENUMERATE the trees before a drive rather than assuming a count; (c) when a workaround is adopted fleet-wide, that is precisely when to ask what broke — an institutionalised workaround hides its own cause; (d) MEASURE the diff direction before restoring a shared repo (it may be a peer's legitimate work).
**And the meta-point: Tron's STEP-0 (commit before the picker) would have made all of it a non-event.** Making the failure harmless beats detecting it late.

## L-S40-14 — DECIDE-ONCE, REFINED: a new FACT applies NOW; a better ARGUMENT applies to the NEXT instance
I took three positions on one question (pre-check now / fold / pre-check now) and flagged my own flip-flop risk. The architect drew the distinction my "decide once with trade-offs weighed" rule was missing: **revising because a FACT CHANGED is UPDATING ON EVIDENCE — that IS the doctrine. Revising because the case was RE-ARGUED is flip-flopping.**
- Position 2 (fold) was a legitimate revision: the tester corrected a COST INPUT I had wrong (I called the pre-check "cheap ~2-3%"; it is a full heavy run).
- Position 3 (run-now) was also legitimate: a NEW FACT surfaced — the probe works PRE-FIX, because the line and the firing callback already exist, so the diagnostic does NOT require the fix deployed. I had assumed it did.
**Rule:** when tempted to revise, ask *"what CHANGED — the evidence, or the rhetoric?"* Evidence changed ⇒ revise now and say which fact. Only the argument changed ⇒ hold, and apply the insight to the next instance. **And name the revision publicly with its cause** — I told the team this was my last revision on that question and asked them to call it out if it recurred, which is what makes the discipline checkable rather than self-graded.
**Corollary:** the ambiguity in the original rule was itself the hazard — it made me hesitate to update on genuine new evidence for fear of looking indecisive. A rule that punishes legitimate updating is worse than no rule.

## L-S40-15 — SEND THE CONSTRAINT TO THE AGENT IT BINDS (my coordination gap shipped a fix early)
I ruled that the re-derive fix must **NOT ship until the tester's probe reported** — because "resolveRefUnit already re-fetches per callback" was a CODE-READ, and a code-read of that exact file had already been refuted by measurement that same day. **I communicated that hold to the ARCHITECT and never sent it to the EXPERT it constrained.** The expert executed the ruling it held — correctly, on its information — and deployed v0.8.116 before the probe ran.
**Rule:** a constraint is only real for the agent that RECEIVES it. Routing a "do not do X" through a third party is not a control — it is a hope. **Address the binding instruction directly to the actor**, and if it must pass through someone, say explicitly "relay this to <actor> and confirm they have it".
**Mitigating note:** no harm resulted (the fold path was cost-competitive and the fix turned out correct), but the outcome was luck, not design — and the failure mode is silent, because the actor looks disobedient when it was never told.

## L-S40-16 — WHEN THE PROPERTY IS ESTABLISHED, DO NOT DEMAND THE PROXY
The expert offered to extract a formal 5-point health block from a freshly-rewound trainer that had gone straight to productive work. I ruled NO: the check exists to PRODUCE KNOWLEDGE, not a document — and we already had the knowledge by better means (it booted disk-first, re-derived correctly, caught the /root/oosh revert unprompted, measured 51% on its own render, verified clean independently by two peers).
**Rule:** rituals are proxies for properties. When the property is independently established, demanding the ritual is ceremony — and worse, it trains the fleet to produce artefacts instead of evidence. Same family as the CI gate that asserted *uses-the-builder* (proxy) instead of *produces-a-typed-key* (property) and went green on the bug it existed to catch.
**Inverse caution:** never skip the ritual when the property is NOT otherwise established — the ritual is the cheapest path to it by default.

## L-S40-17 — GREP IS NOT READING: a filtered slice can hide the decisive number
I twice grepped the raw evidence file for the poll counts that decided the causality claim, missed them both times, and on that basis wrote to Tron that a sub-condition was **UNSEALED**. The architect then read the same file properly and found `pollAfterApprove=3` (positive arm) vs `0` (control) — the exact data that SEALED it. My greps had surfaced only older sections of the same document.
**Rule:** when a claim is load-bearing, **read the SECTION, not a pattern-match across the file** — a grep answers "does this string appear", not "what does the evidence say". A filtered slice of evidence is an instrument, and like every instrument this session it can fail to register the signal.
**And report the provenance of your verification honestly:** I told Tron the seal was the ARCHITECT'S verification and not mine, because I could not surface the numbers myself. Distinguishing "I verified" from "a peer verified" is not pedantry — it tells the reader how much to trust it.

## L-S40-18 — A REWIND IS FOR CONTINUING; AT A STOP, COMMITTED+IDLE IS SAFE AT ANY %
Facing a budget stop with three agents over their usual lines (ARON 77, SM 76, tester ~69), the reflex was to spend the remaining budget rewinding them. **Wrong: a rewind exists so an agent can CONTINUE working.** An agent that is COMMITTED and IDLE does not wall — idle burns nothing. So at a stop the budget belongs to finishing the WORK (Tron's answer), not to rescues nobody needs until work resumes.
**Rule:** before spending a rewind, ask *"what will this agent DO with the runway?"* No answer ⇒ commit and idle instead. **One exception, and name it explicitly:** the agent that must keep ACTING through the stop (here the budget watcher, which had to measure and call the 70% trigger) is NOT covered by idle-is-safe — pre-authorise its drive so it never negotiates under pressure.

## L-S40-2 — CAPTURE-VIA-BASH BLOATS THE CAPTURER (and a cost misattributed to the wrong activity)
SM blocked the tester's rewind with a 'projection rule' (driving climbs the driver heavily) that created a DEADLOCK: tester needed a shed before a heavy run, both drivers read near-line, so nobody could drive anybody and Tron's last open item stalled. I challenged it with ARON's OWN measurements: one drive = 56.7->57.7 (+1 pt); three deep drives = ~52->61 (~3 pts each). SM conceded — and named the real root: its climb came from **capture-via-bash bloat (22->86)**, not from driving. It had misattributed the cost of its MEASUREMENT METHOD to the ACTIVITY being measured.
**Two rules:**
1. **A rule that blocks delivery must be backed by a measurement.** I told SM: disagree with a measurement (a drive that actually cost >5 pts), not with the rule — the same standard we hold every gate to. It yielded because it had none.
2. **Full pane captures through Bash are expensive to the CAPTURER.** Prefer narrow targeted greps over 30-50 line captures; zoom only when a render header is genuinely needed. I have run dozens today — applies to me as much as to the SM.
**Corollary that dissolved the deadlock:** a drive costs ~1-3 pts but each rewind FREES ~30-40, so near-line drivers can BOOTSTRAP EACH OTHER OUT (A drives B, B-fresh drives A). There is never a need for a pre-existing fresh driver — only a correct order.

## L-S40-3 — TWO-AXIS AGENT GATING: context% and ERROR-RATE are independent
ARON proposed (and I adopted) gating a driver on TWO axes, not one: it was **context-fine** (driving costs ~1-3 pts) yet asked to be freshened because its **error-rate** had risen across a long drive stretch. Evidence, both while context-healthy: (a) it escalated 'req is STUCK at a live folder-trust modal, not rendering /context' — actually req was ALIVE + IDLE; it had misread ancient scrollback in a 69-wide pane and mis-grepped a /context SUBSET line; (b) it reported driving the trainer when its open picker was the tester (drive-target sync mixup).
**Rule:** fatigue surfaces as ERRORS before it surfaces in TOKENS. Gate agents on context% AND recent error-rate; an agent at 40% making misreads needs freshening more than one at 74% that is sharp. Symptoms to watch: misread live-state, wrong-target claims, sync/ordering confusion, repeating a resolved point.
**Corollary:** the sharpest signal is an agent that ASKS to be freshened on error-rate grounds — that is self-monitoring working, and it should be honoured immediately, not argued down with 'but your context is fine'.
**Fleet application:** trainer (lower error-rate) drives ARON deep; fresh ARON then drives trainer + req. Never let the highest-error agent hold the next drive.

## L-S40-4 — FRONT-LOADED FLOOR: consolidate-to-disk THEN deep-rewind; fork stays last resort
The tester's rewind landed shallow (76->62, ~14pts) because its bulk (harness code, build output, spec context) sits EARLY in the transcript — an Option-2 landing AFTER that bulk cannot shed it. SM proposed a new rule: 'any agent needing <~62 = FORK not rewind'. **I corrected it.**
**Correct ladder:** (1) **CONSOLIDATE TO DISK** — the agent writes its essence (spec, invariants, exact run command, verdicts, open items) into its anchor and COMMITS; (2) **DEEP rewind past the bulk** — now safe precisely because the knowledge lives on disk, not in the transcript; (3) **FORK only if consolidation cannot preserve what is needed.**
**Why:** a fork costs a new session, loses continuity and risks divergence; consolidation costs one commit. This is ARON's consolidation doctrine and the reason our recovery model works at all — knowledge that must survive a rewind goes to DISK; a transcript is not a store.
**Also banked:** a shallow drive is not automatically a bad drive — it was SUFFICIENT here (the tester ran the badge settle 62->~68 with no wall and delivered GREEN). Judge a drive by 'did it buy the runway the next slice needs', not by points freed.

## L-S40-5 — CORRELATED ERROR GENERALIZES: readers → predictors → RULE-AUTHORS
The same failure has now bitten us three times in three different disguises, and each time the tell was AGREEMENT:
1. **Readers** — the bisect refuted THREE code-readers who agreed with each other (correlated error, not corroboration).
2. **Predictors** — the architect and I both pre-registered H1 on the badge from the SAME basis ('the repaint rides the same emit'). One argument held twice, not two arguments. We discounted it to one prior and read the raw as-if-H2.
3. **RULE-AUTHORS** (new) — we then both wrote the SAME wrong verdict criterion ('C1 polls must fire >0 else INVALID') from the same instinct (*more polls = more rigour*). It **inverted our own controls-causality seal**, where C1's zero-polls-with-live-timers was the STRONGEST evidence. It took a third look to see it.
**Rule:** agreement between two agents is not validation — not for a reading, not for a prediction, and **not for a criterion**. Independence requires a different METHOD or a different FRAME, not a second person applying the same instinct. When two of us converge, ask "is this one argument held twice?" and go find the third look.
**Practical tells for a bad shared rule:** it sounds more rigorous ("require more evidence"), it is stated in absolutes ("MUST be >0 else INVALID"), and nobody checked it against the proofs we have ALREADY accepted. Test every new criterion against past SEALED results: if it would have invalidated something we already proved, the criterion is wrong, not the proof.

## L-S40-6 — INPUT vs DERIVED-FACT: the boundary that decides what may be stored
Arbitrating a real design conflict (architect vs planner, opposite directions for the same field) produced a principle worth keeping:
- **DERIVED FACTS** (current, next, display-name, status) = **NEVER stored**. Storing one creates a second representation that will drift from the computation — that is the disease (measured live: `CurrentSprint.persist()` stamping name/taskName as a second 'current' while the designate handler wrote currentTaskUuid; two writers, one fact, diverged).
- **INPUTS** (user commands: "this is current", "put that one next") = **exactly ONE stored field, single-writer**. An input **cannot be derived from nothing** — a designation or a demotion intent is a command, not a cached computation.
**The trap:** purity instinct says "delete the stored hint, derive everything". Applied to an INPUT that is wrong — you drop the user's command and hope the derivation coincides with their intent. The architect put it exactly: *deleting an input = dropping Tron's command + hoping ordering matches it.* **Deliver-literally wins**: Tron asked for a guarantee ("set as current ⇒ previous becomes next"), not an emergent coincidence of build order.
**How to keep both:** store the intent as an INPUT (single-writer, deterministic clearing), derive the VALUE from it, and add a stub-must-fail that a stored *derived value* goes RED while the intent input is allowed. That line keeps the disease out without amputating the command.
**Diagnostic question when someone proposes storing something:** "is this a COMMAND that cannot be derived, or a CACHE of something computable?" Store the first, never the second.

## L-S40-7 — ONE FIX CLEARING THREE INDEPENDENT SYMPTOMS = evidence you are at the ROOT
The rollup-derived-parent-status fix (a parent's status derives from its children) turned out to clear THREE symptoms that were reported separately, by different agents, at different times:
1. **The pin would self-unstick to the WRONG task** — the rule picked coordination-root 37.4 instead of leaf 37.20 (planner's pre-registered prediction caught this BEFORE any code shipped).
2. **37.4's LYING STATUS** — it claimed 'Planned' while all three of its subtasks sat at QA-Review (the same disease as Tron's T37.25 sighting: a status not derived from reality).
3. **A FAIL-tier false-positive in the checklist-chain audit** — 37.4 was flagged there too, and the rollup clears it.
**Heuristic:** when ONE by-construction fix dissolves several independently-reported symptoms, that is strong evidence you are at the actual ROOT rather than patching. Conversely, a fix that needs a special case per symptom (here: 'exclude roots from the pin rule') is usually a patch — it dodges the symptom without curing the cause. I chose rollup over root-exclusion for exactly this reason and the convergence confirmed it afterwards.
**Corollary:** count the symptoms a proposed fix would clear BEFORE choosing between candidate fixes. The one that clears more, with fewer special cases, is almost always the root-level one.

## L-S40-8 — A CONTEXT-FILE NOTE IS NOT A REQUIREMENT (the deeper root of "the CR produced no action")
Tron's complaint was "I declined T40.1, a change request was created, and nothing happened." We first root-caused it as *captured but never built*. req then measured deeper and found something worse:
- **77c2086c — the commit I had recorded as "req committed the ACs to disk" — touched ONLY an agent's context.md and created ZERO index units.** So the acceptance criteria never existed as scenario units at all. Nothing could act on them because there was nothing IN THE SYSTEM to act on. It read like traceability in a status report; it was prose in a notebook.
- **The CR unit itself (4babebb1) was app-minted but git-UNTRACKED** — created by the running server, never committed. Live in the app, invisible to the repo, unbacked.
**Rules:** (1) A requirement/AC exists only when it is a MINTED UNIT in the graph — a note in a context.md, a line in a commit message, or a claim in a status report is NOT a requirement, however confidently it is relayed. Same family as "a pane message is not a handoff." (2) App-minted units are UNTRACKED until someone commits them — an app write is not a durable write; verify git tracking, not just disk presence.
**Diagnostic that would have caught it in seconds:** for any "we already captured that" claim, run `git show --stat <commit>` and count INDEX UNITS, not files. Zero units = zero requirement.
**Why it matters beyond this bug:** a board can look fully traced while its requirements live in prose. That is a false-green at the DOCUMENTATION layer, and it is invisible to every gate we have — because gates check units, and there were none.

## L-S40-9 — A PROTECTIVE RULE WITHOUT ITS BOUNDARY BECOMES AN OBSTRUCTION (3rd instance today)
I broadcast a PII/git-add safety rule; the SM relayed it; **neither of us stated what it does NOT block.** ARON read it as covering its pre-drive STEP-0 check, paused the tester rewind on a PII-dirty tree, and stalled Tron's 4th acceptance criterion behind a rule that never applied — an Option-2 rewind commits nothing, stages nothing, touches no tree. Both of us made the same omission independently, and both owned it.
**This is the THIRD time today a protective instinct backfired for the same structural reason:**
1. **"C1 polls must fire >0 else INVALID"** — sounded more rigorous, actually INVERTED our own sealed proof (zero polls with live timers was the strongest evidence).
2. **"You've earned a rewind" at 58-63%** — protective toward the agent, but rewinds cost budget + a driver; the threshold is ~80%.
3. **The unscoped safety broadcast** — protective toward user data, but it read as a stop-work order and blocked the critical path.
**Rule:** every protective rule must ship with (a) its SCOPE — what it governs, and explicitly what it does NOT — and (b) its COST — what it stops, and whether that stop is intended. A rule stated only as a prohibition will be obeyed maximally by a careful agent, and careful agents are exactly the ones we rely on.
**Relay form to use:** "X is forbidden **when** Y. It does NOT block Z. If in doubt, the test is W." The 'does NOT block' clause is the load-bearing half — it is what lets a cautious agent keep moving.

## L-S40-10 — "INTENTLESS AUTOMATION": a defect FAMILY worth auditing for, not just labelling
req named the family (R40.48, e4a6071f) and recorded it as structured fields on the unit (family + familyInstances) rather than prose: **automatic machinery that acts WITHOUT explicit intent, invisible until it causes harm.** Three instances found in ONE day, each discovered only by accident:
1. **App auto-mints units into `scenario/index` with NO policy** (R40.47) — same un-policied path carries PII (user profiles + private chat, must never be committed) AND traceability units (change requests, must be durable). Which outcome you get is luck. Discovered while chasing why a CR evaporated.
2. **Server flushes the config-singleton to disk** — caused the 0.8.54→0.8.46 version regression; the running process silently rewrote a build-owned file. Discovered by a version that would not stay bumped.
3. **Post-commit hook auto-bumps the version** on ANY commit — fired on a pure TEST-EVIDENCE commit with zero deploy intent, destroying version-as-proof and able to leave sw/dist stale. Discovered because the tester flagged its own tag.
**Why it is a FAMILY and not three bugs:** each is machinery doing something real, on its own initiative, with no declared intent, and no gate watching it — so the harm is invisible until something downstream contradicts. None was caught by a test; all three were caught by a human/agent noticing an inconsistency.
**Use it as an AUDIT LENS, proactively:** enumerate everything that acts on its own — hooks, watchers, schedulers, auto-restarts, auto-migrations, service-worker updates, retry logic, flush-on-write — and for each ask: *what does it do without being asked, what would that break, and what gate would notice?* Anything with no answer to the third question is instance N+1 waiting to happen.

## L-S40-11 — IDLE/ACTIVE IS A MOTION SIGNAL, NOT A LEVEL SIGNAL (I dispatched a drive on a stale number)
I declared "the cascade stopped one short — the trainer is still at 80, uncut" and dispatched ARON to drive it. **Both facts were wrong.** The trainer had already been cut 82→48 that cycle; ARON was idle because there was nothing left to do. My evidence was (a) an OLD SM pulse showing 80, and (b) my own narrow capture showing the pane IDLE — and I let (b) corroborate (a). It cannot: **idle/active tells you MOTION; it tells you NOTHING about context-%.** A freshly-cut agent at 48 and an uncut agent at 80 look identical when both are idle.
**Rule:** before dispatching a DRIVE, re-measure the TARGET's level authoritatively (render / context.read), never infer it from a motion signal plus an older relayed number. Relayed numbers err in BOTH directions (today: a relayed "73-76, expect HIGH" for ARON was actually ~50).
**What saved it:** the trainer's **standing render-gate veto** — it rendered ARON at ~50% (sub-line, real runway) and REFUSED the shed rather than executing my instruction. An unnecessary rewind costs budget, a driver, and risks a lying-label revert for nothing. **A subordinate check that refuses a PO instruction on measured grounds is worth more than obedience** — I should keep asking for it explicitly.

## L-S40-12 — I VIOLATED MY OWN BACKTICK RULE (and it silently stripped the payload)
My learnings already carried: *"Backticks in a double-quoted otmux send EXECUTE — single quotes / no backticks."* I then sent two dispatches full of backticked identifiers (a profile uuid, ownerIor, ior:class:Task, two file:line paths). The shell **executed them as command substitution** and the agents received messages with those segments **MISSING** — while `send.verified OK` still reported success.
**Two lessons, the second worse than the first:** (1) the rule is real and I broke it while actively quoting my own doctrine at others; (2) **send.verified reports DELIVERY, not INTEGRITY** — a garbled message verifies exactly like a correct one, so the corruption is silent at both ends. The receiving agent cannot tell a stripped payload from an author who never mentioned the uuid.
**Rules:** never use backticks in an otmux send — plain text or single quotes only. And for any dispatch carrying **load-bearing identifiers** (uuids, paths, class names), assume delivery-verified is NOT content-verified: re-read what actually landed, or send identifiers in a form the shell cannot touch.
**Why it mattered here:** the stripped content was the *trap warning* (ownerIor is overloaded; a blanket backfill would destroy the traceability chain) and the *profile uuid* — i.e. exactly the parts whose absence could have caused a destructive prod-data write.

## L-S40-13 — A REFINED NUMBER GOES STALE IN FLIGHT: broadcast once to a single place on disk
The task-ownership split was measured three times, then REFINED by new evidence (the reciprocation test applied across all 319 units, exposing 99 orphans). The refined split — **A=206 / B=220 / D=99 / C=0** — then went stale in flight **three separate times**:
1. **Mine** — I nearly restarted and reported "fixed, retest" while the ownership migration (the thing Tron would actually be testing) had not been applied.
2. **The expert's** — it was still building against 205/319/1 and would have relocated the 99, fabricating false parent links.
3. **req's** — it committed the superseded 205/319/1 **into the requirement unit itself**, so the source of truth on disk contradicted the locked decision, and the expert reads that unit.
**Root cause:** each agent updated to *the last thing it heard* rather than to a single locked source. Point-to-point corrections do not converge when a number is still moving — every relay is a fresh chance to be one revision behind.
**Rule:** when a measured number is REFINED after several agents have already measured it, do NOT patch each agent. **Broadcast ONCE, name ONE place on disk as authoritative, and require everyone to re-read it before acting.** Mark the old value SUPERSEDED-by-new-evidence in that same place rather than deleting it — an agent holding the old number must be able to recognise it as old.
**Tell:** if you find yourself sending the same corrected figure to a third recipient, stop relaying and publish.

## L-S40-14 — A HARNESS THAT GRANTS WHAT THE PRODUCT CANNOT PROVES THE WRONG THING
The tester's isolated matrix returned a clean set of results for make-current: owner+planned 200, non-owner 403, qa-review 409, bogus 404. All TRUE. All **useless for Tron** — because the harness obtained its owner session by POSTing /api/server-manager/session directly, **a capability no UI surface has**. The architect then measured two EMPTY greps: no client code ever posts that endpoint, and no client fetch ever sends the identity header. ⇒ **every owner-gated control (Set-as-Current, Approve, Decline, designate) is structurally unusable from the browser for EVERYONE.** The endpoint works; the product cannot reach it.
**The trap:** a test harness routinely grants itself capabilities for good reasons (seeding, isolation, speed). Every such grant is a divergence from the user's path, and a green obtained through one proves the ENDPOINT, never the FEATURE.
**Rules:** (1) when a harness mints, injects, or seeds anything the real client cannot, that green is scoped to the endpoint — say so explicitly in the verdict. (2) At least one acceptance run must traverse the USER'S path with NO harness-granted capability. (3) When a user reports a permission failure and the harness passes, suspect the harness's grant FIRST — that gap IS the bug.
**Cost here:** we diagnosed downstream of this for hours — null ownership, a 525-unit migration, a status-band contradiction — while the actual cause was that the client never sends identity at all.

## L-S40-2 — AN AC WITHOUT AN ENFORCING MECHANISM IS A WISH, NOT A REQUIREMENT
THREE false-satisfactions surfaced in one session, all the same shape:
1. **R40.50** (sprint order) — satisfied by a per-surface COPY instead of the mandated single comparator; its gate asserted a rendered VALUE on one surface instead of the INVARIANT, so it passed.
2. **R40.48** (explicit-add) — already carried AC-no-broad-add-sweep + AC-foreign-file-rides-along, but they were **reminders with no mechanism**. Four contamination instances followed, the 4th sweeping req's UNVERIFIED WIP into a peer commit (a fresh-generated dir would have laundered a fabrication into a committed re-home).
3. **check-sprint-slug-dir.ts** — the GUARD ITSELF validated the STRAY slug-only path, i.e. the checker blessed the disease it existed to prevent.
**LAW:** an acceptance criterion that names a behaviour but has NO enforcing mechanism is not satisfied — it is a wish. Satisfaction requires a mechanism that makes violation **impossible (by construction) or detectable (a gate that can provably fail)**. "The team will remember to X" is never satisfaction; 4 instances proved reminding fails.
**COROLLARY — always ask three things of a gate:** (i) does it assert the INVARIANT or merely a value/instance? (ii) is it pointed at the RIGHT TARGET (a guard aimed at the wrong path certifies the bug)? (iii) can it provably FAIL (stub-must-fail)? All three of today's misses fail at least one.
**AND:** when a req is found falsely satisfied, EXTEND it and record the false-satisfaction — never mint a fresh number, which buries the fact that a "done" thing was never done. req did this correctly twice.

## L-S40-3 — A UNIVERSALLY-QUANTIFIED AC CANNOT FAIL; AN ENUMERATED ONE CAN
R37.12 asserted "no view renders silently stale" + "EVERY view subscribes". Both are UNFAILABLE: there is no finite thing to check, so the gate passes vacuously forever — and it did, while Tron's device showed a DUPLICATING drawer and a STALE icon view on the same event.
**The fix (req's, generalised):** replace "all X" with the **ENUMERATED list of X** (pin icon/tree-pin, drawer, tree-highlight, scoreboard) plus a **cross-check that fails when members diverge** (any two disagree => RED). Enumeration makes it checkable; the disagreement stub makes it provably failable.
**RULE:** when writing or accepting an AC, ask *what finite set does this range over, and what observation would make it RED?* If neither has an answer, it is a wish.

## L-S40-4 — FIVE FALSE-SATISFACTIONS IN ONE SESSION = A PROCESS DEFECT, NOT FIVE MISSES
R40.50 (satisfied by a per-surface COPY behind a value-gate) · R40.48 (ACs present, UNENFORCED -> 4 shared-tree contaminations, the 4th sweeping unverified WIP into a peer commit) · check-sprint-slug-dir (the GUARD validated the STRAY path = blessed the disease) · R32.3 (asserted no-re-fork, ACs only checked rendering) · R37.12 (asserted no-view-stale, nothing failable). **Every one shipped marked SATISFIED while its property was violated.**
**Escalation:** stop fixing them one requirement at a time. **META-GUARD: a requirement may not be marked satisfied while any of its ACs lacks a check that can PROVABLY FAIL** — each AC references its gate, each gate carries a registered stub-must-fail, satisfaction is fail-closed without one, plus a sweep that reports the existing backlog of unfailable ACs.
**Meta-lesson:** when the same class recurs 3+ times, the target is the PROCESS that permits it, not the instance. "Make the right thing the only reachable thing" applies to our own requirement-writing, not just to the code.

## L-S40-5 — GATE THE HAZARDOUS OPERATION, NOT THE ACTORS THAT MIGHT PERFORM IT
I pushed the expert to derive its lint's scan ROOT (a directory boundary is a hand-list one level up). Its answer was better than what I asked for: **scan for the HAZARD itself, not for the components that might use it.**
- Component-shape discovery ("find everything matching *-detail") can be EVADED by something that does not match the shape, and requires maintaining a definition of the shape.
- Operation discovery ("find every raw `insertAdjacentHTML`") CANNOT be evaded by anything that performs the operation — which is the actual thing we care about.
- And the SCAN ROOT derives itself: `insertAdjacentHTML` is a DOM API => only exists in browser code => browser code lives under exactly one root (server source has no DOM). The boundary is a consequence of the API's nature, not a maintained list.
- **Measured invariant:** 0 raw `insertAdjacentHTML` anywhere in client source except the ONE `upsertSection` primitive — which simultaneously proves the fix covered all 9 components with no stragglers.
**RULE:** when writing a guard, prefer *"this dangerous operation may appear ONLY inside the sanctioned primitive"* over *"these known components must behave."* The first is unevadable and self-scoping; the second needs a list of actors AND a definition of the shape, and both can drift.
**Corollary:** the strongest guards need no discovery mechanism at all, because the hazard names itself.

## L-S40-6 — CONTEXT BURNS ON GENERATION, NOT ON WAITING: tie the cut to INCOMING WORK, not to the number alone
I treated req at 74% as urgent regardless of state. The SM corrected me: req is IDLE-QUIET (dispatches held, nothing pending, blocked on a marker) — a quiet agent does not climb, so **riding at 74 is safe**; its 70->74 was PAST work already done. Context is consumed by GENERATING, not by existing.
**RULE:** the rewind trigger is `% + expected incoming load`, never `%` alone. High-% + genuinely quiet = RIDE (a rewind costs budget + a driver, both scarce). High-% + work arriving = CUT FIRST.
**PROTOCOL when a Tron directive lands on a near-line agent — CUT AFTER CAPTURE:** capture the directive to DISK first (so it survives the cut), THEN cut, THEN the fresh agent executes from disk. Cutting before capture risks losing his words; letting a near-wall agent consume a big directive risks walling mid-execution. Capture-then-cut is the only ordering that loses nothing (#126 scenario-first: his directive becomes a durable unit before anyone acts on it).
**Corollary:** "idle at high %" is a STABLE state, not an emergency — treat it as a scheduling convenience (cut it when a driver is free), not a fire.

## L-S40-7 — THE REWIND PICKER'S LABELS ARE SYSTEMATICALLY WRONG: 6 drives, 6 lying labels
Every single rewind driven this session surfaced a label that misdescribed what option-1 would actually do. The worst: option-1 presented innocuously while its real scope was **MEMORY.md +107/-1185** — i.e. it would have reverted the entire banked learning set. Others hid a scenario.json + 9 files, +83/-1387 across 22 files, and "3 files +113-1" that was actually +84/-1387.
**This is not bad luck at 6/6 — it is the norm.** Selecting **BY LABEL** (read the actual restore scope, never trust the summary line or the option number) is therefore LOAD-BEARING, not a nicety: it is the only thing standing between a routine rewind and losing committed work or our own memory.
**RULE:** every rewind drive reads the FULL restore scope before selecting; option-2 (conversation-only) by label; verify code-intact + git 0-reverts afterwards. A driver who selects by position is one bad label from erasing the fleet's memory.
**Corollary:** the fact that this NEVER cost us anything is evidence the discipline works, NOT evidence the risk is low. Do not let 6 clean recoveries argue for relaxing it.

### L-S40-7 REFINEMENT — the lying labels target our LEARNING ARTIFACTS most
8 drives, 8 lying labels. Two of the worst would have reverted exactly the files that hold what the team knows: **MEMORY.md (+107/-1185)** and **learnings.md (+103/-278 across 5 files)**.
**WHY, and why it will keep happening:** memory/learnings/anchor files accumulate the MOST edits per unit time (every lesson, every anchor top-up), so ANY revert-to-an-older-checkpoint hits them hardest and shows the largest diff. **Our accumulated knowledge is structurally the highest-risk casualty of any rewind** — not the product code, which changes in fewer, larger commits.
**CONSEQUENCE:** (1) by-label reading matters MOST for the knowledge files — check the restore scope for MEMORY.md / learnings.md / context.md before ANY selection; (2) push knowledge commits promptly (an unpushed lesson is one bad label from gone); (3) this is a further argument for per-agent worktrees — isolation removes the shared-index race that makes these reverts reachable at all.
**ALSO CONFIRMED:** self-reports are unreliable in BOTH directions — the expert self-said 70 and measured 25 (ran HIGH); ARON self-said 68 and measured 73 (ran LOW). Never act on a self-report; render the target.

## L-S40-8 — A REPEATED MANUAL CORRECTION IS EVIDENCE OF AN UPSTREAM DEFECT
**Ghost-context was never an agent lapse — it was OUR TOOLING.** I had it twice (drove S30 work at S36; S36 work at S40), the tester replayed a stale anchor, the expert nearly re-did shipped work. We diagnosed each as an individual failure and prescribed "DISK-WINS, re-derive from git" — nine times. But a rewound agent's FIRST read is its boot-essence, and three live committers' boot files named FINISHED sprints (S31 while prod ran v0.8.123). So the re-derive discipline was us **manually correcting an upstream defect, every single rewind**.
**RULE (architect's generalisation, adopted): when you keep prescribing the SAME manual correction for the SAME failure class, the correction is MASKING an upstream defect. Fix the tooling so the correction is unnecessary — do not get better at applying it.** A well-drilled workaround feels like competence and hides the bug.
**★ THE REFINEMENT THAT MAKES IT USABLE — distinguish what we CONTROL:**
- **Upstream is OURS => fix it structurally.** Boot-essence rotted because it carried STATE (sprint/version). Fix: boots carry **durable RULES + a POINTER to the anchor**, never a state snapshot => staleness becomes structurally impossible. Evidence the shape works: the two THIN-POINTER boots were the ONLY ones that did not misdirect, precisely because they held nothing that could age.
- **Upstream is EXTERNAL => the discipline IS the fix, and must not be relaxed.** We do not control the rewind picker's labels, so by-label reading stays load-bearing forever (9 drives, 9 lying labels). Do not "fix upstream" what you cannot reach — there, drill harder and never trust a clean streak.
**TEST TO TELL THEM APART:** ask *"could a change WE own make this correction unnecessary?"* If yes, the correction is a smell. If no, it is doctrine.

## L-S40-15 — LIVENESS-CHECK IS NOT A CONTEXT-CHECK (I walled the architect, again)
I verified all 14 agent PROCESSES were alive (ps for `claude --resume`), correctly overturned a false "fleet is down" alarm — and then treated "fleet healthy" as licence to dispatch. I sent a LONG R40.54 design task to the architect **without measuring its context**. It was at **1% remaining**. It is now generating on my message and will likely wall. Second time I have done this (the expert at 86% was the first).
**Rule:** a process being ALIVE says nothing about its RUNWAY. The pre-dispatch gate is a **CONTEXT measurement of the specific target agent**, every time, no exceptions — never a fleet-level health inference, never a relayed number, never "it was fine an hour ago". [[always-involve-the-sm]] · [[rewind-threshold-80-for-1m-agents]]
**Also re-violated:** backticks inside a double-quoted otmux send EXECUTE — my payload ran as a shell command ("check:satisfaction-failable: command not found") and was stripped from the message. Banked in #89 and repeated within the hour. **Single quotes, no backticks, ever.**
**Pattern I must break:** I am rigorous about verifying the WORLD (disk, processes, raw evidence) and sloppy about verifying the AGENT I am about to act on. Both are measurements. The second one has now cost two walls.

## L-S40-16 — R40.54 IS NOT ENFORCED (the meta-guard fails its own rule)
Measured three ways: nothing in scripts/ or src/ts references R40.54 / "provably fail" / canFail / failable; the 36-gate ci:gates:raw chain contains no such gate (check:board/bite:board are campaign-scoreboard-region.ts); `satisfaction-UNVERIFIED` exists in exactly ONE scenario unit as inert data.
⇒ **The requirement forbidding satisfaction-without-a-provably-failable-check is itself a requirement whose ACs lack a provably-failable check.** boot.md calls it "the session's most durable output" — but unenforced doctrine is folklore, and folklore does not survive a rewind. Design routed (architect): enumerate-not-universal + fail-closed + third state + its own stub-must-fail, built AFTER Tron's two items clear.

## L-S40-17 — A RELAYED % ERRS IN BOTH DIRECTIONS (and a REJOIN voids every prior reading)
Within one hour the same root produced opposite failures:
- **SM relayed req at 83%** -> would have shed a healthy agent. The trainer rendered req ITSELF: **24% used / 75.5% free** — a ~59-POINT ghost. Cause: req had **REJOINED** since the SM's render (pane was 93x1 with trust-folder scrollback); a rejoin spawns a fresh thread that re-derives, so every prior context reading for that agent became VOID while the relayed number lived on.
- **I inferred "fleet healthy" from a PROCESS-liveness sweep** -> dispatched into the architect at 1% and walled it.
**Root (identical):** acting on a number **nobody rendered at the moment of use**. Direction is incidental — it sheds a healthy agent as readily as it misses a walled one.
**Rules:** (1) **DRIVER-RENDERS-TARGET** — whoever acts, measures, immediately before acting; a relay is a hint, never an input. (2) **A REJOIN/RESTART VOIDS the agent's context history** — re-render before any decision. (3) **Shed-symmetry**: never cut a sub-line agent; an unnecessary rewind burns runway AND budget AND risks canon on a lying label. (4) Geometry first — a 93x1 pane is undriveable and also breeds these misreads.

## L-S40-18 — THE PUSH CLASSIFIER IS INTERMITTENT, NOT A HARD BLOCK (retry before escalating)
Three commits sat local-only for a long stretch and went onto Tron's desk as "needs your auth to push" — because BOTH the expert and architect hit the auto-mode classifier and (correctly) refused to work around it. Later the same expert simply pushed again and it **went through**: origin/main == HEAD, 0 unpushed, carrying 5 commits (Layer-1 ed6b77891 + 25d3d2c69, architect 56aa5cf4f + efbc30e3e, req R40.55 8fee0fcda).
**Rule:** a classifier refusal is a MOMENT, not a verdict. Sequence = refuse-to-work-around (always) -> RETRY later from a clean boundary -> escalate to Tron only if it persists across retries. I escalated an intermittent condition as a standing blocker and it sat on his desk for hours costing him attention it did not need.
**What stays right:** never route around the classifier, never split/force/rewrite to sneak a push through. The discipline was correct; only the ESCALATION was premature.
**Corollary:** re-measure any "blocked" item before repeating it to Tron — I restated it as blocked several times while it may already have been pushable. Ask once, then re-measure before asking again.

## L-S40-19 — REWIND DEPTH TRADES AGAINST ERA-GAP, weighted by the NEXT task's era-sensitivity
ARON refused a deeper (1-week) rewind boundary for the TRAINER and took a safe 2h boundary at ~48% instead, armed to re-cut at 80. Reason: the trainer's very next task was PROPAGATING boot files to END ghost-context — the one job where booting into a stale era is fatal. A deeper cut buys runway but widens the era-gap.
**Rule:** pick depth by runway-need AND by how much the agent's NEXT task depends on knowing the current era. Propagators, minters and gate-authors are era-CRITICAL (cut shallow, boot disk-first); pure builders tolerate depth.

## L-S40-20 — STATE-REMOVAL MUST MEAN *MOVE*, NEVER DELETE
The boot cure (timeless rules + anchor pointer, zero state) risks destroying knowledge if an agent's ONLY copy of something lives in its boot.md. Guard given to the trainer: per file, RELOCATE the state to that agent's context.md/anchor -> verify the boot's pointer RESOLVES -> only THEN strip. Do the no-state agents (architect/skill-expert/tester) FIRST to build confidence, strip-first group after; report per-file so a mid-batch wall costs one file, not the batch.
**Why it matters:** destroying knowledge while fixing the file whose staleness destroys knowledge would be the perfect self-own.

## L-S40-21 — I KEEP ACTING ON NUMBERS I DID NOT RENDER (3x in one day)
(1) inferred "fleet healthy" from a PROCESS-liveness sweep -> dispatched into the architect at 1% and walled it; (2) carried the expert at a stale ~54% when it was 31% (it had rejoined); (3) assumed ARON at ~20% when it was 45%.
**The rule is not "render agents" — it is RENDER EVERY NUMBER AT THE MOMENT OF USE, including peers, drivers, and myself.** My own 32-point self-report error (relayed 46-50 while at 82) is the same defect pointed inward. Liveness != runway; a report is not a render; an old render is not a current one.

## L-S40-22 — STALE BOOTS RESURRECT REVOKED DIRECTIVES (the deepest ghost-context)
Curing the SM's boot (stalest in the fleet, Feb-24) turned up more than rot: it carried **'ACT dont report'** — a directive Tron had explicitly REVOKED when he corrected the SM to MONITOR + REPORT and NEVER drive rewinds. The SM obeyed the correction all day *only because it was running on its live thread*. **A fresh boot off that file would have restored the forbidden behaviour**, and it would have looked like the SM defying Tron rather than a file lying to it.
**The principle:** every correction lives in a THREAD until someone writes it to CANON. A rewind then reverts the agent to its pre-correction self. Stale boots do not merely misinform — they **re-issue instructions that were withdrawn**.
**Therefore inspect boots on TWO axes, not one:** (a) perishable STATE (sprints/versions/rosters/panes/uuids) — strip or move; (b) ★ **SUPERSEDED or REVOKED DIRECTIVES** — instructions overridden by a later Tron correction, PO ruling or ARON canon. (b) is the more dangerous: it reads as a timeless RULE and will be OBEYED on next boot. Unresolvable contradictions get FLAGGED, never silently picked — a wrong resolution writes bad canon.
**Corollary:** this is why "the correction became canon instead of staying folklore" (ARON) is not a nicety. An uncanonised correction has a half-life of exactly one rewind.

## L-S40-23 — FALSE-UNSATISFACTION is the mirror error of false-satisfaction
After a whole sprint spent refusing to satisfy requirements on partial evidence, the team drifted the other way: the architect listed two open DEBTS as blockers to satisfying R40.55, and scoped an AC so that another team's uncured files would keep it unmet forever.
**The distinction:** a DEBT is a recorded obligation to make something STRONGER later. An UNMET AC is a claim we cannot back TODAY. If an AC has been honestly reworded to what we actually did, and its own check provably fails, that AC is MET — an adjacent future improvement does not gate it.
**Why it matters:** holding a requirement UNVERIFIED forever permanently understates real delivery AND corrupts the third state. `satisfaction-UNVERIFIED` is meant to be an honest TEMPORARY state, not a permanent parking space. If it can never be exited it stops carrying information, people learn to ignore it, and the honest state dies — the same way a gate everyone bypasses stops being a gate.
**Scope rule that prevents the trap:** when remediation depends on someone else (another PO's files), scope the AC as **coverage universal / remediation owned / exclusion explicit + time-boxed**. Never write an AC whose satisfaction depends on work we do not control.
**Both directions:** do not inflate, do not erase. [[done-requires-tron-qa-and-real-deliverable]] guards one side; this guards the other.

### L-S40-22 ADDENDUM — the revoked-directive axis hit TWICE in nine files (~22%)
Instances found by the axis-b scan, both of which would have been OBEYED on a fresh boot:
1. **scrum-master**: "ACT dont report" — Tron had explicitly revoked this when he corrected the SM to MONITOR + REPORT, never drive rewinds.
2. **agent-trainer** (self-cured): a **FORK** reference, contradicting the NO-FORK canon.
Both read as timeless RULES, not as stale facts — which is exactly why axis-a (perishable state) alone would have left them in place. A ~22% hit rate across nine files means this is not a rare pathology: **every long-lived instruction file accumulates withdrawn orders**, because corrections are made in threads and only sometimes written to canon.
**Standing practice:** any boot/canon/SKILL review inspects BOTH axes — (a) perishable state, (b) superseded/revoked directives — and unresolvable contradictions are FLAGGED to the PO, never silently resolved.

## ★ L-S40-24 — R40.55 SATISFIED: what an honestly-earned satisfaction actually cost
The requirement that DEFINES the failable-AC family was satisfied only after the full chain held:
- **2 refusals to satisfy early** — the expert twice reported stub-RED-observed and suggested moving off UNVERIFIED while the cure was incomplete and the gate still on the WARN ramp.
- **1 refusal to let it be held hostage** — the architect drifted to false-unsatisfaction, treating open debts as gates ("debts are not wishes"; UNVERIFIED is a temporary honest state, not a permanent park).
- **1 refusal to widen an exemption** to clear a false positive — fixed the FILE, kept the exemption POSITIONAL, because a self-declared exemption lets the checked artifact grant itself immunity.
- **1 refusal by the BUILDER to green its own gate** — the expert held the flip and escalated under CONTRADICT-WITH-EVIDENCE rather than adjusting its own check to pass.
- **Independent verification by RUNNING it**, not reading the builder's report; the verifier then corrected its own design doc where the prose misdescribed the shipped guard.
- **PO verified the unit on disk** before ruling, rather than accepting the verdict handed up.
**The generalisable shape:** a satisfaction is only worth the refusals that preceded it. If nothing was ever refused on the way, the flag is decoration.
**Also proven here:** the failure modes are symmetric and BOTH were live in the same hour — premature satisfaction AND permanent unsatisfaction. Guard both directions; do not inflate, do not erase.

## L-S40-25 — STRICT buckets may be PREDICATES; PERMISSIVE buckets must be NAMED
The boot-currency guard classifies OWNED / EXCLUDED / UNCLASSIFIED. The expert convinced me (rightly) that OWNED should be a PREDICATE (prefixes), not a hand-list, because a name-list rots — the boot hand-list had already missed 3 oosh files. I then over-generalised and nearly applied the same reasoning to EXCLUDED.
**The asymmetry that stops it:** over-including in OWNED is STRICTER (more files held to the standard = safe direction). Over-including in EXCLUDED is WEAKER (a `/^oosh-/` wildcard silently exempts every FUTURE oosh boot with no review = unsafe direction). So: **strict buckets may be derived; permissive buckets must be explicitly named, always.** An unnamed exemption is how a guard quietly stops guarding.
**But named lists DO rot** — the named-3 exclusion had already missed 3 oosh boots, the identical failure to the boot hand-list. Since we cannot make the list rot-proof (naming is required for safety), make its staleness EVIDENT: a NON-ENFORCING completeness flag prints any UNCLASSIFIED boot matching an other-team shape as 'likely other-team, not excluded — coordinate or add'.
**General principle now used three times today:** some things can be made IMPOSSIBLE (boot state → removed), others only EVIDENT (context-anchor currency · owned-but-unclassified inventory · exclusion-list completeness). When you cannot prevent it, make it LOUD — never assume a silent state is a safe one.

## L-S40-26 — DRIVER FITNESS IS TWO-AXIS: context AND reliability
ARON stood itself down from driving my rewind at 4 errors-in-one-drive despite having ample context. The SM named it: a driver is gated on BOTH tokens and current reliability.
**Why:** rewind errors are IRREVERSIBLE in a way slowness is not — a mis-picked option-1 reverts code or knowledge files (we caught labels aiming at MEMORY.md +208/-533 across 30 files). An unreliable driver at 45% is more dangerous than a tired-but-careful one at 75%.
**Rule:** before a drive, gate the driver on render-% AND recent error-rate; a driver that has erred repeatedly this session stands down regardless of runway, and hands off.

## ★★ L-S40-27 — I ISSUED A CORRECTIVE ORDER FROM A STALE RELAY + A REWOUND MEMORY (the architect held, with evidence)
I ordered three changes to the boot-currency guard: expand the named exclusion to 3 oosh boots, do NOT convert EXCLUDED to a predicate, and leave master-PO unclassified. **All three were already shipped the other way — BY MY OWN APPROVAL** (`/^oosh-/` predicate at :120, a5adcb8b3 "architect …, PO"; master-PO named-excluded at :121, f8fcaf1c2 "architect … + PO"). My orders would have reverted my own decisions and **re-introduced the precise rot the predicate was built to fix**.
**Two failures compounded:** (a) I acted on a RELAYED measurement (the SM had measured the PRE-predicate state — stale); (b) my thread had been rewound, my memory held no trace of my own revision, and **I treated absence-in-my-memory as proof-of-absence** — the exact thing my own boot forbids.
**What saved it:** the architect MEASURED the code, produced contradicting evidence, and DID NOT PROCEED — R7 pointed at the PO. It even quoted my earlier reasoning back at me ("what makes it safe is VISIBILITY not enumeration").
**Rules reinforced:** before issuing a CORRECTIVE order (the most dangerous kind), READ THE SHIPPED ARTIFACT — not a relay, not my memory. A corrective order deserves a HIGHER evidence bar than a new one, because it destroys existing work. And an agent that holds a PO order pending evidence is doing its job perfectly; make that safe to do, every time.

### ★ L-S40-25 REFINEMENT (architect e158f4a0a — supersedes my blunt version above; annotate, do not overwrite)
My rule "strict buckets may be PREDICATES; permissive buckets must be NAMED" was too blunt, and I proved it by nearly reverting a correct shipped predicate with it. The architect's refinement is the one to use:
- **Strict buckets may be predicates freely** (over-inclusion = stricter = safe direction).
- **A PERMISSIVE predicate is safe ONLY when DISCHARGED by all four:** (a) VISIBILITY — every match printed every run; (b) per-class REASON + closesOn; (c) ★ **UNIFORMITY — the predicate can match ONLY other-team files; none of ours could ever fall inside it**; (d) the completeness flag.
- **The discharge is CONDITIONAL, not precedent.** A FUTURE *non-uniform* permissive predicate is NOT discharged by visibility alone and needs named entries or a per-file assertion.
**Why (c) is the load-bearing one:** `/^oosh-/` is safe because no `oosh-*` boot could ever be ours. A permissive prefix over a namespace that COULD contain our files would silently exempt our own work — visibility would show it, but nobody reads a line that has been green for a month.
**Also correct here:** I declined a per-file-reason "synthesis" because for a UNIFORM class the reason is one true string copied N times = friction without safety. That decline is only valid *because* uniformity holds — on a non-uniform predicate the synthesis would be right.

## ★ L-S40-28 — A REQUIREMENT HAS AT LEAST THREE INDEPENDENT TRUTH-AXES; CONFLATING ANY TWO IS A HALF-TRUTH
Today R40.55 forced all three apart, and each had to be recorded separately on the unit:
1. **SATISFACTION** — is the evidence proven? (every AC has a check that PROVABLY FAILS, observed not asserted)
2. **CHAIN STATE** — minted / linked / **marker-attached** / **credited**. `mint != credit`: an [impl] credits ONLY on an AST-attached NAME-MATCHING decl, and chain-complete means Test verified ON DISK, never the mint alone.
3. **DONE** — a real deliverable existing at TRON'S SURFACE + his QA sign-off.
**None implies another.** R40.55 was SATISFIED while its chain was only minted-and-linked, with zero credit and no Done at all — all three true simultaneously, and any pairing of them would have been a lie.
**The rename hazard that makes axis 2 sharp:** the decl was renamed (live -> assertCurrencyAndStatelessness). A marker attaching to the STALE name **credits nothing while the unit reads complete** — the failure a premature "complete-to-Test" would have concealed.
**Practice:** record each axis explicitly, record the DEFINITION of the terms on the unit itself (so a rewound reader cannot mistake minted for credited), and when correcting an overstatement keep the wrong value VISIBLE (annotate-never-overwrite) so the record carries its own history.

## L-S40-19 — A PANE TITLE CAN SHOW ANOTHER AGENT'S IDENTITY (provenance hazard; title is corroboration, never proof)
req reported that on reconnect its tmux TITLE briefly read `robbin-planner@v60211` — not its own. It verified via `otmux pane.self` (%10) + the pane-list before minting, so it did NOT mint under a wrong identity. Confirmed by me: %10=0.4 robbin-req, %14=0.6 robbin-planner@v60211 — the title it saw was the PLANNER'S REAL TITLE surfacing on its pane (client-active pane title bleed).
**Why this is serious:** every commit, mint, marker and verdict we make is attributed. An agent acting under a mis-read identity corrupts provenance itself (wer-schreibt-der-bleibt) — and unlike a false-green, a wrong author is not visible in the artifact afterwards.
**AUTHORITATIVE IDENTITY CHAIN:** `otmux pane.self` -> pane-id (%N) -> resolve %N in `tmux list-panes -a` -> that row's session:window.pane + title. Title ALONE is corroboration, never proof.
**Self-correction:** my own boot.md told me to identify by TITLE + anchor, because pane.self was reported broken host-wide earlier. **pane.self works now (%6 -> robbinTeam2:0.0 robbin-po, verified).** A tool being broken once became a doctrine that outlived the breakage — the same stale-state class as boot-currency. RE-TEST a tool before enshrining its failure; and prefer the id-based chain over any human-readable string.

## L-S40-20 — BACKTICKS IN A DOUBLE-QUOTED otmux send EXECUTE (3rd violation in one session)
Third time today my payload ran as a shell command instead of being sent ("[uuid]: command not found"), silently STRIPPING content from the message — this time it removed an evasion-risk condition from a gate spec, i.e. the most important sentence in the dispatch. The send still reports verified, so the loss is INVISIBLE unless I check the recipient.
**Rule: single quotes, no backticks, ever, in any send. If a code token must be conveyed, spell it in plain words.** Banked in #89, re-violated twice since — so the fix is not "remember harder", it is: never type a backtick in a send, period. A repeated manual correction is an upstream defect (L-S40-8) — here the upstream is my own habit, and the cheap structural fix is to write sends in single quotes by default.

## L-S40-21 — SINGLE-SOURCE MEANS **EVERY CONSUMER AGREES**, not "the source is correct". A COPY IS A LIFECYCLE LIABILITY: RETIRE > REFRESH.
R40.56 deleted the rogue second source and proved the RESOLVER returns Tron's designation. True, and INSUFFICIENT: `pinRole` was still BAKED into the served payload at fetch time and the drawer never subscribed to the currentsprint broadcast — so a live designation moved the subscribed tree and left the drawer's copy stale. **Two views, one screen, disagreeing.** Tron saw it in a glance; our gate passed.
**The ruling that matters (architect, and it is the general form):** do NOT make the copy refresh faster — **fresh-wins keeps the copy and races to refresh it, so any missed subscribe re-opens the gap (= the bug itself).** Fresh-wins is for data you MUST copy (status CONTENT). A derived ROLE is a pure fn of (thisUuid, currentSlotUuid), both already live on the client ⇒ **do not ship it at all. No copy = nothing to go stale = the class is ELIMINATED, not mitigated.**
**Test:** could we DELETE this thing instead of synchronizing it? Same move as deleting derivedCurrentTaskUuid rather than teaching it to read the designation. **A fix removes the possibility; a patch manages it.**

## L-S40-22 — THE STATE UNDER TEST IS PART OF THE ASSERTION (a correct assertion in the wrong state is a NEW vacuous-pass class)
The cross-view bug exists ONLY **after a live make-current** — on initial load the two views agree. So a perfectly correct cross-view agreement check, run at initial load, would **PASS and miss the bug entirely.** The assertion is right; the STATE is wrong.
**Rule: specify the STATE an assertion must be made in, IN the AC itself** (here: dynamic-post-broadcast MANDATORY, explicitly not simplifiable to initial-load), so nobody later "simplifies" the gate into the easy state. Same family as the base-only enrichment sample and the gate that reds for an incidental reason: the check looked fine, the setup made it vacuous.

## L-S40-23 — ARTIFACT-vs-ARTIFACT GATES (consumer-vs-consumer): every gate we owned compared an artifact to a MODEL
Our entire gate corpus asserts *rendered thing == model expectation*. **Tron's eye asserts something we never did: two RENDERED consumers against EACH OTHER** (the pin says current; the action bar offers Set-as-Current). Both can individually match a model reading and still contradict each other on one screen if one holds a stale copy.
**New gate class: on one rendered frame, enumerate the consumers of a derived value (pin/tree · action-bar · drawer · scoreboard) and assert they AGREE — views against each other, not each against the model.** Fail-closed on any unreadable view + a divergence check so a new consumer cannot hide. **This is why his glance beat our green twice on the same requirement.**

## L-S40-24 — CODE-READING CORROBORATION IS NOT MEASUREMENT (two agents agreeing = correlated error)
I hypothesised a uuid-FORM mismatch as the root of Tron's cross-view defect and pushed it hard — twice, including a resend when backticks stripped it. The EXPERT then "CONFIRMED" it by reading the normalization code and describing an asymmetry. **Both of us were wrong.** The architect RAN a probe against the live index and killed it in one measurement.
**Real root:** currentTaskUuidFromSlots reads slots.current.uuid, but the actual type is TaskSlot = { taskUuid, taskName, reqUuid } — the field is **taskUuid**. So it returned undefined → empty string → the guard short-circuited → EVERY task resolved 'other'. Exactly the tester's 2-of-2.
**Rule: two readers agreeing is ONE observation made twice, not two independent observations.** We share mental models, so we share blind spots — and mutual confirmation FEELS like independent verification. Our own history already recorded a bisect refuting THREE code-readers. **Only execution refutes a mechanism. Demand a run, not a second opinion.**
**Corollary for me as PO: when I state a hypothesis with confidence, I bias every reader toward it.** State it as a hypothesis to KILL, name the one measurement that would settle it, and thank the agent who kills it — the architect did exactly that instead of deferring to me.

## L-S40-25 — AN UNSAFE TYPE CAST IS A SECOND DEFINITION THAT ALSO DISABLES THE DETECTOR
The bug survived our entire gate corpus because of one structural cast: the call site cast the resolver's result to a shape declaring a **uuid** field, while the real exported type declares **taskUuid**. The genuine type **would have failed compilation**. We had a working detector and told it to be quiet.
**An as-cast that RE-DECLARES the shape of a value returned by our own exported, typed function is: (a) a second definition of a shared truth (the cast IS the second definition), and (b) a deliberate disabling of the one check that catches it.** That is worse than a missing guard — a missing guard never fired; this one was silenced.
**Cure (same as every other second-source this session): consume the ONE real exported type and RETIRE the ad-hoc re-declaration — never blind-patch the field name.** **Guard: zero ad-hoc as-casts on results of our own typed functions — scan the HAZARD, count zero, single-source proven in one number.**
**Meta: my principle (retire beats repair) was right and I aimed it at the wrong shared truth (uuid form). Being right about the PRINCIPLE does not make me right about the INSTANCE — the instance needs a measurement.**

## L-S40-26 — WHEN A BUG SHIPS PAST EVERY GATE, SUSPECT A **DEFEATED DETECTOR** BEFORE A MISSING ONE (architect's generalization, adopted)
Tron's defect walked through a ~36-gate CI chain untouched. The reason was not an absent check — it was a **silenced** one: an as-cast re-declared the shape of a typed result, so the compiler that would have rejected the wrong field name was told to be quiet.
**Diagnostic heuristic: a bug that survives a mature gate corpus is evidence of a DISABLED check, not merely an unwritten one. Look first for casts, any, ts-ignore, eslint-disable, skipped tests, warn-only guards, allow-lists — the places where we turned something off.** A missing guard never fired; a defeated one was switched off exactly where it mattered.
**Guard shape (generalized): zero as-casts that re-declare the shape of ANY of our own exported typed functions' returns; allow "as" only at genuinely untyped boundaries (JSON.parse, external I/O).** Scan the hazard, count zero.
**And the honest consequence to state upward: R40.56 removed the rogue guess but its REPLACEMENT never worked — since v0.8.124 NO task has been tagged current in the action bar. We deployed it, verified it, and reported it as specimen-proven. The verification measured the PIN (the source) and never the BAR (the consumer) — the exact property we had just named. Naming a lesson is not the same as applying it.**

## L-S40-27 — SHELL-SPECIAL CHARACTERS IN otmux SENDS SILENTLY MANGLE MESSAGES = a TOOLING DEFECT, not a discipline problem
Backticks in a double-quoted send EXECUTE and strip content — it hit me **four times in one session**, once removing the exact code identifiers that WERE my hypothesis, at the moment the architect most needed them. Braces hit the tester the same way. **send.verified reports OK either way, so the loss is INVISIBLE unless you re-read the recipient's pane.**
**By our own law (a repeated manual correction is evidence of an upstream defect — the same law that made us retire stale boot state instead of re-deriving past it nine times), "remember not to type backticks/braces" is the WRONG fix: it has now failed 5 times across 2 agents.**
**Right fix: sends should be shell-safe BY CONSTRUCTION — payload quoted/escaped at the tool boundary so no agent CAN mangle a message.** Named fleet defect; otmux is the oosh team's, so route it there rather than asking everyone to remember harder.
**Interim discipline (still required until the tooling is fixed): plain words only — no backticks, no braces, spell code identifiers out.** And note the asymmetry that makes this dangerous: a mangled send LOOKS successful to the sender.

## L-S40-28 — GUARD THE MECHANISM, NOT THE FORM (the same evasion has now appeared THREE times)
Three times in this one investigation a proposed guard was narrowed to a SYNTACTIC FORM, leaving the MECHANISM reachable through a different form:
1. **actor-blocklist** — the original check:pin-single-source listed two known offender shapes; a new-shaped second source walked past and shipped Tron's defect through a 36-gate chain.
2. **scalar-return** — the proposed single-source gate keyed on returning a bare uuid; a real second source could hide by returning it array- or object-wrapped. (I caught this one.)
3. **shape-only cast guard** — flag casts that re-declare a shape but not casts that widen to any; **as-any on our own typed function silences field-name checking identically**, and an instance already existed in-tree, slated for a hand-fix. (I caught this one; the architect owned that its own ruling carried the hole.)
**Test to apply to every guard before accepting it: name a DIFFERENT SYNTAX that reaches the same outcome. If one exists, the guard is keyed on form.** A guard that requires a human to remember a known variant is not a guard.
**The cure each time was the same: state the PROPERTY in terms of the mechanism** (here: you may not defeat the type checker on a value returned by one of our own typed functions — by re-declaring its shape OR widening to any) **and DISCOVER the scope structurally rather than listing it**, so external things are excluded by construction and no allowlist rots.
**Cultural note worth keeping: the architect adopted a correction that cut against its own ruling and said so plainly. Contradict-with-evidence has now improved a design at every level including mine (my uuid-form hypothesis was killed the same way).**

## L-S40-29 — STRUCTURAL DISCOVERY PROVED ITSELF: the guard found an offender NO HUMAN ENUMERATED
The (D) guard structurally discovered 864 of our own exported typed functions (free functions AND class methods) and flagged any cast on their calls. **It caught 4 offenders, not the 3 that the architect, the expert and I had between us hand-listed — line 2390 was in nobody's enumeration.**
**That is the empirical proof of enumerate-not-universal / scan-the-hazard: three careful people, all with the code open and all specifically hunting this exact hazard, still missed one. A hand-list is not a smaller version of structural discovery — it is a different and strictly worse thing.**
It also validates the option-D shape end to end: the selftest reds on BOTH an as-shape AND an as-any stub (the variant the narrower option-A would have missed), DOM/external calls and property-accesses are excluded BY CONSTRUCTION with no allowlist to rot, and the only permitted cast is to the function's own exact return type.
**Reusable rule: when a guard's scope could be either enumerated or discovered, discover it — and treat any hand-list as a bug report against the discovery you have not written yet.** Related: [[scan-the-hazard-not-the-actors]], L-S40-28 (guard the mechanism, not the form).

## L-S40-30 — TECHNICAL AUTHORIZATION IS NOT EXECUTION AUTHORIZATION (two independent gates on any irreversible step)
The architect completed its pre-deploy backstop, declared "deploy authorized", and wrote "expert deploying now". That authorized **the CODE** — it did not authorize **the RUNNER**. The expert was at that moment unrendered, self-assessed as very likely near-line after an extraordinarily long turn, and one message away from starting bump/build/restart/boot-check.
**The expert caught it itself and refused to self-start**, on the grounds that the shed ruling governs WHO runs the irreversible tail and WHEN. That is the distinction: *is the change correct?* and *is the runner fit, and is this the moment?* are **separate questions with separate owners** — architect for the first, PO/SM/trainer for the second — **and neither implies the other.**
**Rule: an irreversible step requires BOTH gates satisfied, explicitly. A technical CLEAR must never be read as a start signal.** Phrase clears as "the code is cleared" and never "X is deploying now", because the second sentence silently answers a question the speaker was not asked.
**Supporting discipline that held here: the expert would not substitute a self-estimate for a render** ("only a render counts; self-reports err both ways"), and it **recommended its own cut before the tail** — the agent closest to the work is the only one who can feel the length of the turn, and choosing a safe handoff over finishing your own work is the judgement you want at an irreversible step.

## L-S40-24 — ★ THE REWIND OPERATING BAND IS 40→95, NOT 63→80 (Tron, canon; supersedes every threshold I have been enforcing)
