**Verbatim: "80 = ALARM not rewind; LATEST rewind at 95%; a working space 63-80 is NOTHING = pure overhead producing nothing; it needs 95→40 after phase-2."**
- **80 = ALARM + SAVE (write-as-you-go, stay recoverable). NOT a rewind trigger.**
- **95 = the LATEST rewind. Then ONE deep phase-2 cut to ~40.** ⇒ **55 points of real work per cycle.**
**WHAT I WAS DOING WRONG, measured across our own cascade:** we cut at 69-80 and landed at 43-63 = **~20-point working windows**. Six agents shed in one cascade. **We were rewinding, not working** — I read the frequent cuts as good hygiene when they were the thrash itself. I enforced flag-at-75/cut-at-80 on the whole fleet and called it discipline.
**SECOND-ORDER BENEFIT I did not see until Tron's correction:** the trainer has now caught ~9 LYING rewind labels, nearly all aimed at learnings.md/MEMORY.md, one of them MEMORY.md +77/-686 across 25 canon files. **Every rewind is an exposure to that. Cutting 3x less often is also 3x less chance of the one mis-pick that deletes the canon.** The band is a SAFETY property, not just an efficiency one.
**MY OWN EXCEPTION (Tron):** my ~322k front-loaded Read floor means a rewind bottoms out near 48 and can never reach 40 — so **re-cutting me to 63 forever is the WORST thrash. My tool is /compact + the lean-read fix (tight grep/head, never re-read whole files), NOT another rewind.** Fix the intake, do not keep cutting the output.
**GENERAL FORM:** a threshold that feels prudent can be the waste. Measure the WORKING WINDOW a policy actually yields (band size = work per cycle), not just the risk it avoids. [[rewind-operating-band-40-to-95-not-63-to-80]]

## L-S40-25 — ★ CORRECTION TO L-S40-18: the push classifier is SHAPE-DEPENDENT, not "intermittent"
I banked earlier that the classifier block was INTERMITTENT ("refuse, retry later, it clears"). **That explanation was WRONG even though the outcome was right.** The tester disclosed the actual mechanism when it pushed the 448 session commits: its FIRST attempt was a **COMPOUND command** (git push PIPED to grep, plus an echo of the exit code) and was BLOCKED; the **BARE** form — git push origin main, no pipe, no redirect, no chaining — went straight through. Same push, same auth, same moment. Not a force, not a split, not a history rewrite.
**⇒ The rule is deterministic and actionable: push with a BARE command. Compound/piped/redirected shapes trip the classifier.** "Intermittent" led to *retry and hope*; "shape-dependent" leads to *use the right form* — a reliable fix instead of a superstition.
**WHY I GOT IT WRONG:** the first refusal and the later success were both real, so "it varies" fit the data. I inferred a property of the SYSTEM from two samples without varying anything deliberately — the same error as reading a pane render once and declaring an agent down. **Two observations with an uncontrolled variable is not evidence of randomness; it is an unmeasured difference.**
**WHY WE CAUGHT IT:** the tester reported HOW it succeeded, not just THAT it succeeded. A bare "retried, it worked" would have CONFIRMED my wrong theory and hardened it into canon. **Reporting the mechanism, not just the outcome, is what makes a success auditable.**

## L-S40-26 — ★★ THE TESTER GUARANTEES IT WORKS; TRON JUDGES A WORKING THING. HE IS NOT THE TESTER. (2nd correction, same theme)
**Tron, emphatic: "I AM NOT YOUR TESTER — the TESTER must GUARANTEE it working for QA."**
I said "your tap is the acceptance" dozens of times believing it was deference to his authority. **It was offloading DISCOVERY to him.** The distinction I kept collapsing:
- **GUARANTEE (tester's job):** the feature WORKS on the REAL device surface he uses (@390, served prod, all findings), and the tester STANDS BEHIND it.
- **QA JUDGMENT (his job):** is this RIGHT, is it GOOD ENOUGH, is it what I wanted — a judgment on a **guaranteed-working** feature.
**If the tester cannot guarantee it @390, it is NOT QA-ready and IT DOES NOT GO TO HIM AT ALL.** "Awaiting Tron's device-verify" is a category error — it means we shipped him something we hadn't finished verifying.
**PROOF IT MATTERS:** every finding he screenshotted was a tester gate that had gone GREEN without guaranteeing the DEVICE surface — consumer-level/scratch-level green is NOT a device guarantee. That is why his glance beat our green three times.
**REFRAME ALL STATUS LANGUAGE — two states only: "tester GUARANTEES @390" or "NOT-YET-GUARANTEED".** Never "deployed, awaiting his tap".
**GENERAL FORM:** when a stakeholder's authority is used to justify handing them unfinished verification, "respecting their authority" has become an excuse. Ask: am I giving them a DECISION, or am I giving them my WORK?

## L-S40-27 — ★★ I PUSHED A VALUE WHERE I SHOULD HAVE PUSHED A REF — my coordination error is the SAME SHAPE as the product defect we are fixing
I dispatched the expert with an explicit uuid-to-file mapping and **I had the two uuids SWAPPED** (I said f5928a3b→bare-uuid.ts and 3d1b5df8→drawer; the truth is the reverse). The expert did NOT trust me: each Impl unit **embeds its own sourceFile + sourceLine + ownerIor**, so it RE-DERIVED both from disk and placed them correctly, then flagged my swap so nobody re-places off it.
**THE CONVERGENCE, and it is exact:** R37.24's consumer-property says *a subscriber must treat the event payload as a REF to re-derive from, never paint the pushed VALUE.* **I painted a pushed value.** I sent a derived mapping (uuid→file) instead of the ref (the DECL NAME) that lets the agent re-derive from the self-describing unit. My mapping was a stale copy in exactly the sense the product bug was a stale copy.
**RULE:** in dispatches, name the **DECL (function name) + the requirement**, and let the agent resolve the uuid from the unit on disk. **Do not hand agents derived mappings from my memory — my memory is a copy, and copies go stale or get swapped.** If I must cite a uuid, cite it as a hint to VERIFY, never as an instruction to place.
**THIRD uuid-identity error of mine** (task-vs-impl conflation → 8-char prefix ambiguity → now a swap). Three manual corrections of the same class = an upstream defect in MY process, not three slips. [[full-uuid-data-writes-and-prefix-negative-conclusions]]
**WHAT SAVED IT:** the pre-place verify I had mandated (uuid resolves to an Implementation AND the name-token matches the decl). **A guard I imposed on others caught me.** That is what a guard is for — it does not know who wrote the instruction.

## L-S40-28 — ★★★ THE CAMPAIGN'S CENTRAL FAILURE: WE KEEP GATING A WEAKER PROPERTY THAN THE ONE THAT MATTERS (4 instances, one shape)
Every green we had to walk back had the same structure: **the assertion was true, and it measured something adjacent to what was actually required.**
1. **R40.56 — SOURCE instead of AGREEMENT.** We proved the resolver returns Tron's designation. GREEN. He glanced at one screen and saw two views disagree.
2. **R40.57 — DERIVE-AT-RENDER instead of AGREEMENT.** Green on the clean open-AFTER flow; his real sequence (drawer open BEFORE, then tap) still RED. The green would have masked a genuinely incomplete fix **right before he judged it.**
3. **RESOLVER/SCRATCH-GREEN instead of a DEVICE GUARANTEE.** Every finding he screenshotted was a gate green that never guaranteed the surface he uses.
4. **CONSISTENCY instead of LIVENESS.** The board audit checks checklist-vs-chain agreement; his complaint was that statuses do not MOVE while agents work. A board can be perfectly consistent and still be a dead picture.
**THE TEST THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT ALL FOUR — ask before accepting any green: "WHAT EXACTLY DOES THIS ASSERT, AND IS IT THE PROPERTY THE USER EXPERIENCES?"** Not "did it pass". Not "is the assertion correct". **Adjacent-and-true is the most dangerous result we produce, because it stops us looking.** A RED sends us back; a true-but-adjacent GREEN sends us to Tron.
**WHY IT KEEPS HAPPENING:** the weaker property is always the one that is EASIER TO INSTRUMENT — a resolver return value, a scratch harness, a data consistency check. The real property is usually the RENDERED, DYNAMIC, USER-SEQUENCE one, which is harder to stage. **We drift toward what is measurable, not what is required.**
**COUNTERMEASURE, now standing:** state the property in the USER'S terms first ("after HE taps, the two things he can see agree"), THEN design the instrument to that — never the reverse. If the instrument cannot reach the property, the honest output is NOT-GUARANTEED, never a green on the reachable subset.

## L-S40-29 — ★★ AN INFERENCE REPORTED AS A MEASUREMENT (both of us did it) — and DON'T replace inference A with inference B and call it settled
The planner reported the dead board as "MEASURED on disk / PROVABLE": board rows do not subscribe to per-task status. **It had read the CONTAINER's subscribe list statically and never opened the ROW component. No browser, no runtime, no observation.** The architect checked the row: rb-object-item ALREADY subscribes per-ref on render, keys match via the same builder ⇒ **"add subscribe-on-render" would have been a NO-OP that greens against "badges update" while Tron's board stayed frozen.**
**MY PART, which is the larger one: I promoted its inference to DECISIVE, built the strategy on it, and told Tron it was "provable on disk".** I amplified an unverified reading into a plan and into a claim to the user. **An agent's careful inference plus my endorsement does not equal a measurement.**
**THE PLANNER'S OWN DIAGNOSIS IS THE RULE:** report **"inferred from X, Y unverified"**, never an inference in measurement's clothing. It named that itself, unprompted, which is why its reporting stays trustworthy.
**★ THE SUBTLER TRAP I ALMOST FELL INTO NEXT: the architect's contradicting account is ALSO A STATIC CODE-READING.** Better-informed (it read the component the planner missed) but still inference. **Replacing inference A with inference B and calling the question settled is the same error one layer along.** The only thing that settles it is a RUNTIME observation: seam-tick a REGULAR task and watch whether its badge updates @390 with no reload. **Ordered.**
**GENERAL FORM: when two accounts conflict, do not adjudicate by whose reasoning is better — find the observation that discriminates them.**

## L-S40-30 — ★★ THE ARTIFACT-DRIFT FAMILY: a correct reader reasons faithfully from a drifted artifact and is WRONG about the system (3 instances)
1. **A boot file naming a FINISHED sprint** (R40.55) — every agent woke into a dead world and hand-corrected its way out.
2. **A gate grepping a RETIRED code shape** (r4017) — RED on a legitimate refactor while the behaviour was fine.
3. **A comment stating a constraint the code does NOT hold** (CurrentSprint.ts:296 accepts anything not-Done; the comment and two of our ACs say Planned/In-Progress/QA-Review only).
**THE COMMON DANGER — and it explains the whole campaign's pattern of confident wrong answers:** these do not fool careless readers. **They fool CAREFUL ones.** A diligent agent reads the artifact, reasons correctly from it, and arrives at a false model of the system. That is exactly how the planner's container-subscribe reading went astray, and why four of us in a row reported inferences as measurements — **we were each reasoning faithfully from something that had drifted.**
**CURE (candidate AC): a stated constraint is either ENFORCED IN CODE, or it is an unheld INTENT and must be VISIBLE as such — never silent.** Same shape as the boot cure (remove the state so it cannot rot) and the gate cure (judge on behaviour, demote the source-grep to informational).
**AND THE PO-LEVEL COROLLARY:** when a doc and the code disagree, **narrowing the code and widening the doc are DIFFERENT PRODUCT DECISIONS.** Aligning them quietly makes a behaviour change look like a tidy-up. Route it for a ruling; never silently pick.

## L-S40-31 — ★ READING A RETIRED FIELD RETURNS undefined, WHICH READS AS A LEGITIMATE NEGATIVE (2 instances, both self-caught)
Twice in one investigation an agent probed a field that no longer exists and got undefined, then reported it as a real value:
- **Expert:** read .uuid where it needed .taskUuid → false-negative in its own repro (caught it itself before reporting).
- **Tester:** read a RETIRED pinRole/.taskUuid on /api/trace/children children (they carry .uuid post-D3) → reported **"endpoint returns none"**, which drove an entire wrong-culprit investigation, an inverted design §94, and hours of reconciliation. It re-measured and RETRACTED.
**THE HAZARD: a missing field and a genuinely empty value are INDISTINGUISHABLE in the result. "none" can mean the system said none, or that you asked the wrong question.** This is a false-NEGATIVE generator, and it is especially dangerous because a negative finding feels like diligence — you think you have found a bug.
**RULE: when a probe returns a negative/empty, FIRST prove the field EXISTS on that object** (dump the actual key set, as the tester finally did: uuid,type,name,hasChildren,status,pinSlot,role). Only then is the emptiness a finding. Applies to any absence-based conclusion — [[distrust-your-own-negative-reverify]].
**WHAT WORKED: both agents caught their OWN false-read and retracted publicly.** The tester retracted the cause of its own RED — the hardest correction to make, because the RED itself was real and only its explanation was wrong. **Keeping the finding while retracting the mechanism is the precise move.**

## L-S40-32 — ★ DEEP-CUT NAVIGATION: era-stamps LIE, read the CONTENT; deep means shed RECENT WORKING BULK, never era-jump
First in-band deep phase-2 (tester): **84% → 37.3% = ~46 points freed**, landing in Tron's ~40 target — versus the **13-16 points** our shallow 69-80 cuts were yielding. **Empirical proof the 40→95 band is right: one deep cut ≈ three shallow ones, at one third of the lying-label exposure.**
**THE NAVIGATION SKILL (trainer): go DEEP past the shallow boundary, but STOP BEFORE an era-jump checkpoint.** The checkpoint **AGE STAMPS LIED** ("3d" / "1mo"); it read the **CONTENT** to place them. **Deep-to-40 = shedding the recent WORKING bulk. An era-jump = landing in a different sprint/world = manufacturing ghost-context on purpose.** Those are opposite outcomes from the same menu.
**⇒ ANOTHER ARTIFACT-DRIFT INSTANCE (4th): the rewind picker's own age labels misdescribe the checkpoints they name** — same family as the stale boot state, the stale gate grep, and the stale design comment. **Read the content, never the label.** That is now true of the option text (lying diffs), the age stamps, AND the target selection.
**★ AND THE BIGGEST CANON NEAR-MISS YET: option-1 on this cut = learnings.md +153/-1414 across 23 files.** ~10 lying labels caught across the campaign, nearly all aimed at the knowledge files. **The structural protection is what actually saves us now: the canon is PUSHED TO ORIGIN, so a mis-pick is recoverable rather than fatal.** Vigilance caught 10 of 10; the push means the 11th does not have to be caught.
**ALSO VALIDATED: cut on WORK-QUALITY signals, not just percentage.** This cut was called partly because a reader function had been mangled by repeated in-place patching — a degraded workspace. A fresh instance rewriting one line cleanly beats a loaded one patching it a sixth time.

## L-S40-33 — ★ A GOVERNANCE FACT GOES STALE EXACTLY LIKE CODE DOES (and we never check it)
The architect wrote "Tron's A/B on the restore is still unanswered" **three times** — after Tron had answered **A**. Each repetition risked stalling a restore he had already approved. Its own diagnosis, which is the valuable part: **"I carried a stale governance FACT as a standing caveat and kept appending it without re-checking currency — the exact stale-read failure I have been rigorous about on CODE but not on a governance fact."**
**THE CLASS:** we apply measure-not-assume ruthlessly to code, disk, served state, and context numbers — and then carry **decisions, authorisations, scope rulings and blockers** forward as standing caveats that nobody re-measures. **A decision has currency. "Blocked on X" is a measurement, and it expires.**
**Same family as the whole campaign:** stale boot files, a gate grepping a retired code shape, a comment misdescribing its code, a git-fetch reporting up-to-date on a stale tree. **This is the same disease applied to the DECISION LAYER instead of the code layer.**
**RULE: before repeating "blocked on / awaiting / not yet authorised", RE-CHECK IT.** Cheapest version: cite WHERE the block is recorded and WHEN it was last confirmed. A caveat with no timestamp is an assumption wearing a fact's clothes.
**And on my side:** I had to correct it THREE times. **A correction that does not stick is a signal the artifact carrying it was never updated** — the fix was its ANCHOR, not another message from me. Correct the record, not just the reader.

## L-S40-29 — A GATE THAT ASSERTS SOURCE SHAPE ROTS; A GATE THAT ASSERTS BEHAVIOUR DOES NOT (the FALSE-RED mirror)
The tester found r4017-live-pin-norefresh **OVERALL-RED on served v0.8.126 — on a STALE SOURCE-GREP.** It expects the retired raw-uuid subscribe; the code has legitimately used viewBusKey since R37.12/R40.17. **The BEHAVIOUR is GREEN** (live pin swap, no reload, corroborated independently by the agreement gate seeing the pin re-render post-broadcast).
**This is a FALSE-RED — the exact mirror of the false-GREENs that have burned us this campaign, and just as corrosive: a gate that reds for a retired shape trains the team to ignore it, and an ignored gate is a dead gate.** A false-green lies that things are fine; a false-red destroys the signal value of every red.
**ROOT: the gate asserted SOURCE SHAPE (does this symbol appear) instead of BEHAVIOUR (does the pin move without a reload).** Source shape changes legitimately at every refactor/rename; behaviour is the thing the requirement actually claims.
**RULE: retarget it to the BEHAVIOURAL assertion — do NOT merely update the grep to the new symbol, or it rots again at the next rename.** Same family as: hand-list gates rot (check:pin-single-source), name-lists rot (boot hand-list), and enumerated KEEP sets rot (R37.24 chose a PROPERTY instead). **Assert what must be TRUE, never what the code currently LOOKS LIKE.**
**COROLLARY on cadence:** the tester asked for a cut at ~59% ("context DEEP"). Under Tron's corrected band (40-95, alarm 80) that is MID-BAND. **The old 63-80 instinct is still firing in the fleet and I must keep correcting it — cutting at 59 is the thrash itself.**

## L-S40-30 — /compact is FORBIDDEN (Tron: "NO /compact never!!!!!!"); forks need his explicit confirmation
I had banked and repeatedly stated my read-floor remedy as "/compact, not a rewind". **Tron forbade /compact outright today, and forks require his explicit confirmation.** My levers are now: **(a) rewind and accept the ~54 floor, or (b) a fork ONLY with his confirmation.** Corrected everywhere I stated it; do not plan a forbidden action.
**The deeper fix is still mine and unchanged:** the floor exists because ~320k of my context is my own over-wide reading (Read + Bash results). **Tight grep/head, never re-read whole files, never cat a large file** — that lowers the floor itself, which no remedy can do for me.
**Process note:** the SM flagged this as a FACT I might not have (a Tron ruling I missed while rewound), explicitly not deciding my remedy. That is the right shape for a monitor contradicting the PO — bring the ruling, leave the choice.

## L-S40-31 — SELF-ESTIMATES RUN **LOW**, AND THE ERROR IS DIRECTIONAL (3 instances, up to 44 points)
Panel-vs-self this campaign: **SM self-est ~38, panel 82 (−44)** · trainer self-est ~72, panel 82 (−10) · **me: I relayed ~46-50 all session while actually at 82 (−32)**. Three agents, three underestimates, **zero overestimates**.
**⇒ The bias has a direction: an agent FEELS SAFE PRECISELY WHEN IT IS NOT.** A random error would sometimes over-report; this never does. So "I think I'm fine" is not weak evidence — it is *systematically* wrong in the dangerous direction.
**RULE: never report your own estimate as a number. Panel-measure, or say "unmeasured".** And whoever ACTS measures the target immediately before acting (driver-renders-target) — a relayed or remembered number is a hint, never an input.
**THE STRUCTURAL POINT:** the SM applies rigorous measurement to everyone else and was estimating ITSELF. **The exemption an agent makes for itself is the gap** — same shape as my own: I enforced context discipline fleet-wide while mis-reporting my own by 32 points. **The watcher must be watched, including by itself (care-chain must be a CYCLE, not a line).**
**Corroborating cost:** acting on a relayed number errs BOTH ways — it nearly shed a healthy req at a ghost-83 (real 24) and it walled the architect at 1%. Direction of the error is incidental; **acting on an unmeasured number is the defect.**

## L-S40-32 — THE BOOT-CURRENCY GUARD HAS A BLIND SIDE: deprecated STATE files are outside its scan
ARON nearly false-flagged a trainer Phase-1 gap by reading **session/agents/agent-trainer/context.md — DEPRECATED and 2 MONTHS STALE** — while the live state is a PER-HOST anchor its boot.md correctly points to. Verify-not-trust saved it.
**THE HOLE:** we built the boot-currency lint to make ghost-context structurally impossible. **It globs boot.md and asserts currency. A deprecated context.md is not a boot.md ⇒ outside the scan.** The guard does not cover the file that nearly caused the exact failure it exists to prevent.
**SAME SHAPE AS EVERY OTHER GUARD FAILURE THIS CAMPAIGN:** check:pin-single-source blocklisted known actors while a new-shaped source walked past · the boot hand-list missed 3 oosh boots until a glob+divergence check replaced it · r4017 asserted source shape and rotted. **A guard scoped to a FILE TYPE (or a name list) leaves everything adjacent uncovered.**
**FIX:** remove deprecated state files, or mark them unmistakably with a header naming the live anchor, **AND extend the currency scan to agent STATE files, not just boot.md** — plus a divergence check so a new file kind cannot hide. **Sweep for siblings: one stale file per agent is one ghost-context landmine per agent.**
**THE META-RULE THIS KEEPS PROVING:** every time vigilance catches something, ask *what structure would have made the catch unnecessary* — and check whether the guard we already built covers this case or merely resembles covering it.

## L-S40-33 — ★★★ A GATE THAT **MOCKS THE PATH UNDER TEST** IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND (worst false-green class found)
r4017-live-pin-norefresh was GREEN. Measure-first showed why: **page.route served the CurrentSprint body and the test called renderCurrentSprintEagerLazy() DIRECTLY — it mocked away the exact make-current → notify → re-fetch path where the race lives.**
⇒ **It could never fail on that path's defect. Not weak — BLIND. And it greens forever regardless of how broken the product is.**
**HOW THIS DIFFERS FROM EVERY OTHER FALSE-GREEN WE FOUND:** the others measured a real-but-ADJACENT property (source not agreement, resolver not device, consistency not liveness). **This one measured a SIMULATION of the property.** Adjacent-and-true at least touches reality; a mock of the mechanism touches nothing.
**THE DISCRIMINATOR:** mocking an IRRELEVANT dependency is legitimate and necessary. **Mocking the MECHANISM UNDER ASSERTION is a structural blind spot.** Ask of every gate: *does it exercise the real path, or a stand-in for the real path?*
**THE RETARGET SHAPE THAT WORKS (now our standard for racy behaviour):** real owner path · N iterations · **TRANSITION-checked** (the value must CHANGE to one it was not already showing — kills the vacuous OK) · a PRECONDITION that the server actually moved · FAIL on any valid miss inside a bound · and **EXCLUDE unscoreable runs rather than scoring them** (vacuous-skip / precond-fail), which is what makes a RED trustworthy on a non-deterministic defect.
**THE OPEN EXPOSURE:** every green in our suite is currently UNAUDITED against this failure mode. One gate was proven worthless while reading GREEN; the count of others is unknown. **A clean sweep is itself evidence we do not yet have.**

## L-S40-34 — I SEARCHED A STRING AND REPORTED IT AS A TYPE (my measurement error; 5 methods refuted 1)
I ran `grep -rl "ior:class:ChangeRequest"` and treated every hit as a ChangeRequest UNIT. **grep -l returns files that MENTION the string** — a Task referencing a CR, and the `declineToChangeRequest` Impl, both match. **So my "8 CRs with 4 different parents" was files that MENTION a CR, not files that ARE one.** I then halted the expert's backfill on that bad reading and told Tron the render might be structurally wrong.
**THE TRUTH (expert, FIVE agreeing methods — ScenarioIndex probe ×2, /api/trace/children both modes, WebKit DOM, raw-file grep): all 5 T40.1 CRs genuinely have ownerIor → Test c4f8a1d6. The render was FAITHFUL.**
**RULES:** (1) **A grep for a string is not a query for a type** — filter on the unit's OWN `ior` field (what it IS), never on a string it may merely reference. (2) **One method is a hypothesis; N agreeing methods is a measurement** — I refuted a 5-method result with a single mis-scoped grep and acted on it. (3) **The cost of a wrong measurement is not just the error — it is the WORK IT HALTS.** I stopped a correct build and pointed Tron at a defect that did not exist.
**WHAT WORKED:** the expert did not defer to the PO's data. It re-measured, refuted me with evidence, and named my exact mistake. **An agent that overturns my measurement with method is worth more than one that complies with it.**

## L-S40-35 — ★ A DIFF SHOWS POSITIONAL CHANGE, NOT SEMANTIC CHANGE (I escalated a false identity-corruption alarm to Tron)
The expert's diff of 8 room units showed lines reading **"Marcel Donges" → "" and "Marcel Donges" → "Amos Donges"**. I read that as **identity conflation between two real people**, promoted it above every other item, and **told Tron his own identity might be corrupted in his rooms and getting worse on every boot.**
**REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT:** member-token **SET-EQUALITY**, HEAD vs working tree — **identical**. 11 tokens present in both, including Marcel (05e58f81), Amos (8d9be587), c09087ec. **Nothing added, removed, or replaced.** The apparent replacement was **ARRAY REORDERING** — the members Map iterates in a different order than the committed array, so a line-diff pairs unrelated entries and prints them as a substitution.
**RULE: a line-diff of an ORDERED serialization of an UNORDERED collection manufactures fake substitutions. Compare SETS/identities, never diff lines, when asking "did the membership change?"** Real remaining bugs are minor and different: createdAt mutated on re-serialize (should be immutable), display-name blanking when a token has no runtime name, unstable ordering.
**MY SECOND MEASUREMENT ERROR IN THE SAME STRETCH — same root:** I grepped a STRING and reported a TYPE (L-S40-34); here I read a DIFF and reported SEMANTICS. **Both times I inferred meaning from a textual artifact instead of querying the thing itself. Both times an agent refuted me with a proper method.**
**AND THE COST IS ASYMMETRIC WHEN IT REACHES TRON:** a false alarm to him spends his attention and his trust in our reporting — the same currency a false-green spends. **Escalate measured facts; label inferences as inferences.** I should have said "the diff SUGGESTS a name change — verifying by set-comparison" and verified BEFORE promoting it above everything else.

## L-S40-27 — ★ REWIND DEPTH: TARGET THE **BOOT**, NOT A PERCENTAGE (a number target is unachievable by construction)
The trainer drove the expert 75% → **16%** while aiming for my stated ~40. Its honest report contains the general lesson: **freed-% is NOT measurable per-checkpoint BEFORE firing**, so a numeric depth target is an illusion — you cannot steer to a number you cannot read until after you commit.
**What IS controllable is STRUCTURAL: the checkpoint's RELATION TO THE AGENT'S LAST BOOT.** Landing **just above the boot** is deterministic (the boot is a real, findable point), it is exactly where old bulk stops being reintroduced, and whatever % results is fine. 16% here = doctrine-ideal, 83% runway for a long build.
**⇒ RULE (replaces "aim for ~40"): pick the checkpoint that lands JUST ABOVE the last boot.** A number target invites overshoot AND — far worse — undershoot, which is the 63% thrash Tron corrected (cut at 69-80, land 43-63, twenty-point windows, six agents shed per cascade).
**ASYMMETRY THAT MAKES THE DIRECTION EASY:** erring DEEP costs one re-derive from a comprehensive anchor. Erring SHALLOW costs another whole cut plus the thrash. **When a target is unmeasurable, choose the error direction whose downside is cheap — and say plainly which way you erred.**
**GENERAL FORM:** if you cannot measure the quantity you are steering to, stop steering to the quantity. Find the STRUCTURAL landmark that correlates with it and steer to that. (Same shape as: gate the HAZARD not the actors · derive the KEEP set from a property not a list · assert the RENDERED artifact not a proxy.)

## L-S40-28 [CORRECTED BY MEASUREMENT — see the correction block at the end of this entry] — a gate that cannot terminate has no RED
The SM flagged the tester "idle ~20min, 1 shell". I measured the PROCESS from outside instead of asking: **node r4060-pin-rerender-observer-gate.mjs had been running 11,614s ≈ 3.2 HOURS** with a headless WebKit held open the whole time. A @390 gate is a minutes-scale job ⇒ **a HANG, not a long run** — and it predated the rewind, so a fresh agent inherited a stuck process it had no reason to suspect.
**THE CLASS:** an observer/liveness gate that WAITS FOR AN EVENT has **no failure mode when the event never arrives** — it waits forever instead of going RED. By our own R40.54 rule (no AC without a check that can PROVABLY FAIL) **that is not a gate at all.** It is the can-never-fail gate again, in a new costume: previously exitCode=0 always; now "never returns".
**RULE: every waiting/observing gate needs a TIMEOUT that converts 'the event never arrived' into a RED. Silence must be a FAILURE, never a wait.**
**SECOND, SHARPER POINT — detection:** a hung gate is INDISTINGUISHABLE from a slow one until someone measures the process. It burned ~3h of the critical path unnoticed because the SM was watching the AGENT TURN (~20min idle) while the SHELL had been stuck far longer. **The monitored signal and the failing thing were different objects.** ⇒ **A gate must ANNOUNCE its own failure; it must never depend on a human noticing silence.**
**METHOD THAT FOUND IT:** ps from OUTSIDE the agent — pane content showed only stale scrollback and would have told me nothing. Same family as: never declare an agent down from pane render alone; measure with a DIFFERENT instrument than the one raising (or hiding) the alarm.

### ★★ CORRECTION TO L-S40-28 (the tester measured; I had inferred) — THE REAL CLASS IS **NON-TERMINATION HAS NO RED**
**My hypothesis was WRONG:** I said "an observer waiting for an event that never arrived". The tester DISPROVED it — the watch-loop IS bounded (10s → falls through to a RED discriminator) and a background copy of the SAME gate ran GREEN.
**THE ACTUAL MECHANISM, worse and more insidious:** a **LEAKED BROWSER HANDLE**. `finally` called teardown but never closed the browser; an open WebKit handle keeps node's event loop alive; there was no `process.exit()` and no watchdog. So a **logically-FINISHED gate never EXITED — it sat 3.2h AFTER deciding.** The gate had already reached its verdict and simply never announced it.
**⇒ CLASS = NON-TERMINATION HAS NO RED.** We had bounded WAITS everywhere and bounded TERMINATION nowhere. Any leaked handle (browser/server/WS) or any unbounded `fetch()` (undici has NO default timeout) makes a gate hang silently with no failure signal.
**THE DAMNING NUMBER (tester's survey of ~40 gates): ZERO have a process-level watchdog**, several also carry unbounded fetch. **This is not a bug we fixed — it is a hole under the ENTIRE gate corpus.** Any green we ever took could in principle have been a gate that decided and then hung, read by us as "still running".
**FIX PATTERN — now a COMPLETION CONDITION for every new gate, same standing as stub-must-fail:** (1) hard watchdog → `RED: WATCHDOG` + `process.exit(1)`; (2) `finally` ALWAYS closes the browser; (3) explicit `process.exit()` to force drain; (4) `AbortSignal.timeout()` on every node fetch.
**SEQUENCING RULING (so hardening does not eat the critical path):** apply it to the gates the current guarantee run actually executes; the remaining ~35 are a **SEPARATE NAMED sweep AFTER** it. Name the backlog, do not retrofit 40 gates in front of Tron's item-1.
**META:** I inferred a mechanism from a symptom and stated it confidently; the tester measured and corrected it. **Both my "fix" and my class-name would have been wrong in the record.** Label inferences as inferences — and prize the agent that overturns them.

## L-S40-29 — ★ A READINESS SIGNAL MUST BE THE **LAST** THING THAT RENDERS (a wait on an early proxy times a half-built DOM)
The tester's detail-capture was unstable: band+sub-step present in one run, empty in another. **It DIAGNOSED instead of re-capturing** (re-running until a capture agrees is fitting the test to the answer) and found the cause was its OWN instrument: its load signal **fired on title+status BEFORE the checklist painted**. So it was timing a partially-built DOM and reading the gap as a missing feature.
**RULE: a readiness condition must key on the LAST thing to render, never an early one — and never a sleep.** Open once + waitForFunction on that terminal signal. This is **assert-the-artifact-not-a-proxy applied to the WAIT condition**: an early signal is a proxy for readiness, not readiness.
**CONSEQUENCE:** with the race removed BOTH guarantee halves held; the "instability" was the instrument, not the feature. **An unstable measurement is a finding to DIAGNOSE, not noise to average out.**
**★ THE PART I RATE HIGHEST — it concluded AGAINST my framing.** I offered (i) method-race and (ii) content-vanishes as equal options but said (ii) would "outrank everything else", which is a thumb on the scale. It measured its way to (i) and **retracted its own earlier finding** — its second self-correction of the run. **An agent that lands where the PO seemed to be steering is worth far less than one that measures its way elsewhere.** When I frame options, I must not signal which one I expect.

## L-S40-30 — ★★ A GUARD HAS THREE FAILURE MODES: **EXISTS? WIRED? COVERS-THE-CLASS?** All three, or the green is hollow. (tester's formulation — now fleet doctrine)
We have been bitten by each mode SEPARATELY, which is why no single fix caught the pattern:
- **check:pin-single-source** — existed ✓ wired ✓ but **covered a HAND-LIST of known actors** ⇒ Tron's original defect (a new-shaped 2nd source) walked straight past it GREEN.
- **check-detail-primitive** — existed ✓ but **NOT WIRED** into ci:gates ⇒ one-shot, not by-construction; a future good-faith edit ships unguarded. (Architect elevated wiring to a completion gate, overriding my earlier "not yet".)
- **check-status-symbol** — exists ✓, **unwired** ✗, **AND under-covers** ✗: run manually it **PASSES (exit 0) WHILE the band-glyph defect stands**, because it checks only the SERVER glyph table, never the CLIENT BADGE_MAP, never the band status, and its 2nd-vocabulary detector is **report-only** and missed BADGE_MAP entirely.
**★ THE WORST VARIANT is the third: a guard that passes green while the defect it is NAMED FOR stands. It does not merely fail to catch — it CERTIFIES.** That is worse than having no guard, because the board reads it as coverage.
**RULE: for every guard, answer all three questions explicitly. And require the strongest available evidence: the guard must go RED on the LIVE defect that motivated it, BEFORE the fix lands.** A lint that cannot red on its own motivating defect is the wrong lint. (Same discipline as the elimination's RED-baseline-on-the-live-specimen.)
**PROCESS NOTE:** the tester found this by INDEPENDENTLY VERIFYING the expert's flag instead of relaying it — and found it worse than reported. **Relaying a peer's finding propagates it; verifying it can deepen it.**

## L-S40-31 — ★ NEVER READ AN EXIT CODE THROUGH A PIPE (I nearly filed a false accusation against a correct gate)
I ran the tester's new lint to verify it myself. Output showed **"✗ RED"** and **EXIT=0** — and I was one step from reporting "your lint prints red but exits zero", which would have been a **false accusation** AND would have sent the tester chasing a bug that does not exist. **Cause: I piped to `tail`, so `$?` was TAIL'S exit code, not node's.** Re-running without the pipe: **exit 1. The lint was correct all along.**
**I measured a PROXY (the pipeline's exit) instead of the ARTIFACT (the process's exit)** — the identical class this entire campaign has been curing, committed by me while verifying someone else's work against exactly that class.
**RULES:** never read an exit code through a pipe — run the command bare and check `$?`, or capture output to a file. **And before accusing a gate of a defect, re-measure with a different instrument** — the cost of a false accusation is not just noise, it is a peer's time spent hunting a phantom.
**PATTERN WORTH KEEPING:** my instinct to verify the lint MYSELF was right (a load-bearing gate must not be taken on report). The error was in HOW I measured, not WHETHER. **Verify — but check your instrument before you trust its answer against someone else's work.**

## L-S40-32 — ★ A UNIQUENESS CHECK THAT SCANS THE WRONG SURFACE ISN'T ONE (I caused an R40-number collision)
req flagged a possible #126 gap; measuring it found worse: **I told req to mint R40.64 for class-work while R40.64 was ALREADY SPENT** — shipped deploy commit 8a920ba78 reads "R40.63/64/65 (v0.8.139): per-CR approve verb + CR-owner is expandable + device gate on node22".
**req's collision check was CORRECT and still missed it:** it scanned scenario-unit altIds, and no unit carried R40.64. **The number existed only in a COMMIT MESSAGE — invisible to a unit-scan.** So the check covered a real surface, just not the surface where the collision lived.
**⇒ Same family as the whole campaign's disease (covers-the-class): check:pin-single-source scanned actors not the hazard; check-status-symbol scanned the server table not the client render; our number-uniqueness check scans units not git history.** In every case the check was real, ran, and passed — over a case it never looked at.
**RULE: number allocation must scan BOTH scenario units AND `git log --grep` for the number.** More generally: **when you define a uniqueness/coverage check, enumerate WHERE the thing can exist — a name can live in a unit, a commit message, a deploy tag, or a doc — and cover every place it can be created, not just the place it is supposed to live.**
**ALSO CONFIRMED (#126):** three features shipped in v0.8.139 with NO requirement units (per-CR approve verb, CR-owner-expandable, device-gate-on-node22). Backfill ordered to the SHIPPED reality, sequenced BEHIND Tron's live checklist defect — hygiene never delays his fix.
**PROCESS: req flagged and did NOT mint unprompted.** Had it silently minted, the collision would have hardened into the record.

## L-S40-33 — ★★ A DERIVED VALUE CAN **MASK** THE REGRESSION IT WAS BUILT TO PREVENT (gate the AUTHORED state, not only the derivation)
Tron's AC (R40.59): *"a decline must NOT regress to In Progress — QA Review stays [x] + a new Processing-Change-Requests sub-step."* We shipped the band: a derived status `QA-Review-with-open-CR`.
**What actually happened:** the served decline still does `reopen:true` (server.ts:1653), which **UNTICKS QA Review**. So the checklist regressed exactly as his AC forbade — but the BAND derived to `QA-Review-with-open-CR`, so the STATUS never read "In Progress". **The derived value made the regression invisible.**
⇒ **We satisfied the LETTER (status ≠ In Progress) and violated the INTENT (QA Review stays ticked).** A gate asserting only the band would have PASSED over this defect forever. Tron caught it by looking at the CHECKLIST — the authored state — on his own screen.
**RULE: when a value is DERIVED from an authored state, gate the AUTHORED STATE TOO. A correct derivation over a corrupted source is a false green with extra steps.**
**This is weaker-property substitution one level DOWN:** we asserted the derived output instead of the input it derives from. (Campaign tally of this one shape: source-not-agreement · derive-at-render-not-agreement · scratch-not-device · band-not-checklist.)
**MY ERROR, corrected by the architect's measurement:** I suspected a parallel flag feeding the band ("two sources again"). **Wrong — the band is honest, deriving purely from hasOpenCrSubstep in the checklist.** The defect was upstream in the decline path. I pattern-matched to the disease we had been chasing instead of measuring; the architect measured. **A strong prior about the failure mode makes you fast AND makes you wrong in a specific direction.**

## L-S40-34 — ★ WHEN YOU FIND ONE INSTANCE, SWEEP FOR THE CLASS (I ordered the instance; req swept and found more)
I measured an R40.64 number collision and ordered req to renumber **that one**. **req swept ALL its recent mints and found R40.63 had collided too** — the shipped deploy 8a920ba78 spent 63/64/65 together, all invisible to a unit-altId scan. **My order would have left a live collision behind.**
**Fixing the instance is CMM2; sweeping the class is CMM3/4** — Tron's own distinction, and the agent applied it to my order rather than just executing it.
**RULE: on discovering any instance of a class, the FIRST question is "how many others?" — sweep before fixing. A dispatch that names one instance should say "and sweep for siblings", or the fix is guaranteed partial.**
**COROLLARY (procedure vs gate):** we now have two fixes-by-procedure in one day — "scan git-grep when allocating numbers" and "check every guard is wired". **A procedure depends on the next agent remembering; a gate does not.** The architect's meta-check ("every scripts/check-*.mjs must appear in ci:gates:raw, else RED") is the right shape — **convert procedures into gates wherever the property is machine-checkable**, otherwise we have written a rule that decays the moment someone new arrives or someone tired forgets.

## L-S40-35 — ★ MY BACKTICK VIOLATION IS NOW 5x — THE FIX IS MECHANICAL, NOT "REMEMBER HARDER"
Backticks inside a double-quoted otmux send EXECUTE as shell and SILENTLY STRIP the content. Today alone it: ran my payload as commands (twice), removed the evasion-risk condition from a gate spec (the most important sentence in that dispatch), and just garbled a fleet tree-hazard broadcast. **`send.verified OK` still reports success — the loss is invisible unless I capture the recipient.**
**I banked this rule TWICE and violated it FIVE times.** By my own L-S40-8: **a repeated manual correction is evidence of an upstream defect — stop getting better at applying it, and remove the need.** The upstream here is my own composition habit under load.
**MECHANICAL FIX (not a resolution): I do not type the backtick character in a send. Ever. Code identifiers get written in PLAIN WORDS** ("server dot ts line 1409", "the rbadd command", "git branch --show-current" unquoted). If a token genuinely needs delimiting, single quotes. **A rule I must remember at the moment of writing is a rule I will break; a character I never type is not a rule at all.**
**SEVERITY NOTE:** the danger is not the garbling — it is that the send REPORTS SUCCESS. A silent partial delivery of a safety instruction is worse than a failed one, because nobody knows to re-send.

## L-S40-36 — ★★ A RELAYED MEASUREMENT IS A STORED ARTIFACT THAT DECAYS. RE-DERIVE AT POINT-OF-USE. (my worst error this session)
The planner measured `grep resolveChangeRequest = 0`. **I relayed it, escalated it as a HARD BLOCKER, and told TRON THREE TIMES that his CRs could not close and T40.1 could not reach Done.** All false: the function is `approveChangeRequest`, it works, and **he had already approved 2 of his 5 CRs himself.** A NAMING artifact became a blocker in front of the person waiting on delivery.
**Only the expert's R7 stop-and-surface prevented it becoming a DUPLICATE second band-writer — the exact authored-vs-derived disease we spent the campaign eliminating.** I would have shipped the disease while fixing the disease.
**RULE: a measurement I did not take is not evidence — it is a claim with a timestamp. Re-derive before repeating, ALWAYS before escalating to Tron.**
**THE ASYMMETRY THAT MAKES RE-DERIVATION (not distrust) THE CURE:** when the architect re-derived its own prior claims, one had gone stale (DEPLOY-STATE) and one still held (req units genuinely absent). **Re-derivation SORTS decayed claims from live ones; blanket distrust destroys both.**

## L-S40-37 — ★ NUMBERS SPENT IN COMMIT MESSAGES ARE INVISIBLE TO UNIT SCANS (3 instances in one day)
Tron asked twice where tasks 40.19-40.37 were. **Measured: 26 missing (40.13-16, 40.19-27, 40.29-36), and ZERO units EVER existed in the whole git history — never minted, not lost. Yet 141 commit-message references to those numbers.**
**Same surface gap hit 3x today:** (a) three v0.8.139 features shipped whose numbers lived only in a commit message; (b) I allocated R40.64 that was already spent that way and caused a collision; (c) these 26 tasks.
**RULE: every uniqueness/coverage check must enumerate WHERE the thing can be CREATED — units, commit messages, deploy tags, prose — not merely where it is SUPPOSED to live.** A number in a commit message is a claim with no data behind it and nothing we own checks for it.

## L-S40-38 — ★★ "IT'S ON YOUR DESK" WAS ME OFFLOADING MY JOB (Tron: "NOTHING IS ON MY DESK. ITS IN YOURS. DELIVER PO!!!")
I routed operational calls to him — ship the recorder dormant or active? reconcile now or later? which symptom did you observe? — and framed it as deference. **It was not deference. It was me refusing to decide, in front of the person waiting for delivery.**
**THE LINE: he is QA and DIRECTION. Every operational call is MINE.** Asking him is only correct when the decision is genuinely his (his device, his product judgement, his authorisation) — and then it is ONE crisp ask with a recommendation, never a menu.
**TEST BEFORE ASKING HIM ANYTHING: am I giving him a DECISION, or giving him my WORK?**
**Downstream effect that proves it: the moment I took the calls back (recorder ACTIVE, build on hotfix, one deploy, reconcile after) the whole pipeline unblocked in minutes.** My asking had been the bottleneck.

## L-S40-39 — ★ I DROPPED A DIRECT QUESTION AND HE HAD TO ASK "AGAIN"
He asked where 40.19-40.37 were. I did not answer. He re-asked with "again". **A dropped question from Tron is worse than a wrong answer — a wrong answer he can correct; a dropped one he must re-spend his attention on.**
**RULE: a direct question from Tron gets measured and answered in the SAME turn, or explicitly parked with a stated return time. Never silently.**

## L-S40-2 — COVERAGE IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A NAME-MATCH (req corrected my count 3x running)
Tron asked "where are 40.19-40.70" THREE times. My answers kept being wrong, each in a different way:
1. "26 never minted, ZERO units ever existed, nothing lost" — WRONG: conflated *no TASK unit* with *no unit at all*. The REQUIREMENTS existed the whole time.
2. Re-measured by grepping `name|title|altId` for `40.x` → 63 reqs / 17 tasks / "46 missing". Still wrong.
3. req re-measured **structurally** (does `req.tasks[]` or any `task.coveredRequirements` actually POINT at it?) → **66 reqs (R40.1-70, real gaps at 61/63/64/65), 19 covered, 47 MISSING**. The +1 over my count = R40.16: a task NAME-MENTIONS it, but that task's `coveredRequirements` points at a different req — so R40.16 is genuinely uncovered.
**Rule: measure coverage/linkage by the LINK FIELDS, never by string-matching a number in a name.** A name-mention is not a link; a title match is not coverage. Same family as [[marker-attach-full-uuid-chain-vs-task]] and my task-uuid/impl-uuid conflation (L-S37-3) — in a graph, identity and linkage are structural facts, and grep only sees text.
**Meta:** req refused to mint off my number and re-derived first *because* I had told it my previous answers to Tron were wrong. Publishing my own error made the next agent verify instead of inherit. Broadcasting a mistake is a control, not just an apology.

## L-S40-3 — BORN-FALSE vs WENT-STALE: a freshness invariant cannot catch a fact that was never true
The planner re-derived my retracted T40.1 blocker on disk and found the ROOT of specimen #8: `resolveChangeRequest` grep=0 was a **GUESSED-NAME NEGATIVE**. I invented a plausible function name, grepped it, got zero, and escalated "T40.1 blocked" to Tron THREE times. The real function is `approveChangeRequest` (server.ts:1667, wired :1948) — the CR-close path was REAL the whole time.
**The distinction that matters for the cure:** the truth-decay family is being cured with *derive-first, else stored-WITH-revalidation + VISIBLE-STALE*. That catches facts that **went stale**. It does NOT catch a fact that was **false at birth** — re-validating a born-false claim just re-confirms the same wrong query. Two different failure modes:
- **WENT-STALE** → freshness invariant (revalidate, show age).
- **BORN-FALSE** → *provenance/derivation* check: was the premise ever DERIVED, or was it constructed from a guess? A negative result needs a POSITIVE control (prove the query CAN find something — grep a name you know exists) before "absent" is admissible. Same shape as stub-must-fail: an instrument that cannot succeed proves nothing by failing.
**Rule:** never accept a negative from a name/path/id I supplied myself. Either derive the real name from the code, or run the positive control first. [[distrust-your-own-negative-reverify]] + [[confirm-diagnosis-by-different-method-not-circular]].

## L-S40-4 — THE PAIRING LAW: a composed-system claim needs evidence from EACH part
I told Tron the flight recorder was "LIVE and ARMED" after verifying the CLIENT bundle carried the capture trigger. I never checked the SERVER sink — it was 404, so every capture would have fired into nothing. If he had reloaded, we'd have gotten ZERO and believed the instrument had run. The tester caught it.
**Law (now Arm B sub-case 2, folded by the architect):** any claim about a COMPOSED system — ARMED / wired / deployed / covered end-to-end — is inadmissible without evidence from EACH part: client-marker AND sink-403-not-404, producer AND consumer, payload AND rendered artifact. "Assert one half, infer the whole" is the same born-false family as a guessed-name grep=0: both produce a confident claim that was never derived.
**Cost of the version I shipped:** a false ARMED status to Tron on his #1 open complaint, on the one instrument that can diagnose it.
**I am specimen #8 AND #10** in the truth-decay corpus (relayed-measurement, assert-one-half). Appearing twice in the same corpus, in the same role, two different ways, is not embarrassment — it is the evidence that the class is structural and needed a guard rather than more care.
**Practice adopted ahead of the code:** before reporting an absence OR a composed-system status to Tron or the fleet, attach the per-part evidence in the same message. Related: [[assert-the-rendered-artifact-not-a-proxy]], [[correct-by-construction]].

## L-S40-5 — ${...} EXPANDS IN A DOUBLE-QUOTED otmux send (same class as the backtick trap)
Sending an architect message containing the literal pattern `ior:instance:${ref|rawRef}` produced `bash: bad substitution` — the shell expanded/blanked the `${...}` before otmux ever saw it. Same family as the banked backtick hazard: **a double-quoted send is SHELL INPUT first, message text second.**
**Rule:** never put `${`, backticks, or `$(` in a double-quoted otmux send. Use single quotes, or describe the pattern in words/CAPS (e.g. "an ior:instance interpolation of a variable"). Verify by CAPTURE after sending anything containing shell metacharacters — I only knew it landed because I captured the pane.
**Why it matters beyond cosmetics:** this time the meaning survived because the recipient knew its own code. A garbled ROLLBACK or DO-NOT-DEPLOY instruction would not be so forgiving. Related: [[backtick-blanks-otmux-send]].

## L-S40-6 — SECOND FAMILY: text-not-structure (and a boundary correction I accepted)
The architect ruled 'guard/instrument keyed to NAMES rather than the HAZARD' a **sibling family** to truth-decay, not a sub-arm (067ec1270). Root: the instrument matches surface TEXT (name / string / mention / identifier) where it should match STRUCTURE or BEHAVIOUR → **evadable-by-rename, or wrong-by-coincidence**. Corpus 4, three of them mine: my guessed-name grep · my string-match coverage count · name-mention-mistaken-for-structural-link · the name-keyed lint that let the carveout bug walk past. Distinct from truth-decay (wrong-THING-matched vs artifact-goes-stale) but they CO-OCCUR — the r4011 dead-lint is both.
**★ IT CORRECTED MY BOUNDARY AND I ACCEPTED:** I had grouped its fact-2 unmeasured-premise with the four. It split it out — that was *asserted-without-measuring* (truth-decay Arm-B, born-false), not *matched-text-instead-of-structure*. **Precise boundaries keep a family credible; a family that absorbs every failure explains none of them.** Same rigour that kept 'missing-mechanism' out.
**META-GUARD (my proposal, adopted): a LINT OVER OUR LINTS** — scan `scripts/check-*` for name-keyed discriminants (a specific identifier/literal, defeatable by rename) → flag, then RED. It must **self-bite** AND must not itself be name-keyed, or it becomes specimen #5 of its own family; it detects the SHAPE 'discriminant is an identifier' structurally.
**Why this is the highest-leverage guard we have:** a name-keyed guard gives FALSE confidence — worse than no guard, because we stop looking. Cure across all four = [[scan-the-hazard-not-the-actors]].

## L-S40-7 — IN A SERVE-FROM-DISK CHECKOUT, *BUILDING* IS *DEPLOYING* (for client code)
I ruled "hold the deploy until Tron's capture lands, but stage it fully READY — bump, artifacts, deploy.mjs". The expert caught the flaw in my own instruction: on this checkout **client bundles serve from DISK immediately, with no restart**. So `npm run build` would have pushed the r4011 CLIENT change live on Tron's next reload AND superseded the armed recorder build — the exact disturbance + provenance-muddying my hold existed to prevent.
**Law:** where artifacts are served straight from the build directory, **there is no "built but not deployed" state for client code — the build IS the deploy.** Only server-side changes retain a build/restart gap. So "stage it ready" must mean *the ready-to-FIRE sequence*, never pre-built artifacts; durability comes from the SOURCE being committed (fact-2 955d9870b, r4011 6ed9c49d5), not from pre-building.
**Generalisation:** before saying "prepare but don't ship", ask *what act actually publishes here?* — build, commit, restart, or copy. The publishing step differs per artifact class in the same repo (client=build, server=restart), and assuming one model for both is how an unintended release happens.
**Meta:** the expert applied MY hold-reasoning to correct MY instruction. A directive is not exempt from the principle it enforces.

## L-S40-8 — A PATTERN-MATCH NEARLY DELETED 14 LOAD-BEARING UNITS (the family caught inside our own classifier, twice)
The planner reported 49 sprint-less requirements as "JUNK — fabricated uuids", classified by uuid **PREFIX pattern** (d4e5f6a7-*/18*). The architect independently "verified by inspection" and instructed req to **RETIRE** them.
I halted it on shape alone: a prefix is TEXT, and we had stood up the text-not-structure family an hour earlier. I required three STRUCTURAL signals before any retirement — (a) uuidv4-INVALID by version/variant bits, (b) **ZERO inbound references**, (c) empty content — plus a standing "nothing is deleted, only marked/quarantined".
**The structural re-classification INVERTED the finding: 0 junk · 14 LOAD-BEARING (real inbound refs) · 35 real-content · ALL 49 REAL.** Retiring them would have broken live chains. The 28 non-uuidv4 ids were a real DATA-QUALITY signal — but never a delete signal.
**Three lessons:**
1. **Our own classifiers are not exempt from the families we define.** Two different agents committed the same text-matching error on the same data, within an hour of naming it.
2. **The strength of the evidence must scale with the irreversibility of the act.** "Verified by inspection" is fine for a report; it is not fine for 49 near-irreversible deletions. Destructive actions demand mechanical, reproducible, auditable signals.
3. **Inbound-reference count is the decisive test for "is this thing real".** Not its name, not its id shape, not its author's intent — *does anything depend on it*.
**And the culture point:** the planner **contradicted both the architect and me, with evidence, and HELD a destructive action** already dispatched to req. That is what made the save. An agent that defers to two peers here loses 14 units.
**My own correction:** I speculated the debt might be "far smaller than 86" once junk was excluded. Wrong — all 62 are real. Recorded.

## L-S40-9 — "npm start" IS NOT A RESTART, IT BUILDS (and a stale version-stamp LIES)
The expert ran what it believed was a **server-only restart** to ship a one-field version-stamp without touching the armed client recorder. It shipped the ENTIRE held bundle instead: `npm start` → `start.mjs:71` → `build.mjs`. It had verified that *deploy.mjs's restart step* was build-free, but not that *npm start itself* builds — **the right question at the wrong layer.**
**Law:** on this repo, `npm start` = BUILD + restart. There is no build-free restart via npm; a genuinely build-free restart needs a direct process spawn. Before claiming "this ships nothing", trace the ACTUAL command chain to the bottom — verifying one link is not verifying the chain.
**★ The compounding hazard I caught and the builder missed:** the accidental build left **served != committed** while the sink had just started **stamping recordings with the committed version**. So a capture would have been labelled `0.8.144` while running different code — **provenance CORRUPTION from the very feature added to guarantee provenance.** A version-stamp is only meaningful when served==committed; an unbumped stamp is worse than no stamp, because it is confidently wrong.
**Ruling shape worth reusing:** when an accident lands work early, ask *whether the hold's PURPOSE is now satisfied* rather than defending the hold. Here it was (recorder survived + stamped), so completing forward beat reverting — especially since the revert path (novel build-free restart) was UNTESTED and would have been exercised for the first time against the one instrument Tron is waiting on. **Don't test a novel recovery path on your only instrument.**
**Culture:** the expert self-reported immediately, changed nothing without a ruling, and named its own root cause. That is why a mis-ship was recoverable in one clean move instead of a forensic dig.

## L-S40-10 ★ MY DOMINANT FAILURE MODE: I relay AGENT OUTPUT to Tron as MEASURED FACT
Three times in one session I escalated to Tron something I had not verified against stored/observable state:
1. **resolveChangeRequest grep=0 → "T40.1 BLOCKED"** — escalated 3x. The function name was my own guess; `approveChangeRequest` existed and worked.
2. **"the recorder is LIVE and ARMED"** — verified the client bundle, never the sink. Sink was 404; every capture would have been inert.
3. **"our own test wrote a false Done to production"** — relayed a gate LOG LINE as a persisted mutation. Measurement found: `ce981242` is the **SystemTester** token, no task→Done carries it, the graph is clean. Likely a seeded fixture.
**The common shape is NOT carelessness — it is TRUSTING A REPORT AS A MEASUREMENT.** In each case an agent's output was true *as output* and I converted it into a claim about the world without checking the world.
**RULE (binding on me): an agent report is a HYPOTHESIS until I measure it. Before any statement to Tron phrased as fact — especially an alarm, an absence, or a "done/armed/fixed" — I verify the STORED or OBSERVABLE state myself and cite what I measured.** If I cannot measure it, I say "reported by X, unverified" and label it as such. The guards I enforce on everyone else (positive control for absences, per-part evidence for composed claims, structural signal over text) apply to my own reports FIRST — I wrote them and I am the most frequent violator.
**Cost:** false alarms consume Tron's attention on his own product, and each one spends trust that the true reports need. A PO who cries wolf three times has damaged the signal his real escalations depend on.
**Counter-evidence that the fleet works:** every one of the three was caught by an agent (architect, tester, planner) re-deriving instead of deferring. The culture is load-bearing precisely because I am fallible in this specific way.

## L-S40-11 — `head -N` TURNS A POSITIVE INTO A FALSE NEGATIVE (my 4th measurement error today)
I ran `grep -rl "5fbed155" data/ | head -3`, saw 3 log files, and told the expert **"it is NOT on disk — drop the data repair"**, overruling the architect who had said it lived in the model store. There were **4 matches**; the 4th — the actual unit file at `data/model-store/index/5/f/b/e/d/5fbed155-*.scenario.json` — was **cut off by my own `head -3`**. The architect was right; I overruled it with a truncated measurement.
**Rule: NEVER draw an ABSENCE conclusion from truncated output. COUNT FIRST (`wc -l`), then inspect.** A `head`/`tail` limit is a display convenience that silently converts "there is more" into "there is nothing" — and absence claims are exactly where I have failed repeatedly (guessed-name grep, half-checked pairing, this). Pair with the Arm-B positive control: if I am about to say "not there", the instrument must be shown able to find the thing, AND its output must not be clipped.
**What made it harmless:** the expert **followed my instruction but REPORTED the contradicting evidence** ("I DID find it physically, left untouched per your do-not-hunt rule, flagging it") instead of silently obeying a wrong premise. An agent that just complies would have left me confidently wrong on the record. That is the culture doing the work again — 4 of my errors today were caught by agents, not by me.
**Cost here:** near-zero (orphan has 0 inbound refs; the caller fix stops new mints). But the same error on a destructive instruction would not be near-zero — cf. the 49 "junk" units that were actually 14 load-bearing.

## L-S40-12 ★★★ THE ROOT OF EVERYTHING TODAY: A STATUS FLAG IS NOT THE STATE
The architect named it and it unifies BOTH families we built this session — truth-decay (a STALE stored status) and text-not-structure (a SELF-REPORT standing in for the hazard) are the same error in different clothes.
**All of today's faces of it:**
- `readyState` says OPEN while the socket delivers nothing (the iOS zombie) → cure: **unconditional refetch, verify by DOING**
- a gate is GREEN because it **cannot fire** (blind-by-inheritance; 22 gates inert on a stale path)
- an exemption a file **declares about itself**
- a **version stamp** claiming 0.8.144 while serving other code
- **existence != connection** (recorder client present, sink 404 — my "ARMED" claim)
- a **relayed measurement** treated as a fact (my 3 false escalations to Tron)
- a **truncated grep** read as absence (my `head -3` of 4 matches)
- a **name/prefix/shape match** treated as identity (uuid-prefix "junk", guessed function name, string-matched coverage)
**RULE:** any check gated on a STATUS — readyState / satisfied / covered / wired / version / inherits / marked / reported — must **verify by DOING**: refetch, round-trip, scan the hazard, resolve to the real unit, derive from HEAD, count before concluding absence. **A guard built on a self-report inherits that report's blind spot.**
**Why this is MY lesson specifically:** every one of my failures today was trusting a status instead of observing the state — an agent's report, my own truncated output, a branch's disk contents standing in for Tron's screen. The team caught all of them by re-deriving. **The discipline is not "be more careful"; it is "measure the thing itself, every time, including when it is my own claim."**

## L-S40-13 — A PRE-AUTHORIZATION CAN CREATE A DEADLOCK IF IT DOESN'T NAME THE SEQUENCE
I pre-authorized Phase-A to land on the tester's green so nothing would wait on me during my cut. It still froze: the **expert** held for "the tester's green" while the **tester** waited to "gate once the migration deploys." Neither was wrong — my pre-authorization named the TRIGGER (tester green) but never named the **SEQUENCE**, and the dependency is genuinely circular: the gate needs a SERVED surface, but serving IS the land the gate authorizes.
**Rule:** when pre-authorizing work to proceed without me, specify the ORDER and the ENVIRONMENT, not just the trigger — "X gates on surface S, then Y lands" — otherwise two correct agents each wait for the other and delivery stalls silently while both report healthy.
**Resolution used:** break the circle with an ISOLATED surface (R40.31 scratch server: worktree + non-4444 ports + teardown) — a real served target that carries zero prod risk. Gate there, land to prod on green.
**Detection:** both panes idle + both self-reporting "holding for <the other>". Verify MOTION, never that everyone is "fine" — this is the 3rd standby I've caught this way ([[delegated-is-not-driven-drive-to-completion]]).

## L-S40-14 — NEAR-MISS I CAUSED: C-u does NOT clear a multi-line composer, so my Enter FIRED a staged /rewind on the SM
Doing my fleet-watch duty I injected `/context` into the SM's IDLE pane (correct per protocol). A **rewind picker opened**. Nobody was driving it — ARON was busy cutting the trainer, the trainer was idle. Almost certainly mine: a `/rewind` was staged in a MULTI-LINE composer, `C-u` did not clear it (the trainer had already banked that C-u/C-a/C-k/Escape do not clear multi-line drafts — a backspace-burst is required), and my Enter **submitted the staged command instead of my own**.
**Blast radius if unnoticed:** an un-driven picker on the FLEET MONITOR with option-1 (restore CODE) as the default; a stray Enter/cron tick could have code-reverted the SM. It also blinds the watcher while the modal is open.
**Resolution:** Escape ABORTS a picker and performs no rewind ⇒ needs no Tron authorization (cancelling ≠ driving). Verified closed, composer empty, code untouched, SM intact.
**RULE (fleet):** before sending a slash-command (or any Enter) to ANOTHER agent's pane, **CAPTURE the composer and verify it is EMPTY first** — never assume `C-u` cleared it. My Enter belongs to whatever text is staged, not to my intent. Corollary: `send.raw <pane> Enter` is never safe on a pane you have not just read.
**Also:** an unauthorized/unowned picker found OPEN should be ESCAPED promptly — leaving it "for the owner" is not the safe default when no owner exists.

## L-S40-15 — A FORCED SURFACE IS A PROXY SURFACE: gate where the component actually lives
Phase-A HOLD: file+webitem PASSed on /trace; feature+modelelement rendered false there — but they are **not on /trace by design** (:298, they render on their owner-gated home pages /feature-manager and /model). The tempting "fix" was to force all 4 onto /trace so one gate could cover them. The architect ruled that out: **a surface the component does not really live on is a contrived proxy surface Tron never sees** — a green there proves nothing about the surface he actually uses. Instead: owner-auth the ISOLATED scratch and gate each on its REAL surface, same funnel discriminator.
**Rule:** gate where the AC's behaviour actually renders, even when that is harder (owner-gated, multi-surface). Convenience of coverage is never a reason to move the assertion to an easier surface. Extends [[gate-the-ac-surface]] with the forcing case.
**Second rule (classification):** NOT-VERIFIABLE ≠ FAIL, but BOTH produce HOLD — no component may land failing, and none may land UNVERIFIED. 2-of-4 passing + 2 uncovered is a coverage gap, the same disease as a hand-list that hides a violator.
**Third:** a "all components verified" claim MUST state which surfaces were reachable, or the coverage number lies. These 4 span THREE surfaces, two owner-gated.

## L-S40-16 — AN ASSERTED INVARIANT THAT **NO INSTANCE SATISFIES** IS NOT AN INVARIANT, IT IS AN ASPIRATION
Phase-A stated the base funnel signature as `dv-head + dv-fields + dv-parent-children`. The tester then measured per component: **file = dv-head/dv-fields · webitem = dv-field · modelelement = dv-rel/dv-empty · feature = NONE.** ⇒ **not one of the four emits the full stated triple** — including the three that PASSED. So the gate was necessarily implementing something weaker than the stated rule (some-dv-*-present), i.e. a **definition-vs-implementation drift that predated the failure** and was invisible while everything passed.
**Why this is dangerous:** the drift only surfaced because ONE component failed. Had feature emitted any dv-*, all 4 would have gone green under a rule that no component actually satisfied — a green proving something nobody had stated correctly.
**Rules:** (1) When stating an invariant, verify at least one PASSING instance literally satisfies it as written — otherwise you are gating on a weaker implicit rule you never wrote down. (2) When a failure exposes that the stated rule was never the real one, FIX THE DEFINITION openly and **re-run ALL instances under it** — never apply the corrected definition only to the failing one (that is a post-hoc exemption wearing a different hat). (3) Distinguish *renders differently* from *renders less than the AC requires* — variation across components is not automatically a defect, and uniformity is not automatically the requirement.
Related: [[exists-correct-proven-gate-gradient]], [[gate-that-mocks-the-mechanism-is-blind]], [[assert-the-rendered-artifact-not-a-proxy]].

## L-S40-17 — ESCALATE, DON'T LOOSEN: a verifier's refusal is what finds the broken definition
Phase-A's whole arc turned on ONE choice by the tester: at 2-of-4 it **escalated** instead of **loosening**. It could have relaxed the assertion at three separate moments — when 2 components were unreachable, when feature failed the dv-* check, when a single definition-change would have greened everything. Each time it held and escalated. That escalation is what exposed a **definition drift that had been invisible for as long as everything passed**: the stated base signature (`dv-head+dv-fields+dv-parent-children`) was satisfied by ZERO of the 4 components, so the gate had been silently checking a weaker unwritten proxy.
**The chain of refusals that made the green trustworthy:** expert refused to invent a discriminator when none honestly existed → tester refused to shade a RED and proved its own gate could fail → architect eliminated `shown-event`, the criterion that would most easily have ended the HOLD, and ruled on measurement → I overrode two of my own rulings (`served==committed==HEAD` as sufficient; the DETAIL_ARTIFACTS fallback) when each was shown insufficient.
**Rules:** (1) When a gate fails, the FIRST question is "is my rule right?" not "can my rule be relaxed?" — but you only earn that question by ESCALATING, never by quietly loosening. (2) A definition corrected under pressure must be applied to ALL instances and be **outcome-independent** — provably the same rule had the failing case passed. (3) STATED == IMPLEMENTED: write the rule into the gate and grep out the old proxy, or the drift survives the fix (architect's catch: adding the right assertion while leaving the weaker one underneath keeps it alive).
**Meta:** every material error this session was caught by someone other than its author. That is not luck — it is what mutual escalation produces.

## L-S40-18 — A RULE STATED FORWARD-ONLY IS HALF A RULE: ask "what ALREADY violates this?"
I ruled "owner-auth is scratch-ONLY, never prod" while authorizing the Phase-A gate. The tester then found that an **existing** gate (r4062 recorder-survival) had been **owner-authing PROD all along** — WS-IDENTIFY→session to read the owner-gated /model page. My ruling was aimed only at the new work; the violation was already in the tree.
**Rule:** whenever I set or restate a boundary, immediately ask **"what already crosses this line?"** and sweep existing code/practice — a boundary that binds only future work leaks silently through everything written before it.
**Better-than-compliance:** the tester didn't just make the check *permitted* — it removed the need for privilege entirely (committed-manifest-hash + PUBLIC /dist fetch ⇒ served==committed BY CONSTRUCTION). Eliminating the privileged path beats authorizing it.
**And my own error in the same exchange:** I attributed the GET-probe to r4062 without grepping it; r4062 was already method-correct (POST→403) and the GET-405 came from the expert's separate check. My measurement was right, my inference about WHICH artifact was wrong. **Measure the artifact before naming it** — had the tester complied instead of measuring, it would have "fixed" a correct gate on my say-so. Compliance with a wrong instruction is how a correct thing gets broken. Related: [[distrust-your-own-negative-reverify]].

## L-S40-19 — BACKTICKS IN AN otmux SEND EXECUTE IN MY SHELL (3rd recurrence — banning them, not noting them)
For the THIRD time I put backticks inside a double-quoted otmux send. Bash ran the contents in MY shell and interpolated the OUTPUT into the message: a `git show --stat HEAD` fired (read-only, harmless this time) and its result "1 file changed, 9 insertions(+)..." was pasted where the command form should have been — **garbling an instruction that was itself about commit discipline**. A second backtick errored ("syntax error near unexpected token newline") and dropped its text entirely.
**Why noting it has not worked:** I banked this twice as "be careful with backticks". Care is not a control. The blast radius is real — a substituted command RUNS (this time a read-only git show; a destructive one would have executed just as happily), and the recipient receives a corrupted instruction that may look authoritative.
**HARD RULE (not a reminder):** NEVER put a backtick or dollar-brace in an otmux send. Write command forms as PLAIN PROSE — "use the one-step form: git commit -m MSG -- followed by your explicit paths". If a message truly needs literal shell syntax, write it to a FILE and send the PATH instead. Verify delivery by CAPTURING the recipient pane (send.verified only proves keystrokes landed, not that the text is intact).
Related: [[backtick-blanks-otmux-send]] — same root, now with the executes-in-my-shell consequence made explicit.

## L-S40-20 — PARALLEL ACTORS ON ONE P0 PRODUCE A PARTIAL FIX THAT LOOKS COMPLETE (my coordination failure)
I dispatched the same P0 root (dangling task refs breaking the CurrentSprint pin) to THREE agents without naming ONE owner. They returned THREE scopes off the SAME tree: **expert 10** (all current-era sprints), **planner 5** (sprint-40), **architect 3** (sprint-40, from the error message's named sprint). The planner then **acted unilaterally** — carried its 5, committed, and reported *"verified 0 dangles remain"*, which was true **within its scope** and false globally. A restart at that point would have refused again on sprint-37's 7 and handed Tron a SECOND false "fixed".
**I caught it only because the numbers disagreed and I measured the disk myself** (all 7 sprint-37 refs present on origin/main, ZERO on served). Had all three said "3", I would have shipped the partial fix.
**Rules:** (1) On a P0, name a **SINGLE OWNER** for the remediation before dispatching diagnosis widely — diagnose in parallel, ACT through one hand. (2) When agents return different counts off the same artifact, **never average or take the latest — measure the artifact myself**; a disagreement in scope is data, not noise. (3) A completion claim must state its **SCOPE** ("0 dangles remain *in sprint-40*"), or it will be read as global — the same law as "a coverage claim must name its reachable surfaces".
**Also:** the wider sweep came from the agent I had NOT put in the data lane. Ownership follows the best measurement, not the assigned lane.

## L-S40-21 — "GATED" SILENTLY MEANT "GATED WHEN SOMEONE RUNS IT" (~180 gates, 4 invoked)
Chasing Tron's latency regression, the tester measured the runners: **~180 r*.mjs visual gates exist; only 4 device gates are CI-invoked** (gate:device:live:raw). Everything else — including r4066 (the Phase-A gate shipped today), r4062 (recorder-survival), and every gate this team built this sprint — runs ONLY when a human remembers to run it. r301 (/trace eager-lazy structure) was the specimen that made it visible: hardcoded to Sprint-30-era literals, in NO runner, and would RED if executed = looks like coverage, executes nowhere.
**Why this is the largest finding of the incident:** it is the reason a **250x** perf breach reached the user at all. Every "we gated it" claim I have made carries an unstated qualifier. A gate that is not invoked is not a gate — it is documentation that resembles one.
**Root shape:** a gate hardcoded to a point-in-time state (a literal sprint number) inevitably REDs as reality moves; the cheapest human reaction is to skip it; then it dies quietly and still LOOKS like coverage.
**Fix shape:** (a) derive-don't-hardcode — assert the DISCOVERED current pin, never a literal (same discovered-not-hand-listed law as the hazard gate); (b) every gate is either INVOKED by a runner or explicitly MARKED not-invoked WITH a reason, and the un-invoked COUNT is reported (mark-not-silence, discovered not hand-listed).
**Category discipline:** keep STALE-HARDCODED-UNINVOKED distinct from KNOWN-BROKEN-INVOCATION (r241/r245 execute but return null). Merging classes to make one number bigger destroys the number's meaning.
**PO honesty consequence:** when I report something as "gated" I must know whether it is *invoked*. Related: [[exists-correct-proven-gate-gradient]] — this is a new floor BELOW "exists": exists-but-never-runs.

## L-S40-22 — A LATENCY THRESHOLD IS PARTLY A PROXY FOR THE USER'S NETWORK: gate the STRUCTURE you control
I ruled "latency must be a hard assertion, because Tron's AC is 100ms not 1 request" — correct against the fan-out defect, but the architect's measurement forced a refinement: total latency = **work we control + 1 network RTT we do not**. The RTT floor (~80ms throttled) is unreachable-below by any fix, so a bare 100ms threshold is partly gating the user's connection.
**Refined rule — assert the structural invariants we control, HARD and network-independent, and pin the latency measurement:**
(a) client: **1 request per expand** (not O(children));
(b) server: **O(children), never O(total-units)** — no full-index scan on the request path;
(c) latency on a **PINNED profile**, with the threshold set to the **measured achievable floor** (RTT + O(children) work), and the floor STATED IN THE GATE so a later reader sees a measured limit, not a slackened standard.
